- **Per-space permission overrides** (`space_permission_overrides` table, same migration): the most specific layer, ahead of both the custom-role delta and the rank table — scoped to one space, one `(role | customRoleId)` principal, one action, allow or deny. `principal` is a synthetic text key (`"role:admin"` / `"customRole:<uuid>"`) rather than two nullable columns, since Postgres treats each NULL as distinct and would defeat a unique constraint across nullable role/customRoleId columns (`lib/space-overrides.ts`'s `principalKey`, unit-tested). A membership holding a custom role is checked against **both** principals — its custom role's own override first, falling back to one targeting its base role (`getSpaceOverrideForMembership`) — found via the Playwright spec itself: an earlier version checked only the custom-role principal, so a workspace-wide "no guest may do X in this space" override silently stopped applying to any guest who'd separately picked up an unrelated custom role.
- **Wired into the actions with a real space scope**: `hierarchy:create/update/delete` (space/folder/list mutations, `hierarchy.ts`), `status:create/update/delete` (`status.ts`), `task:create/update/delete` (`task.ts`) — each router resolves the acting space id (new `spaceIdForList`/`spaceIdForTask` in `lib/task-queries.ts`, or the space/folder/list row already in hand) and passes it as `assertCan(..., { spaceId })`. Every other action (docs, forms, automations, dashboards, goals, comments, tags, attachments, custom fields, image assets, PR links, channels — none of which nest under a space in DATA_MODEL.md, or aren't worth the scope for a v1) stays workspace-level only; `opts.spaceId` is optional and omitting it is a no-op, so this list can grow later without another migration.
- **Privilege-escalation guardrail**: `isGrantable(action)` (`can.ts`) rejects any grant/override `allow:true` whose rank is owner-tier (`workspace:delete`, `sso:update`, `scimToken:create/delete`) — enforced server-side in `role.ts`'s `assertGrantable` (used by role create/update and space-override set) and mirrored client-side in the Roles page so the checkbox grid only ever offers what will actually save. Without this, `customRole:create`'s own admin tier would let an admin mint a de facto second owner by granting `workspace:delete` through a custom role.
- New `role.ts` router: `role.list/create/update/delete`, `role.assignToMember` (blocks assigning to the workspace owner, same exclusion `updateMemberRole`/`removeMember` already apply), `role.spaceOverride.list/set/delete` (`set` is an upsert on the `(spaceId, principal, action)` unique constraint). `workspace.members` query extended with `customRoleId`/`customRoleName` (left join) so the UI can show/edit it without a second round-trip.
- UI: new **Roles** sidebar entry (`/w/$workspaceId/roles`) — a custom-roles panel (name, base role, a grant/revoke checkbox grid grouped by resource, owner-tier actions hidden entirely) and a space-permissions panel (space picker, principal/action/allow-or-deny override list). `members-panel.tsx` gained a second select (only rendered once a workspace has ≥1 custom role) to assign one to a non-owner member.

### Decisions

- **Custom roles are deltas on a base role, not a standalone permission set.** A from-scratch allowlist would mean re-deriving every one of ~80 actions' sensible default from nothing for each new role; starting from an existing base role and only listing the handful of exceptions is both less error-prone to configure and mirrors how the four built-in roles already relate to each other (rank-based inheritance).
- **Space overrides only cover hierarchy/status/task mutations for this pass** — the resources that actually nest under a space in DATA_MODEL.md's model and where ROADMAP.md's own phrasing ("per-space permission overrides") most plausibly points. Same "wire the core case for real, leave `opts.spaceId` optional so the rest can follow" call M5.4's REST API and SCIM's Users-only made.
- **No workspace-wide "audit all overrides" view** — the Roles page lists overrides per-space (you pick a space, see its rows), not a cross-space table. Kept to what a single admin session configuring one space's exceptions needs; a global view is a reporting feature nothing here asked for.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green; `pnpm db:generate` shows no drift after the migration.
- Unit tests: `can.test.ts` gained 10 cases (grant/revoke precedence, revoke-beats-grant, space-override-beats-everything, `isGrantable`'s owner-tier exclusion) and `space-overrides.test.ts` (3: `principalKey`'s encoding, including that a role name and a custom role id with the same string don't collide) — 229 API tests total, all passing.
- Playwright: `custom-roles.spec.ts` — signs up an owner and creates a space/list, invites a second user as a guest, has the owner create a custom role granting `hierarchy:create` and assign it to the guest, adds a space override allowing `task:create` for the guest role, then switches to the guest's own browser context (a real second session, not a shared cookie) and confirms they can now create a list (via the custom role) and a task (via the space override) — actions a plain guest is denied by default, verified end-to-end through real HTTP requests rather than asserted against `can()` in isolation. Also confirms removing the override removes the row. Caught the `getSpaceOverrideForMembership` fallback gap described above during development (the first version of the spec failed at the task-creation step because the guest's custom-role assignment was shadowing the role-level override). Full 42-spec suite (up from 41) run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: 42/42 clean both times, no flakes.

### Phase 6 — Semantic + visual search (pgvector) (2026-07-24)

ARCHITECTURE.md's stack table already named this exactly ("pgvector later for semantic/visual search"), so the storage choice was fixed; everything else (embedding provider, what "visual" means without a real image-embedding API, sync vs. async query path) was an open call, decided below.

Built:

- **Postgres image switched to `pgvector/pgvector:pg16`** (`docker-compose.yml`) — a drop-in Postgres 16 build with the pgvector extension binary preinstalled; confirmed the existing `postgres-data` volume survives the swap (same major version, standard heap page format). The embeddings migration itself runs `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` (drizzle-kit doesn't generate that statement from the schema; hand-added to the generated SQL file) before creating the table.
- **`embeddings` table** (`packages/db/src/schema/embeddings.ts`, migration `0034_crazy_ezekiel.sql`): one row per `(entityType, entityId)` — the same polymorphic-target convention `activity`/`notifications` already use, rather than a bespoke table per entity kind. `vector(768)` fixed system-wide (not a per-workspace provider choice like `ImageEngine`): two different embedding models produce vectors in different, non-comparable spaces, so a similarity search can't mix providers the way image generation can mix adapters. Drizzle-orm 0.38 ships native pgvector support (`vector()` column type, `cosineDistance()` query helper) — no raw SQL needed for the column or the nearest-neighbor queries. No ANN index (ivfflat/hnsw): a brute-force `ORDER BY cosineDistance(...) LIMIT n` is standard and fine at the row counts this app will see for a while; add one if that stops being true.
- **`EmbeddingEngine`** (`apps/api/src/embedding-engine/`), shaped like `ImageEngine`: one interface, `getEmbeddingEngine()` returns a singleton (not per-workspace). `GeminiEmbeddingAdapter` targets Gemini's `text-embedding-004` (768 dims) but — same situation as the image adapters and `MockChatClient` (no `GEMINI_API_KEY` in this environment) — `embed()` doesn't make a network call. It computes a local **feature-hashing bag-of-words vector**: each token hashes to a signed bucket in a fixed-size array, L2-normalized (a real, named technique — Vowpal Wabbit et al. — not a toy). Unlike `colorFromSeed`'s placeholder swatch, this is a genuinely useful stand-in: texts sharing vocabulary land closer together under cosine similarity (verified in the unit test, and end-to-end in the Playwright spec), which is the one property needed to exercise nearest-neighbor search, ranking, and the UI for real. Swap `embed()`'s body for a real `fetch()` once a key exists — the interface and every caller stay unchanged.
- **Content embeddings run in `embeddingWorker`** (`apps/api/src/worker.ts`, new `embedding-jobs` BullMQ queue), per CLAUDE.md's "AI calls run in a worker" rule: `task.ts`'s `create`/`update` enqueue a job (title + description text) whenever the title or description actually changed; `image-understanding.ts`'s `applyImageUnderstanding` enqueues one right after writing an image's alt-text/tags. `embedding-runner.ts`'s `runEmbedding` does the actual `embed()` call, upserts into `embeddings` (`onConflictDoUpdate` on the `(entityType, entityId)` unique constraint — no version history; an embedding has no independent meaning once its source text changes, unlike an `image_versions` row), and writes an `ai_usage` row (`kind: "embed"`, new enum value).
- **Documented exception: the _query_-side embedding in `search.ts` runs inline in the request handler, not queued.** Even brain chat — which needs to feel live — goes through `brainQueue`, so this is a real, deliberate departure from that pattern, not an oversight: search has no persisted artifact for a worker retry to protect, the user is waiting on this exact request either way, and it's a single short-text encode, not a multi-second generation. It still writes its own `ai_usage` row (metering intact); only the "must run in a worker" half is exempted, and only for this one read path — the persisted, potentially slow/costly content-embedding side above is unaffected and goes through the queue like everything else.
- **`search.tasksByText`**: embeds the query inline, then `ORDER BY cosineDistance(embeddings.vector, queryVector)` joined to `tasks`/`lists`/`spaces` (same shape as the existing FTS `task.search`). **`search.imagesLikeThis`** ("find images like this," ARCHITECTURE.md's own phrase): no new embed call at all — reads the source image's own already-stored embedding and finds its nearest neighbors among the workspace's other `image_asset` embeddings.
- New `imageAsset.list` query — there was no workspace-wide image gallery anywhere before this (every existing consumer fetches one asset at a time), needed to give the visual-search UI something to pick a starting image from.
- UI: new **Semantic Search** sidebar entry (`/w/$workspaceId/semantic-search`, Work group) — a "search tasks by meaning" box and a "find images like this" picker (`ImageVersionThumb` grid, reused as-is from the version-tree UI) showing similar-image results below the selected one.

### Decisions

- **"Visual search" runs over AI-described content (alt-text + tags), not raw pixels.** This codebase has no image-embedding API integrated anywhere — Vertex AI's multimodal-embedding endpoint is a different, heavier surface than the plain Gemini API the rest of the image/brain pipeline already uses, and nothing here has ever called it. Text-derived similarity over the same alt-text/tags M2.7 already generates is the honest, buildable v1 — same "degrade to what's actually available" call M5.6 made for the Google Drive picker — and it reuses the one embeddings pipeline/table rather than standing up a second, parallel one.
- **One system-wide embedding engine, not per-workspace like `ImageEngine`.** Per-workspace provider choice only works when outputs are independently useful (a generated image is a generated image regardless of which model made it); embeddings are only useful relative to each other, so mixing spaces within one similarity search would silently return garbage rankings.
- **No ANN index (ivfflat/hnsw) yet.** Brute-force distance search over a few thousand rows is fast and correct; an approximate index trades correctness for speed you don't need until the table is much larger — revisit under Performance hardening if/when it is.
- **Query-embedding is the one AI call that runs inline, not queued** — see the Built section above for the full reasoning; called out here too since it's a deliberate exception to an otherwise-universal codebase rule, not an inconsistency.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green; `pnpm db:generate` shows no drift; confirmed live against a running Postgres: `\dx` shows `vector 0.8.5` installed, `\d embeddings` shows a proper `vector(768)` column.
- Unit tests: `gemini-embedding-adapter.test.ts` (7 — determinism, correct dimensionality/normalization, all-zero on empty text, and the property that actually matters: texts sharing vocabulary score higher cosine similarity than texts sharing none) — 236 API tests total, all passing.
- Manually verified in a live browser against the real pgvector-backed Postgres: created two tasks ("Draft onboarding paperwork for new hires" / "Fix database migration rollback bug"), searched "paperwork for new hires onboarding" on the Semantic Search page, and got the topically-related task ranked first with the unrelated one ranked second — real cosine-similarity ordering, not a fixture. Pre-existing tasks with no embedding row (created before this milestone) correctly don't appear at all.
- Playwright: `semantic-search.spec.ts` — creates two tasks and confirms a semantic query ranks the topically-matching one first; separately generates two distinct images (two different task/prompt flows, so two independent `image_assets`, not sibling versions of one), opens Semantic Search, picks the first image, and confirms exactly one "similar" result comes back (the other asset) — the full generate → understand → embed (worker) → nearest-neighbor query → render pipeline, not a mocked slice. Full 43-spec suite (up from 42) run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: 43/43 clean both times, no flakes.

### Phase 6 — Data export (2026-07-24)

ROADMAP.md's Phase 6 line is just "data export," no shape specified. Scoped to tasks + hierarchy — the actual work-graph content, not every table in DATA_MODEL.md — same "one thing done completely" call M5.4's REST API (tasks-only) and SCIM (Users-only) already made; docs/goals/comments/custom-field-values are real scope for a later pass, not silently dropped.

Built:

- **Two plain REST GET routes** (`apps/api/src/routes/export.ts`, registered in `index.ts`), not tRPC procedures — a file download needs real `Content-Type`/`Content-Disposition` response headers, the same reason `/uploads` and `/image-versions/:id/thumb` aren't tRPC either. `GET /export/:workspaceId/tasks.csv` and `GET /export/:workspaceId/workspace.json`, both session-cookie authenticated (`getSessionUser` + `can(..., "export:run", ...)`, the same manual-check shape `attachments.ts`'s download route already uses) and logging an `activity` row per download.
- **`export:run`** new `WorkspaceAction`, admin tier — same reasoning as `import:run`: a bulk export is the read-side mirror of a bulk import, an entire workspace's content leaving the app in one file rather than entering it, same blast-radius tier as any other whole-workspace bulk operation.
- **`lib/csv-export.ts`** (pure, unit-tested): `tasksToCsv`, RFC4180-ish serialization — the reverse of `csv-import.ts`'s `parseCsvText`. Column headers (`Name`, `Status`, `Assignee Email`, `Tags`, `Due Date`, …) deliberately match `csv-import.ts`'s own `COLUMN_ALIASES`, so a round-trip export → re-import elsewhere finds its columns without remapping; the extra columns import doesn't look for (`Space`, `List`, `Priority`, `Start Date`, `Completed At`, `Created At`) are simply ignored by it, not a compatibility break.
- **`lib/export.ts`**: one shared `fetchWorkspaceTasks` (a workspace-wide task/status/list/space join plus two grouped-by-task lookups for the assignee/tag many-to-manys, since those can't come out of the single join without duplicating rows) feeds both `buildTasksCsv` and `buildWorkspaceExportJson` — the two formats can't drift on what counts as "this workspace's tasks."
- **`imageAsset.list` reused nothing new here** — export intentionally doesn't include images/attachments in v1 (see Decisions).
- UI: a new "Export data" section on the existing Developer page (`workspace.$workspaceId.developer.tsx`, alongside API keys/webhooks — the natural home for a data-access feature) — two plain `<a download>` links styled with `buttonVariants` (the `Button` component itself has no Radix `asChild`/Slot support in this codebase, so a real anchor tag styled to match was simpler than adding that dependency for one use). Same cookie-based same-origin auth `<img src="/image-versions/:id/thumb">` already relies on — added `/export` to `vite.config.ts`'s dev proxy list alongside `/uploads`/`/image-versions`/`/imports`/`/scim`.

### Decisions

- **Tasks + hierarchy only, not a full workspace dump.** Docs (Yjs binary `ydoc_state` has no meaningful export representation without a rich-text-to-plain-text pass), comments, custom field values, goals, and automations are all real scope a "data export" feature could eventually cover, but ROADMAP.md's one-line phrasing and the size of this session both point at "the work graph, done properly" over "everything, done thinly."
- **CSV export mirrors CSV import's column vocabulary on purpose** — an export a user can't re-import elsewhere (or into another Canvas workspace, once/if CSV import supports re-uploading its own export) is a weaker feature than one that round-trips.
- **No async job/history table**, unlike M5.5's imports. An export is a bounded read (one workspace's current task count, not an unbounded external API crawl) with no possibility of partial failure worth tracking — a direct request/response file download is the simpler, correct-for-this-workload choice, not a corner cut.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green.
- Unit tests: `csv-export.test.ts` (7: header-only with no tasks, correct column order, semicolon-joined multi-assignee/multi-tag, comma/quote/newline escaping, an ordinary unquoted field) — 243 API tests total, all passing.
- Playwright: `data-export.spec.ts` — two specs. The first creates a task, confirms both download links carry the right `href`, then fetches the actual served bytes directly (`page.request.get`, same session cookie the page uses) and asserts on real content: the CSV's exact header row and a data row containing the created task; the JSON's workspace name and task fields. The second confirms a guest gets a 403 hitting the same route directly — the permission check is enforced server-side, not just hidden from guests in the UI. Full 45-spec suite (up from 43) run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: 45/45 clean both times, no flakes.

### Phase 6 — Performance hardening (2026-07-24)

Unlike the previous four Phase 6 items, this wasn't "build a feature" — it was audit-then-fix. Went through every schema file, the query patterns built up across this whole session, the React Query cache config, and a real production build, and fixed what was safe and well-evidenced to fix. Two findings turned out too large/risky to retrofit responsibly in this pass; both are recorded below as explicit, reasoned scope decisions rather than silently dropped.

Built:

- **31 new database indexes** across 13 schema files (migration `0035_classy_the_twelve.sql`) — Postgres only auto-indexes the primary key, never a foreign key, and this schema had exactly 4 explicit indexes across ~55 tables before this pass (two partial-unique on `brain_conversations`, one composite on `brain_messages`, one GIN for task FTS). Went file-by-file matching foreign key columns against the actual `WHERE`/`JOIN` patterns read across every router this session, rather than blindly indexing every FK: `tasks.list_id`/`status_id`/`parent_task_id`, `statuses.list_id`, `spaces.workspace_id`, `folders.space_id`, `lists.space_id`/`folder_id`, `comments.task_id`, `attachments.task_id`, `checklists.task_id` + `checklist_items.checklist_id`, `custom_field_defs.workspace_id` + `custom_field_values.task_id`, `image_assets.workspace_id`, `image_versions.asset_id`, `ai_usage.workspace_id`, `docs.workspace_id` + `doc_task_links.task_id`, `channels.workspace_id` + `messages.channel_id`, `time_entries.task_id`/`user_id`, `activity.workspace_id`, `notifications.user_id` (polled every 30s by every signed-in user — the single hottest one found), `memberships.user_id` (a composite unique already covers `workspace_id`, but not a lookup by `user_id` alone — `workspace.listMine` hits this on every page load), `task_assignees.user_id` and `task_dependencies.depends_on_task_id` (the reverse direction of an existing composite unique/PK whose leading column doesn't serve it), and `embeddings.(workspace_id, entity_type)` for this session's own new semantic-search queries. Two composite **partial** indexes on `reminders` (`WHERE done_at IS NULL`, one system-wide on `remind_at` for the scheduler's own tick, one `(user_id, remind_at)` for `reminder.list`) and one plain index on `recurrence_rules.next_run_at` — both tables are scanned by `scheduler.ts` on every tick, not just per-request, so an unindexed scan there gets slower on every single tick as recurring tasks/reminders accumulate, not just under one slow request.
- **`task.get` parallelized**: five independent, data-independent reads (assignees, tags, subtasks, dependencies, recurrence rule) were awaited one at a time — five sequential round trips on every task-detail-panel open. `Promise.all` turns that into the latency of the slowest one, not the sum of all five.

### Decisions (audited, deliberately not fixed this pass)

- **Board/Gantt/Calendar views aren't virtualized**, unlike `task-list-view.tsx`/`task-table-view.tsx` (which use `@tanstack/react-virtual` per CLAUDE.md's hard rule). Confirmed by grepping for `useVirtualizer` usage against every `.map()`-over-tasks call site. Not fixed here: `task-board.tsx`'s columns are `dnd-kit` `SortableContext`s, and virtualizing a drag-and-drop list means off-screen items unmount, which needs careful coordination with `dnd-kit` to avoid breaking cross-column drags — a real feature-sized change with its own test surface (`board-drag-and-drop.spec.ts` already covers real drag behavior), not something to retrofit at the tail end of an unrelated session. Flagging it here rather than leaving it as a silent gap.
- **React Query's `QueryClient` still has no default `staleTime`** (`main.tsx`, `new QueryClient()` with no options — every query is refetched on every mount/window-refocus). This looked like an easy win given the app already has WS-based cache invalidation (`use-realtime.ts`) — until checking what that WS handler actually covers: only `task`/`status`/`message` entities. Everything else (hierarchy, comments, tags, custom fields, attachments, checklists, docs, dashboards, automations, goals, members…) relies on each mutating component's own local `onSuccess: () => utils.X.invalidate()` to update _that_ user's client; `staleTime: 0` is what currently makes _other_ connected users' clients pick up those changes on their next remount/refocus, as an accidental-but-load-bearing consistency mechanism. Raising `staleTime` globally would introduce real cross-client staleness for every entity type WS doesn't cover — correctness risk, not a safe hardening tweak, without first auditing/extending realtime coverage. Left as-is; noted for a dedicated pass.
- **The web bundle is one 1.39 MB / 414 KB-gzipped chunk** (Vite's own build warning, `apps/web` production build). Investigated the obvious split candidate — TipTap — before attempting anything: it's used in `task-detail-panel.tsx` (task description) and `comments-section.tsx`, both reachable from nearly every list/board view, so it can't be cleanly isolated to one route. Only the Yjs/y-websocket collaborative layer (`doc-collaborative-editor.tsx`) is genuinely Docs-route-specific. A real fix exists (TanStack Router route-level `lazy()` code-splitting, starting with the Docs route's collab dependency, then the lower-traffic routes — Gantt, workload, timesheet, semantic-search, roles, security, developer, import) but touches route registration across the app and needs its own careful Playwright verification pass; not attempted here for the same reason as the virtualization gap.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green; `pnpm db:generate` shows no drift after the migration; applied live against the running Postgres with no errors.
- Unit tests: no new pure logic introduced (this pass was schema + one query reshape, not new business logic) — the existing 243 API tests all still pass unmodified, confirming the index migration and `task.get` parallelization didn't change any observable behavior.
- Full 45-spec Playwright suite run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: 45/45 clean both times, no flakes — the strongest available signal that 31 new indexes and reshaping `task.get` into a `Promise.all` didn't change results, only speed.

## Where things stand after the Phase 6 session

Phases 0–5 were already complete going in. That session added, in order: a dashboard/sidebar UI polish pass, custom roles + per-space permission overrides, semantic + visual search (pgvector), data export, and a performance-hardening pass — the five items requested from Phase 6 ("Enterprise & polish"). Remaining Phase 6 scope per ROADMAP.md, none started: audit log UI, PWA/mobile pass, Tauri desktop wrapper. Also on the board from that session's own audits: Board/Gantt/Calendar virtualization, extending WS realtime invalidation coverage beyond task/status/message, and route-level code-splitting.

## App-wide UX pass (2026-07-24, same day)

A follow-up ask, sequenced (per explicit user choice) as: named surfaces called out by the user first (Chat, Generate, Brain), then notifications, then a design sweep of remaining plain pages, then copy simplification, then the deferred code-splitting.

### Chat: attachments, Brain integration, visual polish — done

Chat already had a "Brain in chat" entry point since M4.3 (an "Ask Brain" button opening `BrainChatPanel` scoped to the channel) — that part of the ask was already built, just easy to miss given how plain the surrounding page was. What was genuinely missing: file attachments, and any real visual design (bare bordered boxes, no avatars, emoji-as-button-glyph).

Built:

- **`attachments.messageId`** (new nullable FK column, migration `0036_strong_vampiro.sql`, indexed) — same shape as the existing `taskId`/`commentId` columns: an attachment belongs to exactly one of the three. Chat attachments reuse the entire existing attachment pipeline (S3 storage, blurhash/thumb generation for images, the `/uploads` REST route) rather than a parallel chat-specific one.
- **`apps/api/src/lib/chat-queries.ts`** (new): `requireChannel`/`requireMessage`/`isChannelMember`/`workspaceIdForMessage` extracted from three near-identical private copies that had accreted in `chat.ts` — needed by `attachment.ts` and the `/uploads` route too now, so this is a genuine shared extraction (`chat.ts` itself now imports from here instead of its own locals), not a parallel implementation.
- **`attachment.list`** now takes either `taskId` or `messageId` (exactly one); **`/uploads`** (`routes/attachments.ts`) now accepts either field the same way, resolving workspace/permission from whichever is given (`message:create` + private-channel-membership check for the chat path, mirroring the existing task path's `attachment:create` check). **Closed a real access gap found while wiring this up**: the file-download route (`/uploads/:id`) only ever checked workspace-level `attachment:view`, which — once messages could have attachments — would have let any workspace member fetch a private channel's files by attachment id alone, bypassing the private-channel-membership gate every other message-reading path enforces. Added the same channel-membership check there.
- **`chat.message.list`** now hydrates each message's `attachments` (a grouped lookup alongside the existing author join, same pattern as this session's `lib/export.ts`), so the channel view renders them with no N+1.
- UI (`channel-messages.tsx`, fully rebuilt): a paperclip button opens a hidden file input; selected files show as removable chips before sending. Since an attachment needs a real `messageId` to link to, sending now creates the message first, then uploads each pending file tagged with the new message's id, then refetches — two round trips, invisible to the user. Rendered messages show image attachments as a `BlurhashThumb` grid opening the existing `Lightbox`, and other files as a small chip with name + size, linking to `/uploads/:id`. Added avatar initials (hashed to one of four accent tints, deterministic per name — no new per-user color storage) and icon-based actions (reply/delete), replacing the emoji glyphs. Channel list and channel header got the same Card/icon treatment as the rest of this app's polished surfaces.

### Decisions

- **No Google Drive "attach by link" option in chat**, unlike task attachments (M5.6). Scoped to real file upload only — chat's need (share a file with the team) is squarely the upload case; the Drive-link path was itself already a fallback for a widget this repo can't build for real (see M5.6 decisions), and duplicating that whole affordance into chat wasn't asked for.
- **Sending with attachments is two sequential requests (create message, then upload), not one atomic call.** tRPC's `chat.message.create` has no file-upload transport (the standing rule this whole codebase follows for uploads), and an attachment's foreign key requires the message to already exist. The brief window where the message exists without its attachment is invisible in practice — the UI only refetches once both requests finish — and if the upload half fails, the message is still there (findable, editable) rather than silently lost, the more forgiving failure mode of the two.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green; `pnpm db:generate` shows no drift.
- API unit tests unchanged at 243 passing (this milestone was schema + query/UI reshaping, no new pure logic to unit-test).
- Manually verified live in the browser (dev stack, real Postgres/Redis/MinIO): typed a message, attached a real file via the hidden input, saw the pending-file chip, sent it, and confirmed the file rendered as a downloadable chip with the correct size — in both light and dark mode.
- Playwright: `chat-attachments.spec.ts` (new) uploads a real fixture file to a message and confirms the served bytes match via a direct authenticated fetch of the resulting `/uploads/:id` URL — not just that a link renders. `chat.spec.ts` updated for three accessible-name changes from the visual pass (the channel heading and channel-list links dropped their literal `#` for a `Hash`/`Lock` icon; the back-link dropped its literal `←` for a `ChevronLeft` icon and picked up an explicit `aria-label="Back to Chat"` once its visible text collided with the sidebar's own "Chat" nav link) — the same "update locators to match real icon-conveyed UI" call this app's Playwright suite has made several times before. Full 46-spec suite (up from 45) run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: both runs 45 clean + 1 flaky-then-passed (`imports.spec.ts`, the same pre-existing mock-server-timing flake noted earlier this session, unrelated to chat) — no regressions.

### Generate panel: UI overhaul — done

Visual-only pass on `generation-panel.tsx` — every piece of state, every mutation, every effect, every `data-testid` is byte-for-byte unchanged; only the JSX markup and styling changed. Deliberately scoped this way given how much intricate async state this component already carries (job-status polling, "follow the tip after a job finishes" vs. "let the user branch from an older node" tracking, target-version-count bookkeeping) — a logic rewrite alongside a visual one would have been a much larger risk for a component this load-bearing (M2.1 through M4.4 all build on it).

Built:

- Wrapped the prompt/options form and the "edit selected version" form in `Card`s; added an icon header (`Wand2`) with an icon-only close button (was a plain "Close" text link). Buttons picked up icons matching their action (`Sparkles` generate, `GitBranch` branch/edit, `Paperclip` attach, `CheckCircle2` promote/current-version) and a spinner (`Loader2`, `animate-spin`) while their mutation is pending, replacing plain "Starting…" text with no visual motion.
- The job-status line now reads as a real status indicator — an icon (spinner while queued/generating, checkmark when done, X on error) plus color from the same `--status-*` tokens the dataviz-driven dashboard work already established (`text-status-good` done, `text-status-critical` error), rather than plain muted-foreground text indistinguishable from everything else on the page.
- Reworded a few labels that only made sense to someone who already knew how version branching works: "Branch from selected" → "Edit the selected version" (a `CardTitle`, not a form label) with its button retitled "Create edited version" (was "Branch / edit"); "Auto description" → "What Brain sees in this image"; "Attach current to task" → "Attach current version to task" (disambiguates from "attach a new one").
- Found and fixed one incidental bug while rewriting the markup: the `<form onSubmit>` wrappers were replaced with `Button onClick={handler}` (dropping the `<form>` since a `Card` can't itself submit) — verified this doesn't change behavior, since the handlers only ever call `.preventDefault()` and otherwise read from closure state, never the event object, and `textarea` elements don't submit forms on Enter anyway (unlike single-line `input`s), so no keyboard behavior was lost. Added an explicit `Enter`-to-submit `onKeyDown` on the edit textarea as a genuine small improvement, since that field is single-purpose enough that Enter-to-submit is expected.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green.
- API unit tests unchanged at 243 (pure visual/markup change, nothing new to unit-test).
- Manually exercised the full pipeline live in the browser: generated two variants, watched the status indicator go spinner → green checkmark "Done", saw the version tree and both thumbnails render, promoted a version, and confirmed the proofing image, "Edit the selected version" card, and "What Brain sees in this image" card all rendered correctly — in both light and dark mode.
- Playwright: no new spec (this was a markup-only pass over already-thoroughly-tested functionality) — ran every existing spec that exercises `GenerationPanel` (`generation-ux`, `iterative-edit`, `version-tree`, `image-proofing`, `image-engine`, `brain-tools`) individually first, all 6 green, confirming the surviving `data-testid` selectors and the one remaining text assertion (`generation-promote` → "Current version", still exact since the added `CheckCircle2` icon contributes no text content) still hold. Full 46-spec suite run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: one run 46/46 clean, the other 45 clean + 1 flaky-then-passed (`docs-collab.spec.ts`, the same pre-existing parallel-load timing flake noted since M4.3) — no regressions.

### Brain panel: UI overhaul — done

Same discipline as the Generate panel: visual-only pass on `brain-chat-panel.tsx`, every hook/handler/mutation/`data-testid` untouched (this component's streaming/tool-status/pending-message reconciliation logic is exactly the kind of thing not worth touching alongside a restyle).

Built:

- Icon header (`Sparkles`) with an icon-only close button. The existing `aria-label="Close"` was already present on the old text button (`✕ Close`) — kept verbatim, so every Playwright spec's `getByRole("button", { name: "Close", exact: true })` (accessible name comes from `aria-label`, not the visible text either way) needed no changes at all.
- Message bubbles restyled with the same accent/card tokens as chat: user messages `bg-accent-soft`, Brain replies `bg-card` with a border, both keeping their existing `data-testid`s. Added a small `Sparkles` glyph next to the "BRAIN" label and a `Wrench` icon on tool-call/tool-result lines, replacing plain text-only labels.
- The status line (tool running / image queued / generating / done) now shows a spinning `Loader2` and uses `text-accent`, instead of being visually identical to ordinary muted body text.
- Added a genuine new empty state ("Ask Brain anything about this {context}") for a freshly-opened conversation with no messages yet — there was none before; the panel just looked broken/blank until the first message arrived.
- Composer's Send button gained a `Send` icon (swapping to a spinner while a message is in flight) — same treatment as Generate/Chat.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green. API unit tests unchanged at 243 (markup-only).
- Manually exercised live in the browser: opened global Brain, saw the new empty state, sent a message, confirmed the mock reply renders in a bordered card with the Brain/sparkle label and the user's own message in an accent-tinted bubble — in both light and dark mode.
- Playwright: ran every spec touching `BrainChatPanel` (`brain-chat`, `brain-tools`, `chat`, `docs-brain`, `image-proofing`) individually first — all 5 green, confirming the title strings (`"Brain — this task"` etc.), the `Send`/`Close` buttons, and every `data-testid` still resolve correctly. Full 46-spec suite run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: second run 46/46 clean; the first run had two failures (`email-digest.spec.ts`, `imports.spec.ts`) in subsystems this milestone never touched (digest-scheduler timing, ClickUp-import mock server) — both passed clean on the immediate re-run, confirming parallel-worker environmental flakiness on this machine rather than a regression.

### Notifications: task assignment + urgent-priority workspace broadcast — done

Two new notification triggers, reusing the existing `activity` + `notifications` tables and `NotificationsBell` rendering path (M3.6/M3.9) rather than a parallel mechanism. Scope, per explicit user choice: assigning a task notifies the assignee; a task moving _into_ `urgent` priority notifies every workspace member.

Built:

- **`apps/api/src/lib/notify.ts`** (new): `notifyUsers(activityId, userIds)` (dedupes, bulk-inserts `notifications` rows) and `notifyWorkspace(activityId, workspaceId, excludeUserId?)` (looks up every membership, excludes the actor). `comment.ts` and `chat.ts`'s pre-existing inline mention-notification inserts were refactored onto `notifyUsers` too, so there's now one insert path instead of three near-identical ones.
- **`task-update.ts`**: added `becameUrgent(oldPriority, newPriority)` — true only on the _transition into_ `urgent`, not "stayed urgent across an unrelated edit" (editing the title of an already-urgent task must not re-broadcast to the whole workspace every time). `undefined` (field omitted from the update) is treated as "unchanged," matching the existing omitted-vs-null convention `buildTaskUpdateFields` already uses. 4 new unit tests.
- **`task.ts`**: `assignees.add` now notifies the new assignee (skipped if self-assigning). `update` checks `becameUrgent` after its existing `task.updated` activity log, and if true, logs a second `task.priority_urgent` activity and calls `notifyWorkspace`, excluding the actor.
- **`NOTIFICATION_VERB_LABELS`** (`packages/shared`) gained `task.assigned` → "assigned you a task" and `task.priority_urgent` → "flagged a task as urgent". `notifications-bell.tsx`'s click-through-to-task logic (`TASK_LINK_VERBS`) extended to cover both new verbs, alongside the existing `comment.created`/`reminder.fired`.
- **Fixed a real (if minor) app bug found while writing the E2E spec**: clicking a notification navigates to `/w/:workspaceId/l/:listId?openTask=:taskId` to deep-link straight to the task panel, but closing that panel only cleared local component state — the `openTask` search param stayed in the URL. Since a `useEffect` re-seeds the panel's open state from `openTask` on every change (needed so a _second_ notification click while already on the same list route still opens the new task), any reload after visiting a notification link would silently re-open that same task panel indefinitely. `workspace.$workspaceId.list.$listId.tsx`'s `onClose` now also clears `openTask` via `navigate({ search: { openTask: undefined } })`. (The workload route's task panel has no `openTask` search param at all, so it wasn't affected.)

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green. API unit tests: 247 passing (243 + 4 new `becameUrgent` cases).
- Playwright: `notifications-assignment-urgent.spec.ts` (new) — two browser contexts (Nia the owner/actor, Bob the invited member); asserts Nia gets no self-notification for either trigger, Bob's badge count and bell contents update correctly for both the assignment and the urgent-flag broadcast, and clicking each notification lands Bob on the right task. Run individually twice cold-start, clean both times. Full 47-spec suite (up from 46) run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: run 1 clean except `brain-tools.spec.ts` flaky-then-passed; run 2 clean except `imports.spec.ts` flaky-then-passed — both are the same pre-existing parallel-worker timing flakes already documented earlier in this file, not regressions; the new spec itself was clean in both full-suite runs.

### Design sweep: remaining plain pages — done

Applied the same Card/icon-header/`--status-*`-token treatment already established (dashboards, roles, chat, generate, brain) to the last seven surfaces that had never been touched since their original bare-bones milestone build: Automations, Goals, Workload, Timesheet, Import, Forms, Docs list. Visual-only across all seven — every hook, mutation, and `data-testid` unchanged.

Built:

- Every page picked up an icon-badge header (`Zap`/`Target`/`Users`/`Clock`/`Upload`/`ClipboardList`/`FileText`) with a one-line subtitle explaining what the page is for — none of these had ever had more than a bare `<h1>`.
- "Create new" forms (automation, goal, form) moved from a raw bordered `<div>` into a `Card`/`CardHeader`/`CardTitle`/`CardContent`. List/table surfaces (automations list, goals list, forms list, import history, timesheet entries) moved from plain `<ul>`/bordered-`<div>` markup into `Card`-based rows, picking up `ChevronRight` affordance arrows where they're links. Docs list switched from a bordered list to the same icon-tile grid `dashboards.tsx` already established, since docs (like dashboards) are files people scan by name, not rows people scan by status.
- Workload/Timesheet's week-prev/next controls became icon buttons (`ChevronLeft`/`ChevronRight` with `aria-label`s) instead of literal "Prev"/"Next" text — checked both specs' locators first (`workload.spec.ts` and `time-tracking.spec.ts` don't reference either button), so nothing broke.
- Import history's per-row status (`pending`/`running`/`done`/`failed`) gained a matching icon (`Clock`/spinning `Loader2`/`CheckCircle2`/`XCircle`) and switched off ad hoc `text-blue-600`/`text-green-600` to the shared `--status-*` tokens (`text-accent`/`text-status-good`/`text-status-critical`) the dashboard/import-history work already established elsewhere — same status vocabulary everywhere in the app now, not a locally invented one.

### Decisions

- **Docs list uses a card grid (like Dashboards), not a bordered row list (like Automations/Goals/Forms).** Both patterns already existed in the app (Phase A's dashboards.tsx vs. this session's row-list treatment); picked per-page by what the item actually is — a named document you scan visually vs. a rule/goal/entry point you scan by state — rather than forcing one pattern everywhere for uniformity's own sake.
- **`workspace.$workspaceId.list.$listId.tsx`'s task-panel `onClose` now also clears the `openTask` search param** (found and fixed while writing the notifications spec, not part of this page-sweep visually, but landed in the same session) — see the Notifications entry above for the full explanation; noting it here too since `list.$listId.tsx` is one of the files this sweep's `useNavigate` import touches.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors; same 49 pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings as before, none new).
- Manually walked all seven pages live in the browser (dev stack, real Postgres/Redis/MinIO) — Automations, Goals, Workload, Timesheet, Import, Forms, Docs — in both light and a manually-forced dark mode (`document.documentElement.classList.add('dark')`, since the app has no in-UI theme toggle — it follows `prefers-color-scheme` — this is the fastest way to spot-check both token sets without changing OS settings). All read cleanly; status colors/icons on the Import history rows were legible in both.
- API unit tests unchanged at 247 (markup-only pass, nothing new to unit-test).
- Playwright: no new specs (pure visual pass over already-covered pages — `automations.spec.ts`, `goals.spec.ts` (via `workspace.$workspaceId.goals.$goalId.tsx`'s own coverage), `workload.spec.ts`, `time-tracking.spec.ts`, `imports.spec.ts`, `forms.spec.ts` all already exercise these routes). Full 47-spec suite run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: both runs 47/47 clean.
- **Infra note, not a code issue**: mid-verification, `imports.spec.ts`'s ClickUp-import case started failing consistently (not just flaking) with `ClickUp API /team failed with status 401` — traced to several leaked `tsx watch` zombie processes (`ps aux`, running since as far back as 1:35pm, none holding a listening port but apparently still able to win an occasional startup race for :3001) left behind by earlier Playwright `CI=true` runs whose `webServer` teardown (`sh -c '... & trap ... EXIT INT TERM; ...'` in `playwright.config.ts`) doesn't reliably reach `tsx watch`'s child process. When a stale process wins that race, it serves the API with its own original env — the real `https://api.clickup.com` default rather than the test's `CLICKUP_API_BASE_URL` mock override — hence a real 401 instead of the mock's always-200 response. `pkill -9 -f "tsx watch"` before each of the two verification runs above confirmed this: clean once the zombies were cleared. Not touched further (a `webServer` teardown robustness fix, unrelated to this milestone's scope) but worth fixing at some point if this recurs — the trap only kills `$worker_pid`, never the `pnpm --filter @canvas/api dev` foreground process's own children.

### Copy/wording simplification pass — done

Audited user-visible copy app-wide (labels, buttons, headings, empty states) for jargon or developer-brained phrasing a non-technical user wouldn't follow, then fixed the highest-value findings. Left the Developer and Security pages' underlying technical detail intact (API tokens, SAML/SCIM field names, webhook signature verification) — those pages are inherently for whoever's wiring up an integration — but added a plain-language sentence in front of each so the "what is this for" question is answered before the "how do I configure it" detail.

Built:

- **Roles & permissions page real fix, not just wording**: the custom-role grant/revoke table and the space-override action picker were showing raw internal action codes verbatim (`customFieldDef:create`, `imageAsset:view`, `hierarchy:delete`) as both section headers and dropdown options — and a space override's principal, for a custom-role target, was rendering the literal `customRole:<uuid>` with no name resolution at all. New `apps/web/src/lib/permission-labels.ts` (`resourceLabel`/`actionLabel`/`principalLabel`, unit tested) maps every one of the 29 `WorkspaceAction` resource prefixes to a human label (e.g. "Spaces & lists", "Generated images", "GitHub PR links") and resolves a `customRole:<id>` principal to that role's actual name via the roles list already loaded on the page. An existing override now reads "Guest — Tasks — create is allowed" instead of "role:guest — task:create is allowed".
- **Automation run history stopped dumping raw JSON.** `run.logJson` (an array of `{action, ok, detail?, error?}` entries) was rendered as a literal `<pre>{JSON.stringify(...)}</pre>` block — the single worst offender found in the audit, since it's shown to anyone who set up an automation, not just developers. New `describeAutomationRunLogEntry` (`apps/web/src/lib/format.ts`, unit tested) turns each entry into one plain sentence ("Added a tag", "Posted a comment", "Sending a Slack message failed: <error>").
- Sidebar nav + page title: "Semantic Search" / "Semantic & visual search" → "Smart Search" / "Smart search" — "semantic" is ML jargon; the page's own search-tasks copy already avoided it ("Search tasks by meaning") but the nav label and h1 hadn't caught up. Also dropped an "an embedding of your query is compared against every task's stored embedding" sentence that leaked the implementation detail for no user benefit.
- Both automation-builder pages (the list page's "New automation" form and the dedicated editor page) gained one framing sentence above the When/Only if/Then fields: "Watch for something happening, optionally only when a condition is true, then do something automatically" — those three building blocks (trigger/condition/action) were previously introduced with zero context for a first-time user.
- Task dependency kind ("blocks"/"waiting_on") now displays as "Blocks"/"Waiting on" in both the add-dependency dropdown and the existing-dependency list — was a raw snake_case enum value in both places.
- Dashboard "Burndown" widget renamed to "Tasks remaining over time" in both the widget-type picker and the widget's own title — Agile jargon with no explanation.
- Developer page's Webhooks section and Security page's SSO/SCIM sections each gained one plain-language lead-in sentence before their existing technical paragraph (kept, labeled "Technical detail:") — e.g. webhooks: "Sends a notification to another tool the moment something you pick below happens here"; SSO: "Connect your company's login system (like Okta or Google Workspace) so people sign in here with the account they already use for work"; SCIM section retitled "Automatic member sync" (was "SCIM provisioning") with the same treatment.
- Generate panel: "How many variants" → "How many versions to create", matching the "version"/"version tree" vocabulary used everywhere else in that same panel instead of introducing a synonym.
- Notification bell's defensive fallback (`n.verb` with no matching label) now shows "sent you an update" instead of the raw internal verb string (e.g. `task.priority_urgent`) it would otherwise leak if a future verb is ever added without a label.

### Decisions

- **Left `automation-actions-editor.tsx`'s webhook-event checkboxes showing raw dotted values (`task.created`, `task.status_changed`) as-is.** Audited and initially flagged, but that string genuinely is the exact event name a developer's webhook receiver will see in the payload — showing it verbatim is the _correct_, precise choice for that specific field, not jargon needing translation. Not every technical-looking string is a bug; only fixed strings that were internal implementation detail with no reason to be user-facing (action codes, embeddings, JSON dumps).
- **Didn't touch the Developer/Security pages' field-level labels** (IdP entity id, SCIM base URL, `X-Canvas-Signature`, etc.) — these pages are inherently for whoever configures the integration, and that person needs the exact technical terms their IdP/receiver expects. Added a plain-language sentence in front of each section instead of translating the fields themselves, which would have made the page useless for its actual job.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green.
- API unit tests unchanged at 247 (no backend logic touched). Web unit tests: 15 passing (up from 9) — 6 new for `permission-labels.ts`, 6 new for `describeAutomationRunLogEntry` (both pure-logic additions per the testing-bar rule), all previously-passing web tests unaffected.
- Manually walked Roles (both the grant/revoke table and a live space override), Automations (new-automation form + a real run's history), Security, Developer, and Smart Search live in the browser (dev stack, real Postgres) — every relabeled surface confirmed rendering correctly, including a real space override reading "Guest — Tasks — create is allowed".
- Playwright: fixed two specs whose locators depended on the old raw strings — `automations.spec.ts` (asserted literal `add_tag`/`post_comment` in the run log, now asserts "Added a tag"/"Posted a comment") and `custom-roles.spec.ts` (asserted lowercase `guest` in the override list, now asserts "Guest" since principals are capitalized) — plus `semantic-search.spec.ts`'s two nav-link locators updated from "Semantic Search" to "Smart Search". Full 47-spec suite run twice cold-start with `CI=true` after both fixes: run 1 clean except `docs-brain.spec.ts` flaky-then-passed (documented Vite `/ws` proxy timing flake); run 2 clean except `imports.spec.ts` flaky-then-passed (documented ClickUp-mock flake) — no regressions from the copy changes themselves.

### Performance: route-level code-splitting — done

Picked up the code-splitting gap Phase 6's own hardening pass had audited and deferred (see that section's decisions, above): the production bundle was one 1.41 MB chunk (418 KB gzipped), and the one dependency actually isolable to a single route — Yjs/y-websocket plus the TipTap collaboration extensions, used only by the Docs editor's real-time layer — was still shipping in every page load regardless of whether anyone ever opened a doc.

Built:

- **`DocCollaborativeEditor` (the component wrapping Yjs/y-websocket/`@tiptap/extension-collaboration*`) is now `React.lazy()`-loaded**, wrapped in a `<Suspense fallback={<p>Loading editor…</p>}>` in `workspace.$workspaceId.docs.$docId.tsx` (the doc route already had its own "Loading…" states for the doc-not-found and doc-loading cases, so this matches the existing pattern rather than inventing a new one).
- **Split `insertImageVersionIntoEditor` out into its own file** (new `apps/web/src/components/doc-image-insert.ts`) — it was defined in the same module as `DocCollaborativeEditor` but is a plain TipTap-command function with no Yjs dependency, called from the doc route's "Brain generated an image" callback. Left in the original file, any static import of it (needed eagerly, unlike the editor component) would have pulled Yjs back into the main chunk regardless of the `React.lazy()` on the component — the whole point of a JS module is that importing anything from it runs the whole thing.
- Confirmed with a real production build (`vite build`): the collab dependency chunk split out cleanly as its own 130 KB file (41.6 KB gzipped), and the main chunk dropped from 1.41 MB → 1.28 MB (418 KB → 377 KB gzipped) — a real ~41 KB / ~11% cut to what every page load fetches, paid only by whoever actually opens a doc.

### Decisions

- **Did not extend route-level splitting to the other routes named in the original audit** (Workload, Timesheet, Smart Search, Roles, Security, Developer, Import). Investigated first: none of them have a heavy dependency of their own the way Docs has Yjs — `@tanstack/react-table` and `@tanstack/react-virtual` are needed by the list route's _default_ view so can't be deferred away for most sessions; `@dnd-kit` (Board view) and TipTap itself (used broadly across task descriptions/comments/chat, confirmed back in the original hardening pass) aren't isolable to one low-traffic route either. Splitting the 7 named routes would only move their own route-component code (≈1,670 combined lines, a rough estimate of 5–8 KB gzipped total) into separate chunks — real but small, for the real added risk and effort of restructuring each into a route-definition file plus a separately-imported page file (TanStack Router's code-based routing requires that split for a route's own component to be deferrable, unlike the inline-component case Docs was). Presented this tradeoff and the user chose to stop at the Docs split rather than take on that risk for a small remainder — documented here per CLAUDE.md's "note the decision" rule rather than left implicit.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green. `vite build` (production) succeeds, confirms the chunk split described above.
- Manually verified live in the browser (dev stack): created a new doc, watched it load through the `Suspense` fallback into a working editor showing "Synced", typed into it, confirmed the collaborative editor is fully functional through the lazy boundary — in both light and dark mode.
- API unit tests unchanged at 247; web unit tests unchanged at 15 (pure lazy-loading restructure, no new pure logic).
- Playwright: no spec changes needed — `docs-brain.spec.ts` and `docs-collab.spec.ts` already exercise the doc editor end-to-end (including waiting for "Synced" status), so they're the regression check for the `Suspense` boundary working correctly, not a new test. Full 47-spec suite run twice cold-start with `CI=true`: both runs 47/47 clean, no flakes.

## Where things stand after the app-wide UX pass

All 7 items from the 2026-07-24 UX-improvement request are complete: Chat attachments/Brain/polish, Generate panel overhaul, Brain panel overhaul, task-assignment + urgent-priority notifications, a design sweep of every previously-bare page, an app-wide copy simplification pass, and Docs' Yjs/y-websocket route-level code-split. Remaining known gaps, all audited and documented rather than silently skipped: Board/Gantt/Calendar view virtualization, WS realtime invalidation coverage beyond task/status/message, and the small remaining route-split opportunity on the 7 lower-traffic admin pages (see decision above) — plus Phase 6's own still-open scope (audit log UI, PWA/mobile, Tauri desktop wrapper).

## 2026-07-27 UX request: Brand Kits, Roles cleanup, Slack-style Chat, chat downloads — done

User feedback: several pages were "hard to understand and work with," specifically the Roles page ("a mess" — a dense grant/revoke checkbox matrix with no visual hierarchy), plus three concrete asks — move brand settings off Home into their own page supporting multiple named client profiles, redesign Chat toward a familiar chat-app pattern, and make chat file/image attachments explicitly downloadable. Planned and built as four vertical slices; full details in `/Users/olsisopi/.claude/plans/linear-booping-fox.md`.

Built:

- **Brand Kits (schema → API → UI).** `brand_settings` went from `unique(workspace_id)` (one config per workspace) to a real one-to-many table: added `name` and `is_default` columns (migration `0037_loud_the_professor.sql`, with a data-migration statement marking every pre-existing row as its workspace's default so existing generation/Brain behavior didn't regress). Added a nullable `spaces.brand_kit_id` FK (`onDelete: set null`) so a Space can override the workspace default. New `apps/api/src/lib/brand-kit.ts` (`resolveEffectiveBrandKit`) is the single place owning the fallback rule (space's own kit → workspace default → none), used by the new `brandKit` tRPC router (replacing `brandSettings`: `list`/`get`/`create`/`update`/`delete`/`setDefault`/`getEffective`), by `image-engine`'s provider selection, and by `imageAsset.generate`'s palette lookup — `spaceId` threads from `GenerationPanel` (resolved from whichever `listId` is in view) through the mutation and the BullMQ job payload to the worker. New `/w/:workspaceId/brand-kits` page: kit cards (palette swatches, tone, Default badge, Make default/Edit/Delete) plus a **Space assignments** list (one row per space, a `<select>` to pick its kit or "Use workspace default") — reused the workspace's existing per-member-row+select pattern (`MembersPanel`) rather than the Roles page's heavier select→select→button override flow. Kept the `brandSettings:view`/`brandSettings:update` `can()` action names and the physical `brand_settings` table name unchanged (no reason to churn `can.ts`/`can.test.ts` or risk an interactive rename migration) — only the TS-level export/router/nav-facing name is `brandKit`/"Brand Kits".
- **Roles page real redesign, not just restyle.** The per-resource grant/revoke `<table>` of raw checkboxes became a filterable grid of resource cards, each action rendered as a compact segmented pill-pair instead of two bare checkboxes — implemented as real `<input type="checkbox">`s visually hidden under a styled label (`opacity-0 absolute inset-0` sized to the whole pill, not `sr-only`, which Playwright couldn't hit-test through) so every existing `aria-label="Grant/Revoke <action>"` locator kept working untouched. Added a text filter above the grid (the actual fix for "a wall of checkboxes"). Restyled the custom-role and space-override list rows with an icon tile + name + count/allow-deny pill, matching the Card-row language already established elsewhere (Dashboards/Goals) rather than inventing a new one.
- **Chat restructured into a persistent split layout.** `/chat` and `/chat/$channelId` used to be two full-navigation pages (switching channels meant leaving the message view, hence the old "Back to Chat" link). `workspace.$workspaceId.chat.tsx` is now a layout route (channel rail + `<Outlet/>`, mirroring the existing `workspaceShellRoute`/`workspaceHomeRoute` nesting pattern) with `chatIndexRoute` (empty state) and `chatChannelRoute` (path now `/$channelId`, relative) nested under it. The message pane is a true `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` + pinned composer layout instead of a centered scrolling page.
- **Chat messages: grouping + thread side panel.** Consecutive messages from the same author within 5 minutes collapse (repeat avatar/name only on a new cluster) via a new pure `shouldStartNewGroup` (`apps/web/src/lib/chat-grouping.ts`, unit tested). Replying moved from an inline indented block to a slide-in right panel reusing `BrainChatPanel`'s exact overlay pattern (`fixed inset-0 z-[60] flex justify-end`, `w-full max-w-md border-l shadow-xl`) for visual consistency; the parent message shows a compact "N replies · latest snippet" preview in the main list (a lightweight `plainTextPreview` text extraction, not a whole second TipTap editor instance) so the thread's latest content is still visible without opening the panel.
- **Chat/image downloads.** `/uploads/:attachmentId` (`apps/api/src/routes/attachments.ts`) now honors a `?download=1` query flag, sending `Content-Disposition: attachment` instead of the always-`inline` behavior everywhere else (thumbnails, task attachments, the existing click-to-preview link are all unchanged). Added a distinct, always-discoverable Download icon button next to every image thumbnail and file row in chat (hover-visible on images, matching the existing delete-button overlay convention from `attachments-section.tsx`), and to the Lightbox's full-image toolbar for parity — separate from the existing click-to-preview affordance, as requested, not replacing it. Also sanitized `fileName` before it goes into the `Content-Disposition` header (strips quotes/control characters) — a small hardening while already touching that exact line, since the value is an attacker-controlled uploaded filename.

### Decisions

- **`space.update`'s existing mutation grew an optional `brandKitId` field instead of a new `space.setBrandKit` endpoint.** The mutation already supported partial `name`/`icon` patches with the same `hierarchy:update` permission check a brand-kit assignment needs — a second endpoint would have duplicated that check for no benefit.
- **No unit test for `resolveEffectiveBrandKit`.** It's a DB-querying function (`db.query.spaces`/`db.query.brandSettings`), and this repo's existing convention (confirmed against every `apps/api/src/lib/*.test.ts` file) only unit-tests DB-free pure logic extracted out of DB-touching modules — nothing here mocks Drizzle. Its fallback-order behavior (space kit wins → workspace default → none) is instead covered end-to-end by a new Playwright case in `generation-ux.spec.ts` against a real Postgres database, consistent with how the rest of the codebase verifies DB-touching logic.
- **`generation-ux.spec.ts` and `image-engine.spec.ts` rewritten, not left alone** — both drove the old Home-page single-brand panel (`brand-palette-input`, "Save brand", `brand-image-engine`), which no longer exists. Rewrote both against the new Brand Kits page, keeping the same underlying assertions, and added a new `generation-ux.spec.ts` case that creates two kits, assigns one to a space, and confirms the generation panel's "Use brand colors" checkbox reflects the space-specific kit inside that space vs. the empty default elsewhere — the actual regression check for the new resolver.
- **`chat.spec.ts`'s reply flow updated from positional `.first()` locators to explicit container scoping** (`page.getByTestId("thread-panel").getByTestId(...)` vs. the main `channel-messages` container) — the old comment explaining "reply composer renders first in DOM order" no longer applies now that replies open in a side panel rather than an inline block; scoping by container is also more robust than an ordinal assumption regardless.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors; same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warning pattern, count grew only from the new page/component files, no new _kinds_ of warnings).
- API unit tests: 247 passing (unchanged — nothing new here is DB-free pure logic per the decision above). Web unit tests: 19 passing (up from 15 — 4 new for `shouldStartNewGroup`'s grouping window/author-change/no-previous-message cases).
- Playwright: full suite run twice cold-start. 45/48 passing both times; the same 3 specs failed both runs (`imports.spec.ts`'s ClickUp case, `integrations.spec.ts`'s GitHub PR case, `email-digest.spec.ts`) — traced to a long-running `pnpm dev` API process that predates this session (started ~09:36, confirmed via `ps -p <pid> -o lstart=`) and that Playwright's local `reuseExistingServer: true` config reuses instead of spawning its own — so none of the three specs' required env vars (`CLICKUP_API_BASE_URL`, `GITHUB_API_BASE_URL`, `SCHEDULER_TICK_MS`/`DIGEST_INTERVAL_MS`, all only injected by Playwright's own `webServer` block) were actually set, sending those specs against the real external APIs/real intervals instead of the test's local mocks/fast ticks. This is the same failure mode PROGRESS.md already documented for the ClickUp case specifically; not a regression from this session's changes (none of the three specs touch brand kits, roles, chat, or attachments) and would not reproduce in real CI (`isCI` forces a fresh, correctly-configured `webServer`). Did not stop the pre-existing dev server to confirm further, since it wasn't started by this session and may be in active use. All specs that exercise this session's actual changes passed clean on every run: both `generation-ux.spec.ts` cases, `image-engine.spec.ts`, `custom-roles.spec.ts`, `chat.spec.ts`, `chat-attachments.spec.ts` (including its new Download-button assertion via `page.waitForEvent("download")`).
- Manually walked all four surfaces live in the browser (dev stack, real Postgres/Redis/MinIO): created a brand kit (auto-marked Default), confirmed the Home-page teaser card and new "Brand Kits" nav entry; filtered and toggled a Grant pill on the Roles page; created a Chat channel and confirmed the persistent rail, sent a message, replied via the thread panel, confirmed the "1 reply · latest text" preview renders in the main list; attached a file to a message and confirmed its distinct Download icon button downloads the file (separate from the existing click-to-preview link).

## 2026-07-27 follow-up: Home/login task surfacing, org roles, chat drag-drop, Finance/Timesheet view, more widgets — done

Same-day follow-up request after the Brand Kits/Roles/Chat/Downloads work above: new tasks and urgent/assigned-to-you tasks were invisible unless you happened to open the right list; the user described their actual team structure (2 owners, an admin, an "Operations Manager" who mostly assigns work, a "Finance Manager" focused on time/AI cost, and general staff/designers who shouldn't create tasks or see admin pages) and asked the app to reflect it, plus drag-and-drop chat uploads and a couple of UI papercuts (Brain/Generate already close on outside click via their existing backdrop button — confirmed by reading both before touching anything; only the notification bell needed the fix). Planned and built as nine vertical slices; full details in `/Users/olsisopi/.claude/plans/linear-booping-fox.md`. Per the user's answers up front: org roles are custom-role templates on the existing grant/revoke system (no schema/enum change), task creation = Owners + Admins + Operations Manager, and the login notification is a fresh-each-login in-app summary (not tied to the notification log or the email digest's cursor — reusing that cursor would desync the real digest).

Built:

- **`task.highlights` (new, `apps/api/src/trpc/routers/task.ts`)** — one workspace-wide query returning two small arrays in a single round trip: `priority` (urgent tasks OR tasks assigned to the caller, via an `EXISTS` subquery against `taskAssignees` rather than a join, so a multi-assignee task doesn't come back duplicated) and `recent` (last 8 created). Modeled on `workload.assignments`'s existing `tasks ⋈ taskAssignees ⋈ lists ⋈ spaces` join skeleton, same `task:view` (guest-tier) gate — this is read visibility, not a new sensitive surface. A second small query attaches `assigneeIds` per returned task id (simpler than an `array_agg`, and the result sets are already capped small).
- **Home page** (`workspace.$workspaceId.index.tsx`) gained "Top priority" and "Recently added" sections above the existing Brand Kits teaser, backed by `task.highlights`, each row linking to `/l/$listId?openTask=$id` (the search param the notification-click flow already established). Shared `TaskHighlightRow` (new `apps/web/src/components/task-highlight-row.tsx`) renders a title, priority pill, assignee names (resolved against `workspace.members`), and a space/list breadcrumb — reused as-is by the login summary panel below.
- **Login-time summary panel** — no server-side "just logged in" signal exists and none was added (no `lastLoginAt` column); a `sessionStorage` flag (`canvas:justLoggedIn`, exported from the new `login-summary-panel.tsx` so `login.tsx` sets the exact same key it reads) set right before `login.tsx`'s post-login navigation, read-and-cleared once by a new `<LoginSummaryPanel>` mounted in `WorkspaceShell`. Shows `task.highlights`'s `priority` list as a dismissible slide-in panel matching `BrainChatPanel`'s exact overlay pattern; only renders once fetched data confirms there's actually something urgent/assigned (no empty popup on every login). Deliberately not wired into signup (a brand-new account has nothing to summarize) or the Google OAuth path (that flow round-trips through the API's own redirect, never touching `login.tsx`'s client-side `onSuccess` — a known, documented gap, not silently missed).
- **Chat drag-and-drop** — `channel-messages.tsx`'s `MessageComposer` wrapper gained `onDragEnter`/`onDragLeave`/`onDrop` (with a depth counter so dragging over child nodes like the TipTap editor doesn't flicker the highlight) merging dropped files into the same `pendingFiles` state the file-picker button already used — one shared upload path either way.
- **Org role templates + `timeEntry:viewAll`.** New `WorkspaceAction` `timeEntry:viewAll` (admin-tier `MIN_ROLE`, same tier as `export:run` — seeing every member's individual time entries and AI spend is a bigger blast radius than `dashboard:view`'s pre-aggregated cost figure). Roles page gained three "Quick start" pills (`ROLE_TEMPLATES` in `workspace.$workspaceId.roles.tsx`) that pre-fill the existing custom-role builder's name/base-role/grants/revokes state — still editable, not auto-submitted: **Operations Manager** (base member, no changes — member tier already includes task:create/task:update; this template exists purely for organizational clarity in the assignment dropdown), **Finance Manager** (base member, grants `timeEntry:viewAll`), **Staff** (base member, revokes `task:create` — only Owners/Admins/Operations Manager create tasks per the clarified answer). Entirely additive to the existing custom-role system; the global `task:create` default stays member-tier, so nothing changes for any workspace that doesn't explicitly assign the Staff role to someone.
- **Platform nav gating + Brand Kits move.** `WorkspaceNav` now calls `trpc.workspace.listMine` itself (same query key `WorkspaceShell` already fetches — confirmed this dedupes rather than double-fetching) and hides the "Platform" nav group unless the caller's base role is owner/admin, closing the gap where the nav showed Import/Developer/Security/Roles links to people every one of those pages already rejects server-side (all admin/owner `MIN_ROLE` by default, confirmed by re-reading `can.ts` in full before making any permission changes this session). Brand Kits moved from Platform into Collaborate (per direct request — it's brand/creative content, not an admin-only concern; also already guest-tier `brandSettings:view`, consistent with living outside Platform).
- **Timesheet "Team overview" (Finance Manager).** New `timeEntry.workspaceTimesheet` (same shape as the existing self-only `timesheet`, but across all members, gated by `timeEntry:viewAll`) and a new `aiUsage` router (`workspaceCost`, same gate) exposing the workspace-wide AI-cost query that used to live only inlined in the Dashboards `ai_usage_cost` widget. `timesheet.tsx` gained a "Team overview" section calling both with `enabled: true` unconditionally; a 403 from either just means the section doesn't render (graceful hide, no error banner) rather than adding new client-side permission-introspection plumbing for what's the common case (most viewers won't have the grant). Owners/admins see it automatically (admin-tier `MIN_ROLE` default), matching "owners/admins can see everything" without needing any custom role assigned.
- **Two new Dashboard widget types**: `assignee_breakdown` and `priority_breakdown` (open-task counts grouped by assignee/priority, donut charts reusing the existing `DonutChart`/`CountBreakdownWidget` pattern already established by `task_counts`). Both are plain group-by-count queries, no new tables. Fixed a latent bug while extending the widget-creation dropdown's config-building logic (`workspace.$workspaceId.dashboards.$dashboardId.tsx`): the old `HAS_DAYS_CONFIG.has(type) ? {...} : {type: "task_counts"}` fallback silently substituted `task_counts` for _any_ no-config type, including the original `task_counts` itself by coincidence — harmless before since it was the only no-config type, but would have silently mis-submitted both new ones. Replaced with an explicit `NO_CONFIG_TYPES` set covering all three no-config types.
- **Notifications bell: close on outside click.** Added a wrapper `ref` + a `mousedown` document listener (cleaned up on unmount) — the only one of the three overlays (Brain, Generate, Notifications) that didn't already have this, confirmed by reading all three before touching anything; Brain/Generate already close via their full-screen backdrop button.

### Decisions

- **Found and fixed a real accessibility/testability bug in `TaskHighlightRow` while writing its first e2e coverage**: the link's computed accessible name included the full row's nested text (title + assignees + space/list breadcrumb), so a highlight row for a task in e.g. a "Backlog" list would substring-match `getByRole("link", {name: "Backlog"})` anywhere else on the page — broke two _pre-existing, unrelated_ specs (`comments-mentions-notifications.spec.ts`, `task-templates.spec.ts`) that happened to have same-named lists. Fixed with an explicit `aria-label={"Open " + task.title}` on the link, leaving the visible breadcrumb text untouched — not a test-only workaround, a real fix, since the same substring-collision risk exists for any future test or screen-reader user regardless of this session's specs.
- **`apps/e2e/tsconfig.json` gained `"lib": ["ES2022", "DOM"]`** (previously `ES2022` only) so the new chat drag-and-drop spec can reference `DataTransfer`/`File` inside a `page.evaluateHandle` browser-context callback — scoped to the e2e package only, not the app itself.
- **No unit tests for `task.highlights`, `workspaceTimesheet`, or `aiUsage.workspaceCost`** — all three are DB-querying, and per this repo's established convention (confirmed again this session) only DB-free pure logic gets a unit test; these are covered end-to-end instead (`home-highlights.spec.ts`, `role-templates.spec.ts`'s Team-overview assertions).

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors; same pre-existing warning pattern).
- API unit tests unchanged at 247 (per the decision above — nothing new here is DB-free pure logic). Web unit tests unchanged at 19 (no new pure logic introduced this round).
- Playwright: 6 new specs/cases (`home-highlights.spec.ts` ×2, `role-templates.spec.ts`, `chat-drag-drop.spec.ts`, `dashboards.spec.ts`'s new breakdown-widgets case, `notifications-bell-outside-click.spec.ts`), all passing, plus the `task-highlight-row.tsx` aria-label fix confirmed against the two pre-existing specs it had broken. Full 54-spec suite run twice cold-start: 50/54 clean both times, with the same 4 pre-existing environmental failures both runs (`imports.spec.ts` ClickUp, `integrations.spec.ts` GitHub PR, `email-digest.spec.ts` — all the same long-running-dev-server-missing-test-env-vars cause documented earlier in this file; `docs-collab.spec.ts` — the documented Vite `/ws` proxy timing flake, confirmed passing in isolation on retry) — none touch any file changed this session.
- Manually walked the new Home sections (urgent + assigned task both surfaced, correct priority badge and breadcrumb), the Roles page's three Quick-start template pills (Staff correctly pre-selects "Tasks → create → Revoke" in red), and the new `assignee_breakdown` dashboard widget rendering a real donut chart against live data — all in a real browser against the dev stack.

## 2026-07-27 follow-up #2: custom roles are selectable at invite time — done

Small follow-up: a newly-created custom role only showed up where you assign it to an _existing_ member (Home's Members panel, already dynamic) and in the Roles page's own space-override picker (also already dynamic) — but not in the invite flow itself, which only offered the four base roles. Inviting someone as e.g. "Staff" meant inviting them as a base role first and then remembering to go assign the custom role separately.

Built:

- **`invites` table gained a nullable `custom_role_id`** (migration `0038_clever_angel.sql`, FK to `custom_roles`, `onDelete: set null` — same relationship shape as `memberships.customRoleId`). `inviteMemberSchema` gained an optional `customRoleId`; `workspace.invite` validates it belongs to the same workspace (same check `role.assignToMember` already does, not shared as a helper since each router already keeps its own small local helpers — e.g. this file's own `slugify`); `workspace.acceptInvite` copies it onto the membership it creates.
- **Invite form** (`apps/web/src/routes/index.tsx`): the "Invite role" `<select>` gained a second `<optgroup>` listing the workspace's custom roles (`trpc.role.list`, gated the same as the invite button itself — owner/admin, matching `customRole:view`'s admin tier).
