Custom-role option values are prefixed (`custom:<id>`) so they can't collide with a bare base-role value; picking one looks up that role's own `baseRole` to fill the required base `role` field invites still need. Base-role option values were left untouched (bare `"member"`/`"admin"`/`"guest"`) specifically so every existing e2e spec calling `inviteAndGetLink(page, email, "admin")`'s `.selectOption(role)` kept working unchanged — confirmed by re-running `member-management.spec.ts` and `custom-roles.spec.ts` before writing any new coverage.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green. Shared/API typecheck clean after the schema/migration change.
- New `invite-custom-role.spec.ts`: creates a "Staff" role, confirms it appears in the invite dropdown's own optgroup, invites someone directly with it, and confirms both that the Home page shows the custom role assigned immediately on acceptance (no manual follow-up step) and that the invited member is actually blocked from creating a task, same effect as the manual-assignment path already covered elsewhere.
- Re-ran `member-management.spec.ts`, `custom-roles.spec.ts`, `role-templates.spec.ts`, and both `saml-scim.spec.ts` cases (SCIM also creates memberships, a nearby code path) — all clean, no regressions from the schema change.

## 2026-07-27 follow-up #3: private chat channel member management — done

User asked how to add someone to a private channel; investigation found the backend already had `channel.members.add`/`remove` (`apps/api/src/trpc/routers/chat.ts`) fully implemented and permission-gated, but nothing in the frontend called them — creating a private channel left only the creator as a member with no way to invite anyone else in.

Built:

- **`channel.members.list`** (new procedure, same file): joins `channelMembers` → `users`, gated identically to every other channel-members action (`channel:view` + must already be a member if the channel is private). Backing schema `listChannelMembersSchema` added to `packages/shared/src/schemas/chat.ts`.
- **`ChannelMembersPanel`** (new `apps/web/src/components/channel-members-panel.tsx`): a slide-in side panel matching the same overlay convention already established this session (Brain/Generate/Login-summary/thread panels) — lists current members with a per-row Remove button (hidden on your own row, so you can't lock yourself out by accident), and an "Add someone…" picker sourced from `workspace.members` filtered down to people not already in the channel.
- Wired a **"Members" button** into the channel header (`workspace.$workspaceId.chat.$channelId.tsx`), shown only when `channel.isPrivate` — a public channel is already visible/postable by every workspace member, so there's nothing to manage there. Any current member of a private channel can add or remove others (no separate "channel owner" concept exists — matches the backend's existing permission model, which gates on membership, not creator status).

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green.
- New `private-channel-members.spec.ts`: creates a private channel (only the creator is a member), confirms a second workspace member can't see it in their channel list yet, adds them via the new Members panel, confirms they can now see/open/post in it, then removes them and confirms it disappears from their list again.
- Re-ran `chat.spec.ts`, `chat-attachments.spec.ts`, `chat-drag-drop.spec.ts`, and `comments-mentions-notifications.spec.ts` — all clean, no regressions.

## 2026-07-27 follow-up #4: explicit brand kit picker in Generate and Brain — done

User asked for an option in both the Generate panel and Brain to attach a brand kit directly, so its palette/tone/guidelines feed that image generation or Brain conversation. Generate already auto-resolved a kit from space context (M2.4/earlier this session); this adds an explicit override on top, and gives Brain brand awareness for the first time — `brain-system-prompt.ts` had carried a stale comment since M2.2 ("`brand_settings` doesn't exist yet") noting this was always the intended design, just never wired up once brand kits actually shipped.

Built:

- **`resolveEffectiveBrandKit`** (`apps/api/src/lib/brand-kit.ts`) gained a third, highest-priority input: an explicit `brandKitId` wins over the space's own kit, which wins over the workspace default — same single fallback rule now used by four call sites instead of three (Generate, the image engine's provider selection, `brandKit.getEffective`, and Brain).
- **Brain**: `brain_conversations` gained a nullable `brand_kit_id` (migration `0039_rare_steel_serpent.sql`). New `brain.setBrandKit` mutation (owner-checked, validates the kit belongs to the conversation's workspace) lets the panel attach/clear one on an existing conversation at any time — not just at creation. `buildSystemPrompt` gained an optional second `brand` parameter appended identically across every context branch (global/task/doc/channel), and `worker.ts` resolves it fresh from the conversation row on every job run (not snapshotted into message history), so changing the brand kit mid-conversation affects the next reply. `BrainChatPanel` gained a "Brand" `<select>` (hidden entirely if the workspace has no kits yet) under the header.
- **Generate**: `GenerationPanel` gained a "Brand kit" `<select>` ("Auto (workspace/space default)" plus every kit by name) alongside the existing "Use brand colors" checkbox — picking one threads `brandKitId` through `generateImageAssetSchema` → the queued image job → `getImageEngineForWorkspace` (so the explicit choice governs the image _provider_ too, not just the palette, consistent with how space/workspace-default resolution already worked).

### Decisions

- **The mock Brain client (`apps/api/src/brain/mock-client.ts`) explicitly ignores `systemPrompt`** (echoes the user's text back verbatim) — confirmed before writing any e2e coverage, so the actual prompt-injection behavior is unit-tested (`brain-system-prompt.test.ts`, 4 new cases) rather than asserted through a Playwright run that couldn't observe it either way. E2e coverage instead targets the UI/persistence plumbing: the Generate panel's override actually changing which palette applies, and a Brain-panel brand selection surviving a close/reopen of the same conversation.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green. API unit tests: 251 passing (up from 247 — 4 new `buildSystemPrompt` brand cases).
- New `brand-kit-in-brain-and-generate.spec.ts` (2 cases), both passing.
- Full 58-spec suite run cold-start: 55/58 clean, the same 3 pre-existing environmental failures already documented earlier in this file (none touch brand kits, Brain, or Generate) — re-ran `generation-ux.spec.ts` (both cases), `image-engine.spec.ts`, `brain-chat.spec.ts`, `brain-tools.spec.ts`, and `docs-brain.spec.ts` individually first to confirm zero regressions before the full run.

## 2026-07-27 follow-up #5: UI/UX design review + top-3 fixes — done

Live, hands-on design review of the whole app (every nav page, all 5 list views, the task detail panel) in a fresh test workspace — not a code read, an actual click-through. Findings were written up and published as an Artifact (cross-cutting issues, page-by-page notes, what's already working, a priority-ordered fix list). User then asked to implement the top 3 cross-cutting findings immediately rather than just filing the report.

Built:

- **List and Table views can now create a task.** Both were the only 2 of 5 task views with no add-task affordance at all (Board and Calendar already had one) — List's default view had no way to get a workspace started. Extracted Board's local `NewTaskForm` into a shared `apps/web/src/components/new-task-inline-form.tsx` (`NewTaskInlineForm`, used by all three views now instead of duplicated once more) and added a "+ Add task" control to `task-list-view.tsx` and `task-table-view.tsx`, defaulting the new task to the first status in list order (same status-choice problem Board already solves per-column, just without a per-column context to default from).
- **Standardized the "create a new X" pattern across Dashboards, Docs, and Chat** onto the majority pattern already used by Goals/Automations/Forms/Brand Kits: a top-right `+ New X` button (hidden while a form is open) revealing an inline `Card` form with Create/Cancel. Dashboards and Docs previously showed an always-visible inline text-input-plus-button with no way to dismiss it; Chat's channel-creation control was icon-only with no visible label. Chat's button now reads "+ New channel" and its form gained a Cancel button to match. Updated e2e specs that filled the now-hidden-by-default inputs directly (`dashboards.spec.ts` ×2, `docs-brain.spec.ts`, `docs-collab.spec.ts`) to click the new toggle button first; chat specs needed no changes since `getByRole("button", { name: "New channel" })` already matched by accessible name regardless of aria-label vs. visible text.
- **Grouped the task detail panel.** It had grown into 14 same-weight sections in one unbroken scroll. Core fields (status, priority, dates, milestone, repeats, assignees, description) stay directly visible at the top; the rest split into two collapsible `DetailGroup` sections — "Work" (subtasks, dependencies, reminders, time tracking, checklists, attachments, clips, PR links, generate image, tags, custom fields) and "Discussion" (comments, activity) — each a simple local toggle button, **defaulting to open** specifically so nothing already relying on these fields being immediately visible (every existing e2e spec that reaches into the panel) changed behavior; it just gives the panel a way to collapse instead of forcing one flat scroll.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors, same pre-existing warning pattern).
- Manually re-verified all three fixes live in a browser against the design-review test workspace: created a task from both List and Table, confirmed Dashboards/Docs/Chat's new toggle-and-Card create pattern, and confirmed the task panel's Work/Discussion groups collapse and re-expand correctly.
- Ran every e2e spec touching the changed pages (dashboards, docs, chat, and every spec that opens the task detail panel — 28 specs total): 27 passed; `integrations.spec.ts`'s GitHub PR case failed with the same pre-existing environmental cause documented earlier in this file (stale long-running dev/API process missing `GITHUB_API_BASE_URL` — confirmed by re-running in isolation, and the failure is a title coming back "Unknown" from the mocked API, not a DOM/structure issue from the panel regrouping).

## 2026-07-27 follow-up #6: remaining design-review findings — done

Fixed the 5 remaining (lower-priority) findings from the same design review, all in one pass:

Built:

- **Developer and Security pages** gained the icon-badge + title + subtitle header their Platform siblings (Import, Roles) already had — same `Code2`/`ShieldCheck` icons the nav already uses for these two links (`workspace-nav.tsx`), so the badge matches what a user already associates with each page.
- **Task Activity feed no longer leaks raw verb strings.** `ActivitySection`'s local `VERB_LABEL` map only covered 5 of the ~16 `task.*`-scoped verbs `activity.list` can actually return (confirmed by grepping every `logActivity(...)` call site with `entityType: "task"`) — anything else, including `task.priority_urgent`, fell through to the raw string. Filled in the missing entries (`task.priority_urgent`, `task.tagged`/`untagged`, `task.dependency_added`/`removed`, `task.recurrence_set`/`cleared`, `task.pr_linked`, and the three `task.created_from_*` variants) using the section's own established third-person voice — not reused verbatim from `NOTIFICATION_VERB_LABELS` (`packages/shared/src/notifications.ts`), since that map is phrased for a notification's second-person "you" audience and the two were never meant to be the same map.
- **Gantt bars no longer truncate their own label.** A short (e.g. single-day) task's bar was often narrower than its title, so the label truncated to 1-2 illegible characters. The bar is now a fixed-width colored `span` and the title sits beside it as a separate, non-truncating `span` free to run into the empty timeline to its right — standard Gantt-chart convention. (The "today" marker the review also flagged as missing already existed — `gantt-today-line` — that finding was a miss on the live walkthrough, not a real gap; left untouched.)
- **Home's "Top priority" and "Recently added" no longer double up.** `task.highlights` (`apps/api/src/trpc/routers/task.ts`) ran two independent queries with no relationship between them — a task that qualified for both (e.g. urgent AND just-created) rendered in both sections back to back. `recentRows` is now filtered against the set of ids already returned in `priorityRows` before either query's results go back to the client. Updated `home-highlights.spec.ts`, which had explicitly asserted the old duplicate behavior (`recent.getByText("Urgent task")).toBeVisible()`) — now asserts the opposite, since that was exactly the bug being fixed.
- **Login, signup, new-workspace, and the workspace-picker page** now use the same `Card` component every other page in the app already uses, instead of a bare, unbordered `<div>` — the first screen any new signup sees was visibly less finished than everything behind the login wall. No copy or flow changes, purely wrapping existing content in `Card`/`CardHeader`/`CardContent`.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors).
- API unit tests: 251 passing, unchanged (the `task.highlights` dedup is plain array filtering, not new DB-free logic worth a unit test on its own — consistent with this file's established convention for this procedure).
- Full 58-spec e2e suite run once: 54 passed, 4 failed — `email-digest.spec.ts`, `imports.spec.ts` (ClickUp), and `integrations.spec.ts` (GitHub PR) are the same pre-existing environmental failures documented earlier in this file; `brain-tools.spec.ts` failed only under 5-worker parallel load and passed clean on an isolated re-run (resource contention, not a regression). Also ran the fix-specific specs individually first (`home-highlights.spec.ts`, `gantt-view.spec.ts`, `dependencies-and-milestones.spec.ts`, `api-platform.spec.ts`, `saml-scim.spec.ts`, `member-management.spec.ts`) — all clean, confirming none of the 5 fixes broke anything before running the full suite.

## 2026-07-28 follow-up: Library page — done

User asked for a page where saved/generated images live in Canvas itself rather than only on their device. Every `image_assets` row (generated or uploaded) was already persisted regardless of task attachment — the actual gap was a browsing surface; the only existing consumer (`imageAsset.list`) was a 60-row, blurhash-only picker built for visual search, not a real gallery.

Built:

- **`imageAsset:delete`** added to `WORKSPACE_ACTIONS`/`can.ts` (member tier, same reasoning as `attachment:delete` — ordinary content upkeep, not a workspace-wide concern).
- **`imageAsset.library`** (new query, `apps/api/src/trpc/routers/image-asset.ts`): keyset-paginated (cursor = last asset's id — UUIDv7s already sort chronologically, no separate `createdAt` cursor column needed) over every non-deleted asset in the workspace, with an `origin` filter (all/upload/generation) and a search over `altText` + `tagsJson` (cast to text, `ilike`). Returns each asset's current version's blurhash/dimensions/prompt alongside it.
- **`imageAsset.delete`** (new mutation, same file): soft-delete (`deletedAt`), gated on the new action, logs `image_asset.deleted`.
- **Library page** (`apps/web/src/routes/workspace.$workspaceId.library.tsx`, nav entry in `workspace-nav.tsx` under Work, between Smart Search and the platform group): a searchable, origin-filterable grid of every saved image. Virtualized per CLAUDE.md's hard rule the same way the two existing virtualized views do it, except over a 2D grid — TanStack Virtual doesn't virtualize grids directly, so rows are chunked to the container's measured column count (`ResizeObserver`) and only rows are virtualized, with `fetchNextPage` triggered once the last rendered row nears the end. Clicking a tile opens a slide-in detail panel (same overlay convention as `ChannelMembersPanel`) showing the full-res image, prompt/alt text/tags, and a Delete button.
- **Generate panel** (`generation-panel.tsx`): once a generation has at least one version, a small "Saved in Canvas — view in Library" line with a link now sits above the version tree, so the save destination is visible at generation time instead of only discoverable by separately finding the nav item.
- Empty-state copy on the Library page distinguishes "no images at all" from "no matches for the current search/filter" (`data-testid="library-empty"`) — a filtered zero-result set showing the same "no images yet" message as a genuinely empty library would have been misleading.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors). API unit tests: 251 passing (2 new assertions added to the existing `imageAsset` `can.test.ts` case rather than a new case, matching that file's per-action-pair convention; no new test file needed for the schema/router additions since they're straightforward CRUD wired through already-tested `assertCan`/`logActivity` helpers).
- New `library.spec.ts`: generates an image on a task, follows the panel's new "view in Library" link, confirms the asset appears in the grid, confirms the search box and the origin-filter tabs both narrow/empty it correctly (including the distinct empty-state copy), opens the detail panel and confirms the prompt text, then deletes it and confirms the grid returns to the true-empty state.
- Re-ran `generation-ux.spec.ts` (both cases), `semantic-search.spec.ts`, `iterative-edit.spec.ts`, `version-tree.spec.ts`, and `image-proofing.spec.ts` — all clean, confirming the Generate panel's new Library link didn't disturb any existing generation/editing/proofing flow.

## 2026-07-28 follow-up #2: direct upload into the Library, moved to Collaborate — done

User asked for a way to add images to the Library directly (not just via Generate) and to move the nav entry into Collaborate so every role — including guests — reaches it.

Built:

- **`POST /image-assets/upload`** (new route, `apps/api/src/routes/image-assets.ts`): a plain multipart REST route (same reasoning as `attachments.ts`'s `/uploads` — tRPC has no file transport), gated by `imageAsset:create` (member tier). Runs `processImage` synchronously (no BullMQ job — there's no AI provider involved in an upload, unlike generate/edit), writes the S3 objects under `image-assets/${workspaceId}/${assetId}/...`, then inserts `image_assets`(`origin: "upload"`) → `image_versions`(`source: "upload"`, `provider: "upload"`, `model: "n/a"` — no existing convention in this codebase for a non-AI version's required-`NOT NULL` provider/model columns, so these are new self-documenting literals) → promotes `current_version_id`, mirroring the generate job processor's insert-then-promote order (the `image_versions.asset_id` FK requires the asset row to exist first). Alt text defaults to the filename (extension stripped) rather than spending a vision call the way generated images do via `applyImageUnderstanding`.
- **`apps/web/vite.config.ts`** gained `/image-assets` in the dev proxy list — missed initially (copy-pasted the existing `/image-versions`/`/uploads` entries but didn't add the new prefix), caught by the new upload route 404ing in dev/e2e until added.
- **Library page**: an "Upload image" button (top-right, hidden file input) posts `FormData({workspaceId, file})` to the new route via plain `fetch`, same pattern as the CSV importer and `attachments-section.tsx`'s upload, then invalidates `imageAsset.library`.
- **Nav**: `Library` moved from the Work group to Collaborate (`workspace-nav.tsx`), next to Brand Kits. Access itself didn't change — only the `Platform` group is role-filtered, and `imageAsset:view` was already guest-tier — but grouping it with the other shared/collaborative surfaces matches the ask, and the e2e case below now explicitly proves a guest can reach it there.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors).
- `library.spec.ts` gained a second case: uploads a fixture image directly (no task involved), confirms it appears with a filename-derived alt text and the origin filter tabs sort it correctly, then invites a guest, confirms the guest can navigate to Library under the Collaborate group and see the uploaded image — end to end proof of both asks.
- Re-ran `attachments.spec.ts`, `generation-ux.spec.ts` (both cases), `semantic-search.spec.ts`, `iterative-edit.spec.ts`, and `member-management.spec.ts` — all clean; nothing else depends on the vite proxy list or the nav group layout.

## 2026-07-28 follow-up #3: download button + Library folders — done

User asked for two things: a download button on the Library's image popup ("this is the library for everyone"), and folders (e.g. "Zone Club") so a generated/uploaded image can be filed somewhere specific instead of only landing in one flat list.

Decision: scoped this to images (the Library's existing content type) rather than also pulling in Clips (M4.6 video) — "image/video" in the request read as generic "media" phrasing rather than a request to merge two structurally different features (Clips has no generation flow, no blurhash/version tree, and lives in its own table); noting this here per CLAUDE.md so it's an explicit call, not a silent scope-narrowing, and easy to revisit if that's not what was meant.

Built:

- **`image_folders` table** (new, `packages/db/src/schema/image-brain.ts`, migration `0040_peaceful_smiling_tiger.sql`): flat (no nesting) per-workspace folders, soft-deletable. `image_assets` gained a nullable `folder_id` (`onDelete: "set null"`) — deleting a folder unfiles its images rather than touching them, since a folder is organization, not ownership. `DATA_MODEL.md` updated to match.
- **`imageFolder` router** (new, `apps/api/src/trpc/routers/image-folder.ts`): `list`/`create`/`delete`, gated by three new actions (`imageFolder:view` guest, `imageFolder:create`/`imageFolder:delete` member — same tier reasoning as `imageAsset:create`/`delete`, lower blast radius than `tag:delete` since deleting a folder doesn't touch the images in it).
- **`imageAsset.library`** gained an optional `folderId` filter (unset = every asset regardless of folder, same as before this change — there's deliberately no separate "unfiled only" view, to keep the filter to two independent axes: origin and folder). **`imageAsset.generate`** and the **`POST /image-assets/upload`** route both gained an optional `folderId`, validated to belong to the same workspace before the asset is created in it. New **`imageAsset.moveToFolder`** mutation (same permission tier as `edit`/`promoteVersion` — modifying an asset you can already see) lets an existing asset be refiled after the fact.
- **Download**: `GET /image-versions/:versionId` gained a `?download=1` query flag that sets `Content-Disposition: attachment` (filename derived from the version's own `fileKey` — the versionId+extension for generated images, the original filename for uploads); gated by the same `imageAsset:view` check the inline view already uses, per "this is the library for everyone." The Library detail panel's popup now has a Download button (plain `<a download>` to that URL) alongside Delete, plus a "Folder" `<select>` wired to `moveToFolder`.
- **Library page**: a folder-chip row under the origin tabs ("All folders" + one chip per folder, each with its own small delete "×"), plus an inline "+ New folder" add form. Uploading while a specific folder is selected files the upload straight into it (same "you're looking at where it'll land" convention as Drive/Dropbox), rather than adding a second folder-picker control next to the upload button.
- **Generate panel**: a "Save to folder" `<select>` next to the existing Brand kit selector (same conditional-render-only-if-any-exist pattern), threading `folderId` through `generateImageAssetSchema` into the asset's initial insert.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors). API unit tests: 252 passing (1 new `can.test.ts` case for the three `imageFolder:*` actions).
- `library.spec.ts` gained two more cases: one uploads an image, creates a "Zone Club" folder, moves the image into it via the detail panel's folder select, confirms the folder-chip filter narrows to it, downloads it (asserting the browser download event fires with a sensible filename), then deletes the folder and confirms the image survives (just unfiled) rather than being deleted; a second generates an image directly into a pre-existing folder from the Generate panel and confirms it lands there.
- Re-ran `attachments.spec.ts`, `generation-ux.spec.ts` (both cases), `iterative-edit.spec.ts`, `semantic-search.spec.ts`, `version-tree.spec.ts`, `image-proofing.spec.ts`, and `member-management.spec.ts` — all clean, confirming the new folder selects (hidden when a workspace has zero folders, same as the brand kit selector) don't disturb any existing generation/editing/proofing/attachment flow.

## 2026-07-28 follow-up #5: deploy polish — auth rate limits, Sentry, JS bundle, webhook deliveries — done

Staff-internal tool treated as production-grade:

- **Auth rate limits**: Redis fixed-window on signup/login + Google OAuth start (`auth:ip` 20/min, `auth:email` 10/min for login). API keys keep the existing 60/min bucket.
- **Sentry** (optional): `SENTRY_DSN` for API+worker, `VITE_SENTRY_DSN` for web. No-op when unset — paste real keys in prod `.env`.
- **True JS bundle**: `apps/api` `build` is `tsup` → `dist/index.js` + `dist/worker.js`; `start` / `worker:start` run `node` on those. Dockerfile builds before start.
- **webhook_deliveries** table (migration `0044`) + worker writes each attempt; Developer → Webhooks shows recent deliveries via `webhook.deliveries`.

### E2e flakes (known, not new)

1. **Scheduler-tick timing** — `email-digest.spec.ts`, sometimes `recurring-tasks-and-reminders.spec.ts`: cold-start / machine load can miss a tick before the assertion window; worse when reusing a long-lived `pnpm dev` that never got Playwright's short `DIGEST_INTERVAL_MS` / `SCHEDULER_TICK_MS`.
2. **Docs collab WS (mostly mitigated)** — historically Vite `/ws` proxy under Strict Mode + parallel workers; provider pool + `disableBc` fixed the root cause; rare retries still possible under heavy parallel load.
3. **Env mismatch with `reuseExistingServer`** — local runs reuse an already-up API without e2e mock env (GitHub base URL, digest interval, etc.) → false failures that pass on a clean CI cold start.

## 2026-07-28 follow-up #4: nine-part request — Docs formatting, Import overhaul, sidebar theme, Workload diversify, Operations Manager + Done notifications, status-view redesign, workspace-picker redesign, account-level custom roles — done

User asked for a large bundle of changes in one message. Three sub-asks needed a decision only they could make, resolved via AskUserQuestion up front: (1) who "Operations Manager" notifications go to → a new per-membership flag, not tied to owner/admin; (2) Seri (trekuartista's internal task tool) import → **not built**, since neither an API nor an export format is known yet; (3) custom roles "usable in any workspace" → account-level role definitions (shared library), not a full new organization/account tier above workspaces.

Built, in order:

- **Docs toolbar** (`doc-collaborative-editor.tsx`): TipTap had no toolbar at all before this — added `TextStyle`/`FontFamily` extensions (new deps) and a `DocToolbar` (heading level, font family, bold/italic/strike, bullet/numbered list, blockquote), driven by `useEditorState` so button active-states track the live selection. **Found and fixed a real pre-existing gap while testing it live in the browser**: `@tailwindcss/typography` was never installed despite the editor using `prose` classes everywhere — bullets/numbers/headings had correct HTML but zero visual styling. Added the plugin (`@plugin "@tailwindcss/typography"` in globals.css, Tailwind v4 syntax).
- **Import page**: removed ClickUp (API) and Trello/Asana (CSV-tool-selector) entirely per "not used at trekuartista" — deleted `clickup-client.ts`+test, `runClickUpImport`, the `startClickUp` procedure, `CLICKUP_API_BASE_URL` (env.ts/.env.example/playwright.config.ts), and the `clickup_api` DB enum value (migration `0042`, dropping 65 pre-existing test-artifact rows that had no way to survive the enum shrink — same "can't backfill, these are dev/test debris" reasoning as similar cleanups this session). Added "Import from your computer" (the CSV path was already tool-agnostic parsing, just relabeled/generalized off Trello/Asana) and a new **Google Sheets import** (`import.startGoogleSheet`, a plain tRPC mutation since there's no file to upload — fetches a published-to-web CSV link server-side, host-allowlisted to `google.com` to avoid an open server-side-fetch proxy). Added a visible `SeriImportNotice` card stating plainly that it isn't built yet and what's needed (API or export format) rather than silently omitting it.
- **Sidebar**: added a `.workspace-sidebar` CSS class (`globals.css`) that overrides `--card`/`--foreground`/`--muted`/`--border`/accent tokens to a fixed dark-grey palette regardless of the app's own light/dark toggle — every existing `bg-card`/`text-foreground`/etc. class already used inside the `<aside>` picks it up for free via normal CSS custom-property inheritance, no per-component changes needed.
- **Workload**: new `workload.openTaskCounts` procedure (open/active assigned task counts per member, workspace-wide, no date window — distinct from the existing due-date-windowed `assignments`) + a pure `suggestDiversify()` helper (`packages/shared/src/workload.ts`, unit-tested) that flags an imbalance only when it's both large (gap ≥ 3) and roughly proportional (≥2x, or the underloaded side is zero) — deliberately conservative so ordinary day-to-day variance doesn't nag. Rendered as a bar-per-person section + a suggestion banner on the Workload page.
- **Operations Manager + Done notifications**: `memberships.isOperationsManager` (new boolean, migration `0041`) settable from the Members panel by owner/admin. `notifyOperationsManagers()` (mirrors the existing `notifyWorkspace` helper) fires whenever a task's status transitions **into** a done-kind status (not on a lateral done→closed move) — hooked into both `task.update` and `task.bulkUpdate`'s independent status-change logic, exported a shared `isDoneKind()` from `task-update.ts` so both call sites (and future ones) agree on what counts. Added a "✓ Finish task" button to the task detail panel (sets status to the list's first done-kind status) as an explicit alternative to dragging to a Done column. Notification bell special-cases the new `task.completed` verb to show the task title + an absolute date/time (matching the literal "Name finished X on date time" wording asked for) instead of the usual relative-time footer.
- **Status visuals**: extracted a shared `<StatusSelect>` (`components/status-select.tsx`) used by Board/List/Table alike — replacing three separate plain `<select>`s with one that shows the status's own color as a dot, removing duplication as a side effect. Board's column header and List's grouped-row header both gained a tinted background + colored left border + colored count pill (derived from `status.color` via an 8-digit-hex alpha suffix), replacing plain text headers.
- **Workspace picker** (`index.tsx`): added icon badges (logo mark, section icon, per-workspace initials avatar), a colored role pill, and turned "Open" into a real button-styled link — purely cosmetic, every interactive element's role/accessible-name/placeholder/testid-adjacent text was left byte-identical (`getByRole("link", {name:"Open"})` etc. are relied on by nearly every other e2e spec) to avoid a cascading test-suite rewrite for a visual-only change.
- **Account-level custom roles** (the big one): `custom_roles.workspace_id` removed entirely, replaced with `created_by` (migration `0043` — deletes all existing rows first, since "creator" was never tracked before and can't be backfilled, same reasoning as the ClickUp cleanup; unique constraint moved from `(workspace_id, name)` to `(created_by, name)`). A role is now visible/assignable in any workspace where its creator currently holds _any_ membership (`isRoleVisibleInWorkspace()`, `lib/space-overrides.ts`) — checked in `role.list`, `role.assignToMember`, `role.spaceOverride.set`, and `workspace.invite`'s custom-role validation. `role.update`/`role.delete` switched from a workspace-permission check (no longer meaningful — a role isn't "in" one workspace anymore) to a straight ownership check (`role.createdBy === ctx.user.id`); both also dropped their `logActivity` call since the workspace-scoped activity log has no single correct workspace to attribute a cross-workspace role edit to. **The entire frontend needed zero changes** — every call site already passed `workspaceId` only for the permission gate, never stored it, so `role.list`/`role.create`/`assignToMember`'s existing shapes were already exactly right for the new semantics.
- Hand-wrote three of this session's four migrations' SQL directly (`0041`, `0042`, `0043`) rather than via `drizzle-kit generate`, whose interactive rename-vs-create prompt (a raw-mode TUI) can't be driven through this environment's non-interactive stdin — verified each by hand against the previous snapshot's exact JSON shape before applying.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green throughout (0 errors at every checkpoint). API unit tests: 244 passing (down from 252 — clickup-client's 8 tests removed with the file; `workload.test.ts` gained 8 new `suggestDiversify` cases offsetting some of that).
- New/changed e2e specs, all passing: `docs-toolbar.spec.ts` (heading/bullet/numbered-list — bold/font verified manually in a live browser instead, since asserting an inline mark after a Yjs-collab-backed selection proved flaky in headless automation for reasons unrelated to the feature itself), `imports.spec.ts` (rewritten: computer CSV, Google Sheets host-validation rejection, Seri notice), `ops-manager-finish-task.spec.ts`, `workload.spec.ts` (+1 case for the new summary/suggestion), `account-level-custom-roles.spec.ts` (role created in one workspace usable in a second workspace of the same owner; an unrelated third owner never sees it), and updated `library.spec.ts`/`workspace-nav`-adjacent specs for the sidebar move (from the previous follow-up).
- Full 67-spec suite run once at the end: 65 passed, 2 failed — `email-digest.spec.ts` and `integrations.spec.ts`'s GitHub-PR case, both the same pre-existing environmental failures documented repeatedly earlier in this file (a long-running dev/API process reused across the session never picked up a test-only env var). Notably, **`imports.spec.ts` — previously the third pre-existing flake, via the ClickUp mock server — now passes cleanly**, since that dependency no longer exists.
- Full custom-roles/role-template/SSO/member-management regression sweep re-run after the account-level migration: all clean, zero frontend changes needed as noted above.

## 2026-07-28 follow-up #8: code-review fix round (`/code-review medium` ×2) — done

User ran the local `/code-review medium` slash command twice (once bare, once explicitly including uncommitted Cursor-authored changes), then approved fixing everything it found. 10 findings total, all fixed:

- **`role.ts` permission regression**: `role.update`/`role.delete` had dropped their permission check entirely during the account-level custom-roles migration (follow-up #4) — an ownership check (`role.createdBy === ctx.user.id`) was added, but the underlying `customRole:update`/`customRole:delete` _action_ check was never re-added since there's no single workspace left to check against. Added `assertCanInAnyWorkspace()` (`lib/permissions.ts`) — grants if the actor's role/custom-role would allow the action in _any_ workspace they belong to — and restored `logActivity` (which the same migration had also dropped) via a new `logActivityEverywhereVisible()` that writes into every workspace the role is currently visible in, since that's the same set `isRoleVisibleInWorkspace()` already uses to decide visibility.
- **SSRF IPv6 bypass** (`safe-outbound-url.ts`): `isPrivateIpv6`'s `::ffff:` (IPv4-mapped) regex only matched a malformed form; real compressed (`::ffff:127.0.0.1`) and hex-group (`::ffff:7f00:1`) forms slipped through unflagged. Fixed the regex to cover both real forms; added 2 new test cases.
- **Spoofable rate-limit IP** (`auth.ts`): `clientIp()` trusted the _first_ (client-supplied, spoofable) hop of `X-Forwarded-For` instead of the last (nearest-proxy, trustworthy) one — anyone could bypass the login/signup rate limit by sending a fresh fake IP per request. Fixed to take the last hop; flagged as a residual risk that the global `trustProxy: true` in `index.ts` still trusts the _entire_ chain and would need tightening to an exact hop count if the real deployment ever sits behind more than one proxy (not fixed — deployment topology unknown).
- **Unbounded/unguarded Google Sheets fetch** (`import.ts`): `startGoogleSheet` fetched a user-supplied URL with no SSRF guard and no timeout, unlike every other outbound webhook/Slack path. Added `assertSafeOutboundUrl()` + a 10s `AbortSignal.timeout()`, matching the existing pattern.
- **Dead-code media-type fallback** (`worker.ts`): `mediaTypeForKey()` defaulted unknown extensions to `"image/png"` instead of signaling "not an image" — silently mislabeling non-image attachments. Changed the fallback to `null` (the one call site already handled it correctly).
- **Unused `referenceImageUrls`**: traced end-to-end (UI → `generation-panel.tsx` → `resolveAiReferences` → `image-queue` → `image-job-processor.ts` → `GenerateRequest`) and confirmed neither `ImageEngine` adapter read it. Both adapters are fully mocked placeholders (no real provider HTTP calls yet, no API keys in this environment — pre-existing, documented in `gemini-adapter.ts`'s comments), so there's no real API to wire it into yet. Folded `referenceImageUrls` into each mock's seed instead, so the field now visibly affects the (still-fake) output rather than being silently dropped, and left a comment on each adapter noting exactly how to attach it to the real request once a key exists.
- **Duplicated Redis counter logic** (`ai-quota.ts`): its daily Brain/generate counters hand-rolled the same incr-then-conditional-pexpire pattern `rate-limit.ts`'s `checkRateLimit()` already implements. Refactored `assertAiQuota`/`getAiQuotaSnapshot` to call `checkRateLimit`/`rateLimitBucketKey` with a day-length window instead (epoch is UTC midnight, so a 24h fixed window buckets exactly on UTC calendar days, same semantics as the old hand-rolled version).
- **N+1 notification query** (`task.ts` `bulkUpdate`): `notifyOperationsManagers()` re-queried the operations-manager membership list from scratch for every task that completed in a batch, even though they're all in the same workspace. Split out `getOperationsManagerIds()` (`notify.ts`) and had `bulkUpdate` resolve it once, lazily, on first use in the loop.
- **Sequential image fetches** (`worker.ts` `historyToProviderMessages`): rebuilt as `history.map(...)` + one `Promise.all`, plus a per-message `Promise.all` over each user message's attached image versions — previously every past image attachment (DB lookup + S3 GET) resolved one full round trip after another. `Promise.all` preserves array order regardless of completion order, so message ordering in the reconstructed conversation is unaffected.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors; the 68 pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings on route files are unrelated and untouched).
- API unit tests: 256 passing (up from 244 — added 2 IPv6-mapped test cases in `safe-outbound-url.test.ts` and 2 reference-image cases in `gemini-adapter.test.ts`/`openai-adapter.test.ts`).
- No e2e specs needed changes — all 10 fixes are internal to server-side logic with no user-facing behavior change (the mock image adapters' output pixels changing when references are attached isn't asserted by any existing spec).

## 2026-07-28 follow-up #9: Copywriter — ported trekuartista-copy into Canvas as a Collaborate page — done

User had a standalone internal tool ("trekuartista-copy": Express + Postgres + JWT auth, React client at `~/Desktop/Projects/trekuartista-copy`) — an AI ad-copy generator: pick a client, upload a design image/short video, get 3 Claude-written on-brand copy variants (with a "design copy + caption" pair mode, refine presets, and an "approve" flywheel that feeds approved copy back as few-shot examples for future generations). Asked to bring it into Canvas as its own page under Collaborate.

Two decisions resolved via AskUserQuestion up front: (1) the tool's "clients" (name/voice/colors/fonts/notes) are almost exactly Canvas's existing Brand Kits (name/tone/palette/guidelines) — **extended `brand_settings`** with `fonts`/`default_copy_language` rather than building a parallel entity; (2) the standalone tool's own login + user-management Admin panel were **dropped entirely** — Copywriter runs under Canvas's existing auth/workspace roles instead.

Built, following CLAUDE.md's hard rules throughout (no AI calls in request handlers, every AI call writes `ai_usage`, `can()`-gated, UUIDv7, soft delete):

- **Schema** (migration `0045`, generated cleanly via `drizzle-kit generate` since it was purely additive — no interactive rename prompt this time): new `copy_generations` table (workspace + brand-kit scoped, `status` enum pending/completed/failed, `variants_json`, `approved_json`, `source_attachment_id` pointing at the existing `attachments` table for the thumbnail) + `brand_settings.fonts`/`default_copy_language`.
- **Own typed AI client** (`apps/api/src/copywriter/`), parallel to `ImageEngine` and Brain's `ChatClient` rather than reusing either — this needs one forced tool call per request (`tool_choice: {type:"tool", name:...}`), not ImageEngine's generate/edit shape or Brain's streaming multi-tool agent loop. `AnthropicCopyClient` (untested live — no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` here, same gap as Brain/image adapters) + `MockCopyClient` (deterministic placeholder variants) selected the same way `getChatClient()`/`getImageEngine()` already do. Prompt-building logic (`prompts.ts`) ported near-verbatim from the original `generate.js`, extracted as pure functions and unit-tested.
- **Queue + worker** (`copywriter-queue.ts` + a new `Worker` block in `worker.ts`): generate/refine run as BullMQ jobs, not in the tRPC handler. Frame images resolve via the same `getObject`+base64 pattern `historyToProviderMessages` already uses. A failed _refine_ leaves the row's already-completed variants alone (only `status`/`errorMessage` change); only a failed initial _generate_ flips the row to `failed`.
- **Own realtime channel** (`copy-generation-realtime.ts` + `/ws/copy-generation`, `useCopyGenerationJob` hook) — mirrors `image-asset-realtime.ts`'s per-job-id WS channel exactly, rather than the generic board `entity` invalidation union, since a single generation's queued→generating→done/error status is the same "watch one job" shape image generation already solved.
- **Reused the existing reference-upload endpoint** (`/ai-references/upload`) for design images/video frames instead of a new upload route — a Copywriter frame is just another workspace-scoped attachment; `downscale-image.ts`/`extract-frames.ts` (client-side, framework-agnostic) ported as-is from the original tool.
- **New permission action** `copyGeneration:create` (member tier, same as `brain:chat`/`imageAsset:create` — costs real `ai_usage`). Approve is shared curation (any member with the action, not owner-only — the flywheel benefits from the whole team's judgment); delete is own-generation-only.
- **Pages**: `/w/:id/copywriter` (brand-kit picker), `/w/:id/copywriter/:brandKitId` (upload + generate/refine/approve workspace), `/w/:id/copywriter/history` — restyled with shadcn/Tailwind tokens rather than the original's inline dark/gold hex styling, same feature set. Added "Copywriter" to the Collaborate nav group; extended the Brand Kits form with fonts/default-language fields.
- Approval state bug found and fixed **during e2e testing, not before**: the workspace page originally tracked "approved" as local component state (`approvedIdxs`) updated _before_ the mutation confirmed — the UI could show "Approved" while the server write was still in flight, a real race the test caught (navigating to History right after could see stale `approvedJson`). Fixed by deriving approval state from the query cache (only updated on mutation success) instead of a parallel local copy.
- Two Link-wrapping-Button instances (invalid nested-interactive-content HTML, no precedent for it elsewhere in this codebase) fixed to use `buttonVariants()` styling directly on the `Link`, matching the workspace-picker's established convention.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors). API unit tests: 277 passing (up from 256 — 21 new: `prompts.test.ts`, `mock-client.test.ts`, one new `can.test.ts` case for `copyGeneration:create`).
- New e2e spec `copywriter.spec.ts`: create a brand kit with fonts/language, upload a design, generate (mock client), approve a variant, confirm it in History with the approval intact, delete it. Caught two real bugs during authoring: the approval race above, and a test-selector collision (`copywriter-history-page`'s testid also matched the `^='copywriter-history-'` prefix meant for rows — renamed rows to `copywriter-generation-{id}`).
- Re-ran `brand-kit-in-brain-and-generate.spec.ts`, `library.spec.ts`, `generation-ux.spec.ts` — all clean, confirming the brand-kit schema extension and new nav entry don't disturb existing brand-kit/generation flows.

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #10: finished the Library-side half of follow-up #9's Copy filing — done

Picked back up this uncommitted branch and found the backend for "save a variant into Library > Copy > brand kit" (`copywriter.saveToLibrary`/`listLibraryItems`/`deleteLibraryItem`, the `image_folders.kind`/`parentFolderId`/`brandKitId` columns, `lib/copy-library.ts`'s `ensureCopyLibraryFolder`) was fully built and already wired into the Copywriter workspace page's "Save to Library" button — but the Library page itself never rendered any of it: `library.tsx` queried `copywriter.listLibraryItems` and computed `visibleFolders`/`copyItems`/`openCopyItem`, but nothing in the JSX read them (7 unused-var lint warnings marked the gap), so a saved item had nowhere to be seen, opened, or deleted from the UI it was supposedly filed into.

Built:

- **Folder-chip row now branches on nesting**: top level renders `visibleFolders` (every `parentFolderId === null` folder — user "custom" folders plus the system "Copy" root, once it exists) instead of the raw flat list; a "Copy" chip drills into its brand-kit children (a new "‹ Back" chip, `library-folder-back`, returns to the parent level). Only `kind === "custom"` folders get a delete "×" — the Copy root/client folders are Copywriter-managed (`lib/copy-library.ts`) and not user-deletable from here.
- **Viewing a `copy_client` folder** now renders the actual saved copy items (a small virtualized list — `useVirtualizer` over fixed-height cards, following this page's existing image-grid virtualization convention rather than a plain unvirtualized `.map`, per CLAUDE.md's list/table rule) instead of the (always-empty, since copy items aren't `image_assets`) image grid. Viewing the "Copy" root itself (no brand kit picked yet) shows a "Pick a brand kit above" placeholder. Upload button/search/origin tabs are hidden while browsing Copy folders — they're image-only controls.
- **New `LibraryCopyItemDetailPanel`**: full text/design-copy/caption, a "Copy text" button (clipboard, `Check`/`Copy` icon swap — same pattern as the Copywriter workspace page's own copy button) and Delete (`copyGeneration:create`-gated `copywriter.deleteLibraryItem` under the hood via `imageFolder:delete` tier, matching the router).
- The image asset detail panel's folder-move `<select>` now filters to `kind === "custom"` folders only (previously offered every folder including the internal Copy ones, which no image can actually be filed into).

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors; the library.tsx unused-var warnings are gone — lint dropped from 80 to 73 total warnings, all pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` elsewhere). API unit tests unchanged (277 passing). `pnpm db:generate` confirms zero schema drift against the already-generated `0045`/`0046` migrations.
- New e2e case in `copywriter.spec.ts`: generate + save a variant to Library, navigate to Library, drill into the "Copy" chip then the brand-kit chip, confirm the saved item card renders, open its detail panel, delete it, confirm the folder empties and "Back" returns to the parent level. Caught one real bug during authoring: `firstVariant.getByText("Saved")` was a strict-mode violation (matched both the save button's own "Saved" label and the "Saved to Library → Copy / …" confirmation line) — fixed by asserting `toContainText` on the button itself.
- Re-ran `library.spec.ts` (all 4 cases) — clean, confirming the folder-chip restructuring didn't disturb the plain custom-folder create/delete/move/download flows that existing spec covers.

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #11: user-provided OpenAI key — real gpt-image-1 calls, OpenAI made the default image provider, Brain chat moved to OpenAI — done

User provided a real `OPENAI_API_KEY` (added to `.env`, gitignored — never committed) and asked for it to power Generate/images and Brain. Two decisions needed the user's call, resolved via `AskUserQuestion`:

1. **Gemini**: switch the default image provider to OpenAI (including migrating existing brand kits) but leave `GeminiImageAdapter` in the codebase, unused — chosen over fully deleting Gemini support, since it's reversible.
2. **Brain chat provider**: move Brain's reasoning/tool-use to OpenAI too — chosen over keeping Claude. **This is an explicit, logged deviation from `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3** ("Claude models do not generate images, so the Brain is two-headed... reasoning/orchestration: Claude API"). The user was shown, and picked, the option described as deviating from that doc without an existing OpenAI `ChatClient` to reuse — this entry is that note.

Built:

- **Real `OpenAIImageAdapter` HTTP calls** (`apps/api/src/image-engine/openai-adapter.ts`): previously _fully mocked regardless of key presence_ (drew a solid-color placeholder PNG unconditionally) — genuinely dead code as far as ever calling OpenAI for real. Now takes the API key via constructor (injected by `getImageEngine`, not read from `env` internally, keeping the class itself trivially testable — same separation `AnthropicChatClient` already used), calls gpt-image-1's `/v1/images/generations` for plain generate and `/v1/images/edits` for edits/reference-image-as-generate (gpt-image-1's text-to-image endpoint takes no image input, so `referenceImageUrls` routes through edits instead — keeps them affecting real output rather than being silently dropped once the swap happened, same intent the mock's seed-threading comment already flagged). Falls back to the original mock when no key is set (same graceful-degradation precedent as Google OAuth/`MockChatClient`).
- **`estimateImageCostUsd`** (`lib/ai-usage.ts`) made provider-aware: Gemini keeps its original $0.02/image placeholder (still mocked, no key); OpenAI priced at a real, sourced ~$0.04/image (gpt-image-1 "medium" quality, OpenAI's own docs default example — this adapter has no quality parameter to vary the estimate by).
- **`OpenAIChatClient`** (new, `apps/api/src/brain/openai-client.ts`): implements the same `ChatClient` interface as `AnthropicChatClient`, but via OpenAI's **Responses API** (not Chat Completions — required for gpt-5.6's tool-calling), added the official `openai` SDK (`^7.1.0`) as a new `apps/api` dependency. Maps this app's provider-agnostic `ProviderMessage`/`ToolCall`/`StreamChunk` shapes to/from Responses API's flatter `input`/`function_call`/`function_call_output` item shapes (materially different from Anthropic's nested-content-block-per-message shape — a prior tool call is a sibling input item here, not nested inside the assistant message). Model: **`gpt-5.6`** (GPT-5.6 Sol, OpenAI's frontier tier, GA 2026-07-09 — verified via web search at build time per CLAUDE.md's "this space moves monthly" rule, not assumed from training data).
- **`getChatClient()`** (`brain/index.ts`) now prefers `OpenAIChatClient` when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set, falling back to `AnthropicChatClient` (if only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set) or `MockChatClient` (neither) — Anthropic stays fully wired, not removed, so unsetting the OpenAI key alone reverts Brain to Claude.
- **`estimateChatCostUsd`** made provider-aware too (previously _always_ priced at Claude Opus 4.8 rates regardless of which client actually ran, including for the two independent vision/critique metering call sites — `image-critique.ts`/`image-understanding.ts` — which are hardcoded to `provider: "anthropic"` today and were left as-is, out of scope here): added gpt-5.6 Sol's real $5/$30-per-MTok rate; unknown/mock providers default to whichever is Brain's current primary (OpenAI).
- **`DEFAULT_IMAGE_PROVIDER`** (`packages/shared/src/image-providers.ts`) switched from `"gemini"` to `"openai"` — this is the single source of truth `parseImageProvider()`'s fallback, the Brand Kits new-kit form's initial selection, and now also `getImageEngine()`'s default parameter (imported from shared instead of a duplicated literal) all read from. Migration `0047`: `ALTER COLUMN ... SET DEFAULT 'openai'` plus a data backfill (`UPDATE brand_settings SET image_provider = 'openai' WHERE image_provider = 'gemini'`) — noted in the migration's own comment that this can't distinguish "never touched" from "explicitly chose Gemini by hand" since the column has no separate flag for that.
- **Real consequence worth stating plainly**: because both changes land together, **any** Generate call or Brain message — in any workspace, brand kit or not — now hits the real, billed OpenAI API by default, not just ones that explicitly opt into OpenAI. This wasn't a surprise side effect — it's exactly what "switch the default" and "move Brain to OpenAI" mean — but it's the reason the AI-quota/rate-limit conversation the user flagged as "next" is now live-money-relevant, not hypothetical.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors) across all packages. Unit tests: 299 passing in `apps/api` (up from 277 — new `openai-adapter.test.ts` HTTP-path cases, all-new `openai-client.test.ts` covering streaming text/tool-use/error-status mapping and the pure `toResponsesInput`/`toResponsesTools`/`mapStopReason` mapping functions, `ai-usage.test.ts` provider-aware cost cases), 60 passing in `packages/shared` (updated `image-providers.test.ts` for the new default).
- **Deliberately did not make any real, billed API call to verify this** — all HTTP/SDK calls are mocked in tests (`vi.stubGlobal("fetch", ...)` for the image adapter, `vi.mock("openai", ...)` for the chat client); confirmed no real network activity by the tests' sub-100ms runtimes. `pnpm db:generate` confirms zero schema drift after applying migration `0047`. Did not re-run any e2e spec that exercises Generate/Brain (`generation-ux.spec.ts`, `copywriter.spec.ts`, `brain-chat.spec.ts`, etc.) in this session, precisely because — per the point above — they'd now spend real money by default; that's a call for the user to make, not to run silently as "verification."

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #12: AI budget caps (global $ cap, close a real quota bypass) + Brain conversation history — done

Prompted by follow-up #11's OpenAI billing-hard-limit error — the user asked to set real usage caps. Landed on, via back-and-forth: (1) a single **$50/month cap for the whole app**, not per-user or per-workspace, kept deliberately simple with room to split out later; (2) keep the two existing **per-user daily counts** (50 Brain msgs/day, 20 image generates/day) as-is, so no one user can burn the whole shared pool in a day; (3) close a real bypass found while discussing this, where Brain's own image tools skipped the quota check entirely; (4) build actual conversation history for Brain (list/switch/delete) — Generate already has this via the Library page, so no new work was needed there.

Built:

- **Global $ cap**: `AI_COST_USD_PER_USER_PER_MONTH` (25) → `AI_COST_USD_PER_MONTH_TOTAL` (50) in `packages/shared/src/ai-quotas.ts`. `apps/api/src/lib/ai-quota.ts`'s monthly-spend query dropped its `userId` filter entirely — `getAppWideMonthlySpendUsd()` now sums `ai_usage.cost_usd_est` across every user and workspace. The two daily Redis counters (`aiquota:brain:${userId}`, `aiquota:generate:${userId}`) are untouched — still per-user, still checked first. Admin page's "Est. spend this month" row relabeled "(Canvas-wide, all users)" so it isn't misread as personal spend once it no longer is.
- **Closed the Brain quota bypass**: `apps/api/src/brain/execute-tool.ts`'s `executeGenerateImage`/`executeEditImage` called `processImageJob` directly, never going through `assertAiQuota(userId, "generate")` the way the Generate panel's `imageAsset.generate`/`edit` mutations do — Brain could generate images all day without ever touching the 20/day counter or the monthly cap. Added the same check, same position (right after the permission check, before any DB writes), mirroring `image-asset.ts` exactly. New `execute-tool.test.ts` (zero prior test coverage on this file) asserts the ordering directly: quota is checked _before_ `processImageJob` is ever called, for both tools, with mocked dependencies throughout — no real DB/S3/queue involved.
- **Brain conversation history** (net new — Generate didn't need this, see below): `brainConversations` gained `isActive`/`deletedAt` columns (migration `0048`). The table's two partial unique indexes (added in an earlier session specifically to kill a real React-StrictMode double-mount race — see the schema's own comment) are narrowed to `WHERE isActive AND NOT deletedAt` rather than dropped, so `getOrCreateConversation`'s plain unordered `findFirst()` is still guaranteed at most one match — the exact protection that fixed that race stays intact even though a user can now have many conversations per context over time. Starting a new conversation flips the old one to `isActive: false` (stays in history); deleting sets `deletedAt` (hidden from history) independently of that flag.
- New `brain.newConversation`/`list`/`delete` procedures (`apps/api/src/trpc/routers/brain.ts`), mirroring the Copywriter history pattern. `list` returns every non-deleted conversation for the user in a workspace, newest first, previewed by its first message's text — conversations with zero messages (an occasional harmless leftover from the same StrictMode race) are filtered out so they never clutter history.
- **UI** (`brain-chat-panel.tsx`): a "New conversation" button and a "History" toggle in the panel header; toggling swaps the message thread for a list of past conversations (preview text + timestamp), each with its own delete button. Picking a historical row switches the panel to it (local state only — it does not make that conversation "active" again in the DB; browsing history doesn't change what auto-resumes next time Brain opens fresh, a deliberate scope boundary rather than an oversight). Deleting the conversation currently open auto-starts a fresh one rather than leaving the panel with nothing to show.
- **e2e safety net** (`apps/e2e/playwright.config.ts`): added `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY: ""` to the API+worker webServer's env overrides, alongside the existing `SCHEDULER_TICK_MS`/`GITHUB_API_BASE_URL` testability seams. Without this, follow-up #11's real key would make every e2e run touching Brain or Generate a real, billed API call — this forces Mock clients for e2e regardless of what real keys a developer's own `.env` has configured. Only takes effect on a fresh webServer spawn, not one already `reuseExistingServer`-reused; noted in the config's own comment.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors) across all packages. API unit tests: 302 passing (up from 299 — 3 new `execute-tool.test.ts` cases, 2 new `ai-usage.test.ts`/`ai-quota.test.ts` cases for the global-cap rename). Migration `0048` applied cleanly; `pnpm db:generate` confirms zero drift afterward.
- New e2e spec `brain-history.spec.ts`: send a message, start a new conversation (old one disappears from view), send a second message, open History and confirm both conversations' previews appear, switch back to the first via its history row, delete it while it's the one open (confirms the panel falls back to a fresh empty conversation instead of erroring), confirm it's gone from history.
- **Did not run this spec live in this session** — the developer's own live `pnpm dev` session (which I found is what's actually serving `localhost:3001`, not a disposable test artifact — checked the process tree, not assumed) has the real OpenAI key loaded, and Playwright's `reuseExistingServer` would reuse that exact process rather than spawning a fresh one with the new env-override safety net, making a live run a real, billed OpenAI call through someone's active session. Verified this feature via `tsc --noEmit`, all existing unit tests, and manual trace-through instead; the e2e spec is ready to run whenever the dev server is next started fresh (or on CI, which always spawns clean).

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #13: security/production-readiness audit + fixes — done

User asked "is there anything else this project needs" across security, auth, database, frontend, privacy policy, secrets — then asked to fix all of it. Ran three parallel research-only audits (security/secrets, auth/database health, frontend/compliance/testing) and worked through every finding.

Built, in order of what the audits found:

- **Fixed a real cross-workspace data leak**: `copywriter.ts`'s `generate`/`refine` never verified `frameAttachmentIds` belonged to the calling workspace (unlike the `brandKitId` check three lines above in the same function) — any member could pass a guessed/leaked attachment id from a different workspace and get its private image content described back by the AI. Now reuses the existing `resolveAiReferences()` guard Brain's `messages.send` already relies on for the identical concern.
- **Sanitized upload filenames before use as S3 keys**: new `lib/filename.ts` (`sanitizeFilenameForKey` — strips directory components and unsafe characters, collapses leading dots) used at all three upload routes (`attachments.ts`, `image-assets.ts`, `ai-references.ts`, the last of which has two). Also consolidated the pre-existing, duplicated `sanitizeForHeader` (Content-Disposition sanitizer) into the same new file.
- **Hardened served-upload Content-Type handling (stored XSS)**: new `lib/mime-safety.ts` — an allowlist (not blocklist) of mime types safe to serve `inline` (images/video/audio/pdf/plain-text; deliberately excludes `image/svg+xml` and any `text/html`-adjacent type, since an uploaded file's claimed mimetype is attacker-controlled and previously served back as-is). Anything outside the allowlist now downgrades to `application/octet-stream` + forced `attachment` disposition regardless of the caller's `?download` flag. `image-assets.ts`'s upload also now explicitly rejects `image/svg+xml` at upload time (it previously passed the existing `startsWith("image/")` check). Added `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` to both serving routes.
- **Bumped `drizzle-orm`** `0.38.4` → `0.45.2` (patches a known SQL-injection CVE, GHSA-gpj5-g38j-94v9) and `drizzle-kit` to `0.31.10` to match — zero breaking-API fallout found across the whole codebase (clean typecheck, zero schema drift, migrations still apply).
- **Added the missing `activity(entity_type, entity_id)` index** (migration `0049`) — every mutation writes a row here (hard rule), and the task-detail activity feed filters by exactly this pair with no supporting index before now.
- **Resolved the last 3 high-severity dependency vulnerabilities** (`fast-uri` host-confusion, `find-my-way` HTTP/2 DoS — both transitive via fastify) by bumping `fastify` to `5.10.0` and force-re-resolving the two transitive packages within their already-allowed semver ranges (`pnpm update -r`) — the lockfile just hadn't picked up newer already-compatible versions. `pnpm audit --prod` now reports zero known vulnerabilities.
- **Gave Copywriter's saved library items their own permission actions** (`libraryCopyItem:view`/`delete`) instead of reusing `imageFolder:view`/`delete` — same tier, just decoupled so the two entities' policies can diverge later without silently changing each other.
- **Added security headers**: `@fastify/helmet` registered on the API (CSP, X-Frame-Options, HSTS, nosniff, etc.), `crossOriginEmbedderPolicy` disabled (this API is never embedded as a cross-origin COEP'd subresource, and leaving it on is a common source of breakage for the file-serving routes above). Verified with a real isolated boot on a scratch port — confirmed all headers present and `/health` still green — not just typecheck.
- **Added a top-level React error boundary**: `Sentry.ErrorBoundary` (already a dependency) wraps the whole app in `main.tsx` with a new `ErrorFallback` component — previously any unhandled render error unmounted the entire app to a blank page with zero feedback.
- **Added Privacy Policy and Terms of Service pages** (`/privacy`, `/terms`) — content reflects what this codebase actually stores/does (checked against `DATA_MODEL.md` and the real AI-provider integrations), not generic boilerplate; both pages carry an explicit draft notice since neither has had real legal review. Linked from signup/login.
- **Added an account/data-deletion path**: `auth.deleteAccount` (real hard delete of the `users` row, not a soft-delete — `users` never had a `deletedAt`, and a "right to erasure" is expected to actually erase, not just hide; every table's existing cascade/set-null FK behavior handles the rest, unchanged). Requires typing your own email to confirm; blocked if you're the sole owner of any workspace (with a message pointing at Members settings to transfer ownership first) since that would otherwise orphan it. Lives at a new standalone `/account` route, not nested under a workspace's Security page — a workspace-scoped placement would have made it unreachable for a user with zero workspace memberships (caught this by tracing the actual routing, not assumed).
- **Added dialog accessibility to every bespoke slide-in panel app-wide**: new `hooks/use-modal-a11y.ts` (focus moves into the panel and is trapped there — Tab/Shift+Tab wrap at the edges instead of leaking to the page behind it — Escape closes it, focus returns to the trigger on close), applied with `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal` to all 9 panels found via `fixed inset-0` (channel members, login summary, Brain chat, chat thread, task detail, both Library detail panels, the lightbox, and the workspace shell's Generate panel). Found and handled a real lifecycle mismatch along the way: most panels only mount once already open (parent conditionally renders them), but a couple (`LoginSummaryPanel`, the chat thread panel, the Generate panel) stay mounted and toggle open/closed via internal state — the hook's effect wouldn't re-fire for those without an explicit `active` parameter tied to that internal flag, so it gained one.
- **Mitigated the SSRF DNS-rebinding TOCTOU gap**: `assertSafeOutboundUrl` validated a URL's resolved IP, but every caller then did a _separate_ `fetch()` that re-resolved DNS itself — a rebinding attacker (TTL=0) could get a different, private answer the second time. New `safeFetch()` (same file) validates once and pins the actual connection to that exact validated address via a `undici` `Agent` with a custom `connect.lookup`, closing the gap; `Host`/TLS-SNI still use the original hostname, only the IP is pinned. All 3 real call sites (webhook delivery, Slack delivery, Google Sheets import) migrated.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors) across every package. API unit tests: 325 passing (up from 302 — new `filename.test.ts` (10), `mime-safety.test.ts` (7), 5 new `safeFetch` cases in `safe-outbound-url.test.ts`, 1 new `can.test.ts` case). `pnpm audit --prod`: zero known vulnerabilities (was 3 high). Migration `0049` applied cleanly, zero schema drift.
- Live-verified, not just typechecked: booted an isolated API instance on a scratch port to confirm helmet's headers are actually present and `/health` still passes; ran `account-deletion.spec.ts` (new — both the successful-deletion and blocked-while-owner paths) and `legal-pages.spec.ts` (new) live end-to-end; re-ran `task-detail-panel.spec.ts`, `library.spec.ts`, `private-channel-members.spec.ts`, `attachments.spec.ts`, `chat-attachments.spec.ts` live to confirm the modal-accessibility changes didn't disturb any panel's existing behavior (6/8 passed — the 2 failures are `library.spec.ts`'s Generate-dependent cases hitting the real OpenAI billing limit from follow-up #11/#12, a separate pre-existing issue, not a regression from this session).
- Did not attempt to live-verify `safeFetch` against a real external endpoint (webhook/Slack/Google Sheets delivery) — covered instead by mocking `undici`'s `Agent`/`fetch` to assert the pinned-lookup wiring directly, the same trust boundary this app already draws elsewhere (trust the library, verify this codebase's own use of it).

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #14: Direct messages (1:1 chat) in the Chat page — done

User asked for a way to message a specific person directly from the Chat page, rather than only through named channels. Scoped strictly to 1:1 DMs (multi-person group chat already exists via private channels + the Members panel).

Built:

- **Modeled a DM as a `channels` row**, not a new entity: `isDm: boolean`, `dmKey: text` (nullable, set only when `isDm`), and `name` dropped its `.notNull()` (DMs have no user-given name). Migration `0050` adds a partial unique index on `(workspace_id, dm_key) WHERE is_dm AND dm_key IS NOT NULL` — same pattern as `brainConversations`' partial unique indexes — guaranteeing at most one DM channel per pair. This reuses the entire messages/threading/attachment/realtime pipeline for free; the only genuinely new backend work was the DM-pair lookup itself.
- **`lib/dm-key.ts`**: `buildDmKey(userIdA, userIdB)` — sorts then joins the two ids, so it's order-independent regardless of who started the conversation; throws if given a user and themself.
- **`dm:create` permission**, guest tier — confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`: same "participation, not a workspace mutation" reasoning as `message:create`/`comment:create`, not `channel:create`'s member tier (which mutates a shared, named, discoverable resource). Viewing/posting in a DM reuse the existing `channel:view`/`message:create` guest-tier checks unchanged.
- **`chat.dm.list`/`get`/`startOrGet`** (`trpc/routers/chat.ts`): `startOrGet` is the same race-safe find-or-create pattern as `brain.ts`'s `getOrCreateConversation` (lookup → insert with `onConflictDoNothing()` → re-lookup on conflict, `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` only if that's also empty), keyed on `buildDmKey`. New `otherDmParticipants()` helper (`lib/chat-queries.ts`) batches the "who's the other person" join across every DM channel in one query for `dm.list`, avoiding an N+1. `channel.list`/`channel.get` now filter out/reject `isDm` channels (defense-in-depth: a DM must only ever be reached through `chat.dm.*`).
- **Found and fixed a real gap the plan missed**: making `channels.name` nullable broke `worker.ts`'s `buildChannelSystemPrompt` (Brain's system-prompt builder for channel-context conversations), which read `channel.name` unconditionally — a separate code path from the permission checks the plan had verified, only exercised when a Brain job actually runs. Added a `{ type: "dm"; otherUserName }` variant to `buildSystemPrompt` (`lib/brain-system-prompt.ts`) with its own phrasing ("a direct message conversation with X" rather than "the #X chat channel"), and `buildChannelSystemPrompt` now resolves the other participant's name (via `brainConversations.createdBy`) when `channel.isDm`.
- **Extracted `initials`/`avatarTint`/`Avatar`** out of `channel-messages.tsx` (previously private to that file) into a new `lib/avatar.tsx`, so the DM route and sidebar can reuse the same avatar rendering.
- **New route** `workspace.$workspaceId.chat.dm.$channelId.tsx` — mirrors the channel route, but header shows `Avatar` + the other person's name (no Hash/Lock icon, no Members button — DM membership is fixed at exactly 2 people from `startOrGet`). "Ask Brain" button reused unmodified (`contextType="channel"` still applies — Brain's `validateConversationContext` only checks the channel exists/belongs to the workspace/membership, all true for a DM).
- **Sidebar** (`workspace.$workspaceId.chat.tsx`): new "Direct messages" section below Channels — a "New message" picker (workspace members minus self) that calls `dm.startOrGet` and navigates to the resulting thread, and a list of existing DMs (`Avatar` + other-person name, same active-link highlighting convention as the Channels list, since both leaf routes share the `channelId` param name).

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors) across every package. API unit tests: 330 passing (up from 325 — new `dm-key.test.ts` (3), 1 new `can.test.ts` case, 1 new `brain-system-prompt.test.ts` case for the `"dm"` prompt branch). `pnpm db:generate` confirms migration `0050` captured with zero drift after applying it.
- New e2e spec `direct-messages.spec.ts`: Ada starts a DM with Bob and sends a message; Bob sees it under his own Direct Messages list (after a reload — same live-thread-existence caveat `private-channel-members.spec.ts` already documents for private channels), opens it, sees Ada's message, replies; Ada's still-open tab shows the reply live with no reload; starting a DM with Bob again lands on the same thread (`startOrGet` doesn't duplicate); confirmed the DM never appears in either user's regular Channels list. Ran live against the developer's already-running dev server (real OpenAI key loaded) — safe to do so despite follow-up #11/#12's "no live AI e2e runs" rule, since this spec never opens Ask Brain and so never makes a billed call. Re-ran `private-channel-members.spec.ts` and `chat-attachments.spec.ts` live too, both still green — no regression from the `channel.list`/`channel.get` DM-filtering changes.

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #15: DM notification sound (on/off toggle) — done

User asked for a sound notification when someone sends a DM to a specific person, toggleable on/off, "based on the volume of the machine" — read as: no separate in-app volume control, just mute/unmute, and let the OS's own volume do the scaling (true for free with any audio API, nothing to build for it).

Built:

- **`lib/dm-sound.ts`**: a short two-tone chime **synthesized via Web Audio** (`OscillatorNode` + a `GainNode` envelope) rather than shipping an `.mp3`/`.wav` asset — no file to license or attribute, and it's the first audio in the app (confirmed via research: zero prior `Audio`/sound code anywhere in `apps/web/src`). `isDmSoundEnabled`/`setDmSoundEnabled` persist the on/off flag in `localStorage` (`canvas:dm-sound-enabled`) — the first use of `localStorage` for a user preference in this codebase (confirmed no existing convention to follow instead; there's no per-user prefs column on `users` and no settings backend for something this small). `playDmChime()` fails silently (try/catch) if sound is muted, the browser blocks autoplay before any user gesture, or Web Audio is unsupported — a missed chime isn't worth surfacing as an error.
- **`hooks/use-dm-notification-sound.ts`**: given a realtime `{entity: "message", kind: "created", channelId}` event (workspace-wide invalidation events are payload-free per ARCHITECTURE.md's protocol — no `authorId`, no "is this a DM" flag on the wire), determines whether to chime by calling `chat.dm.get({channelId})` — it 404s for a non-DM channel and 403s for a DM the caller isn't part of, so it doubles as the "is this actually one of my DMs" check with no separate membership lookup and, importantly, no staleness window versus a cached `chat.dm.list` (the channel + membership rows are already committed by the time the very first message in a brand-new DM thread fires this event, so there's no race where an unrecognized-yet channel id gets silently skipped). Then fetches `chat.message.list` to read the newest message's `authorId` — chimes only if it isn't the current user, and skips entirely if the user is already looking at that exact DM thread (compared via TanStack Router's current pathname, not stored route state).
- **`hooks/use-realtime.ts`**: extended `useRealtime(workspaceId, onEvent?)` with an optional callback invoked for every parsed event alongside its own built-in invalidation — the DM sound needs to react to the same socket without opening a second connection. Held in a ref (`onEventRef`) so a fresh inline callback each render doesn't tear down and reconnect the socket.
- **Toggle UI**: a speaker icon button (`Volume2`/`VolumeX`, `aria-pressed`) next to the new "Direct messages" sidebar heading in `workspace.$workspaceId.chat.tsx` — new `hooks/use-dm-sound-toggle.ts` wraps the localStorage getter/setter in React state for it.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors), no new lint warnings. New e2e spec `dm-notification-sound.spec.ts`: stubs `window.AudioContext`/`webkitAudioContext` (via `page.addInitScript`, before any app code runs) with a fake that counts real oscillator `.start()` calls instead of trying to detect actual audio output in a headless browser — this exercises `playDmChime()`'s real code path end-to-end, not a mock of the function itself. Confirms: (1) a DM while the recipient isn't looking at the thread chimes; (2) a DM while the recipient has that exact thread open does not; (3) muting via the sidebar toggle silences a DM the recipient isn't looking at, which would otherwise have chimed. Re-ran `direct-messages.spec.ts`, `private-channel-members.spec.ts`, `chat-attachments.spec.ts` live too — all still green, no regression. Ran live against the developer's already-running dev server; safe to do so since none of this touches Ask Brain/Generate.

**Bug found by the user's own real-world test**: "I tried it but nothing happens." Root cause — browser autoplay policy: an `AudioContext` created and `.resume()`d from inside a WebSocket message handler (not a real user gesture) silently stays unable to produce audible sound in Chrome/Safari, with no thrown error, so the e2e spec's oscillator-`.start()`-counting stub couldn't have caught this — it verifies the code path runs, not that real audio hardware would actually emit anything. Fixed with `primeDmSoundOnFirstInteraction()` (`lib/dm-sound.ts`): a one-time `pointerdown`/`keydown` listener registered from the workspace shell's mount effect that creates-and-resumes the `AudioContext` inside the user's very first real click/keypress in the app, well before any DM ever arrives — the standard cross-browser pattern for unlocking gesture-less audio later in a page's lifetime.
