Re-ran all 4 chat/DM e2e specs after the fix, still green (the stub can't exercise real autoplay-policy behavior either way, so this was a targeted fix guided by the user's report, not something the test suite could have caught on its own).

**Follow-up: still reported as silent** — since audio autoplay behavior can't be fully verified without a human actually listening (the e2e stub only proves the code path runs, not that real hardware emits sound), the user asked for a bottom-right visual widget as a more reliably-confirmable notification alongside the sound. Built:

- **`lib/message-preview.ts`**: walks a message's TipTap `bodyJson` (text + mention nodes only — never converts to/stores HTML) into a short plain-text preview string, for the one surface so far (the toast) that can't render rich text directly.
- **`lib/dm-toast.ts`**: a minimal external store (plain module state + a subscriber `Set`, no provider/context) for active toast entries — deliberately not a full toast library; this is the only surface in the app that needs one so far (confirmed: no `sonner`/toast primitive existed anywhere). `pushDmToast()` auto-dismisses after 6s; `dismissDmToast()` is also called on click-through or manual close.
- **`components/dm-toast-container.tsx`**: reads the store via `useSyncExternalStore`, renders a fixed bottom-right stack — `Avatar` + sender name + message preview, clickable straight to the DM thread, plus its own dismiss button. Mounted once in the workspace shell alongside `LoginSummaryPanel`.
- **`hooks/use-dm-notifications.ts`** (renamed from `use-dm-notification-sound.ts`, since it now does both): on a qualifying DM message-created event, calls both `playDmChime()` and `pushDmToast()` — the toast is deliberately **not** gated behind the sound on/off toggle, since it's meant to work as a visible fallback regardless of whether audio is audible on the recipient's machine/browser. Still skips both when the recipient is already looking at that exact thread.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, no new lint warnings. Renamed e2e spec to `dm-notifications.spec.ts` and extended it: after a DM while not viewing the thread, asserts the toast is visible, contains the sender's name and message text, and that clicking it navigates straight to the thread and dismisses it; asserts no toast (and no chime) appears for further messages while the recipient is actively viewing the thread; asserts the toast still appears — with the correct new preview text — for a message sent after muting the sound, proving the two are independent. Re-ran `direct-messages.spec.ts`, `private-channel-members.spec.ts`, `chat-attachments.spec.ts` too — all still green.

## 2026-07-29 follow-up #16: personalized account profile (editable name, avatar upload, bio, title tag) — done

User asked whether a personalized profile section was "worth it" (custom avatar, nickname, bio, a "role" tag, plus email/password change). Scoped down via `AskUserQuestion`: **basics first** — editable name, avatar upload, bio, and a cosmetic role/title tag; email/password change explicitly deferred to a separate future request.

Research found two things worth designing around before building: `users.avatarUrl` already existed in the schema and was read/displayed in ~10 places (chat, DM sidebar/header/toast, workspace members, api-v1) but **nothing had ever been able to set it** for a normal signup (only SSO/SCIM provisioning populates it), and **nothing actually rendered it as an `<img>`** — every "avatar" anywhere in the app was the initials-circle `Avatar` component. Also, this app already has a governed workspace-membership **`role`** concept (owner/admin/member/guest, gated through `can()`/`ROLE_RANK`) — the new personal tag needed a distinct name (`title`, not `role`) with zero interaction with permissions, called out in both the schema comment and the account page's own copy.

Built:

- **Schema**: `users` gained nullable `bio`/`title` columns (migration `0051`). `title` explicitly commented as cosmetic-only, unrelated to `memberships.role`.
- **`auth.updateProfile`** (`trpc/routers/auth.ts`): self-only mutation (`ctx.user.id`, no `can()` check), full-replace semantics for `name`/`bio`/`title` (empty string coerced to `null`) rather than partial-update, avoiding omitted-vs-cleared ambiguity. No `logActivity` call — same structural reason `deleteAccount` already has none: `activity.workspaceId` is `NOT NULL` and every activity feed is workspace-scoped, but a profile edit has no single owning workspace.
- **`SessionUser` ripple**: added `bio`/`title` to the interface and every place that hand-builds one — `signUp`/`logIn`/`updateProfile` returns, `api-v1.ts`'s `authenticateApiRequest`, `execute-tool.ts`'s worker-context test-shortcut literal, `can.test.ts`'s fixture. Caught by `tsc` on the first typecheck pass, same "the ripple is real, not hypothetical" lesson as follow-up #14's `channels.name` nullability.
- **Avatar upload** (`routes/avatars.ts`, new): plain multipart Fastify route mirroring `image-assets.ts` (tRPC has no file transport) — rejects non-image and `image/svg+xml` (XSS), reuses `processImage()` unchanged for a 512px webp thumb. Unlike Library assets, stored at a **fixed key per user** (`avatars/{userId}/thumb.webp`, overwritten in place) rather than a fresh id per upload — no version history needed, no orphaned S3 objects to manage. Cache-busting via a `?v=<updatedAt.getTime()>` baked into the stored `avatarUrl` itself, not an ETag scheme — the one served-image URL in this app where the same entity id's content can actually change. `GET /avatars/:userId` is authenticated-only (not workspace-scoped): every UI surface that hands the client a userId to fetch already sits behind its own workspace-scoped `can()`/membership check, so a low-sensitivity image fetch doesn't need a redundant one.
- **`Avatar` component** (`lib/avatar.tsx`) gained an optional `avatarUrl` prop — renders a real `<img>` (with `onError` fallback back to initials, covering a broken/expired URL) instead of always showing initials. Wired through every existing caller (channel messages, DM sidebar/header/toast) plus two callers that had their own bespoke inline initials badge instead of using the shared component: the workspace shell's footer "you" badge (a real user avatar) and — left alone — `index.tsx`'s workspace-row badge (confirmed that one's a _workspace_ name initial, not a user avatar, out of scope). The footer badge also became a `Link` to `/account`, since nothing inside a workspace linked there before this — the whole feature would otherwise only be reachable by typing the URL directly.
- **`account.tsx`**: added the shadcn `Textarea` primitive (didn't exist yet — `pnpm dlx shadcn@latest add textarea`), an avatar-with-camera-button upload control (same `fileInputRef`/`FormData`/`fetch(..., {credentials:"include"})` pattern as `attachments-section.tsx`, with client-side image/SVG/size pre-checks mirroring the server's), and a name/bio/title form wired to `auth.updateProfile`, calling `useSession()`'s `refetch()` on success so the change flows everywhere immediately. Existing Danger Zone (account deletion) section untouched below.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green across every package; 330 API unit tests still passing (no new pure logic needed — `processImage` was already covered, and there's no existing precedent in this codebase for unit-testing a plain Zod schema in isolation, so none was added here).
- New e2e spec `profile.spec.ts`: edits name/bio/title and confirms the new name round-trips through `useSession` into the workspace shell's own footer badge (not just the account page); uploads the existing `test-image.png` fixture and confirms the account page renders `img[src*="/avatars/"]` instead of initials; sends a chat message and confirms _that_ message's avatar is also a real image — the concrete proof the new `avatarUrl` flows through the whole existing avatar-display pipeline, not just the one page that set it. Re-ran `direct-messages.spec.ts`, `dm-notifications.spec.ts`, `private-channel-members.spec.ts`, `chat-attachments.spec.ts`, `account-deletion.spec.ts`, `legal-pages.spec.ts` too (8 total) — all green, confirming the `Avatar`/`account.tsx` changes didn't disturb the Danger Zone flow or any existing chat/DM behavior.

## 2026-07-30 follow-up #17: back buttons everywhere, live goal progress, timesheet → task detail, doc copy/share, task-completion forms, Home quick-complete — done

User's ask spanned six separate gaps found while actually using the app: no way back from a form/goal/dashboard/automation detail page; a goal's progress bar not visibly moving when its linked tasks finished; a timed timesheet entry with no way to see what the task actually was; no way to copy/share a doc; no way to hand a task to someone outside the app with a "finished" signal that notifies Ops; and a completed Home-page task lingering in Top priority/the login panel instead of dropping off. Also asked what task dependencies are for (answered in-conversation: `blocks`/`waiting_on`, M3.4, informational only — a `●` marker on the Gantt/task panel, not a completion gate; user chose to leave it that way rather than add enforcement).

Research before building found two of the six were smaller than they looked: **M5.3's goal progress math (`task_completion` metric) was already correct** — the real bug was that `use-realtime.ts` never invalidated `goal.list`/`goal.get` on a task event, so the number was right on a fresh load but never _moved_ live, which is exactly what was reported. And **Docs already had a working back link** in its normal render (only the not-found branch was bare) — so only Forms/Goals/Dashboards/Automations detail pages actually needed one.

Built:

- **Back links**: `workspace.$workspaceId.{forms,goals,dashboards,automations}.$id.tsx` each gained a `← List` link at the top, matching the existing "← Docs" pattern already used by the doc editor and Copywriter pages — no new shared component; a one-line `Link` copy-pasted four times follows the codebase's own existing precedent for this exact pattern rather than introducing an abstraction for it.
- **Live goal progress**: `use-realtime.ts`'s `task` branch now also calls `utils.goal.list.invalidate()` / `utils.goal.get.invalidate()` (and `utils.task.highlights.invalidate()`, for the Home page item below) — blanket invalidation, not a per-goal id, since the wire event carries no `goalId` and workspace-scale goal counts don't warrant tracking `goal_links` client-side just to target it precisely.
- **Timesheet → task detail**: `workspace.$workspaceId.timesheet.tsx`'s entry rows are now buttons that open the existing `TaskDetailPanel` (`taskId` was already selected by the `timeEntry.timesheet`/`workspaceTimesheet` queries, just never wired to anything) — same `openTaskId` state + panel pattern `workload.tsx` already established, not new plumbing.
- **Doc copy/share**: "Copy text" (`editor.getText()` off the already-captured `editorRef`) and "Copy link" (the current in-app URL) buttons next to Ask Brain on the doc editor page — same `navigator.clipboard.writeText` + "Copied" toast idiom the Forms editor's public-link copy button already uses. Scoped to an in-app link only (confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`) — a public no-login doc viewer would need its own `publicToken` column, public route, and a way to turn Yjs `ydoc_state` into readable content for a non-editor, none of which exists today and none of which was actually asked for.
- **Task-completion forms** (the "share a task externally" ask) — extended the existing Forms feature (confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion`, over building a separate lightweight link type) rather than a new entity:
  - `forms.taskId` (nullable FK, `set null` on the task's own deletion, migration `0052`) — when set, a form is in "task completion" mode instead of "intake" mode. `formSchemaSchema` (the stored-shape validator) no longer requires a title field or a non-empty `fields` array — that's now an intake-mode-only rule, enforced in `createFormSchema`'s own refine (mode is known upfront at create) and, for `update`, in the router handler itself (checked against the _resulting_ `taskId`, since an update might be the one changing it).
  - `apps/api/src/lib/task-completion.ts` (new): `completeTaskWithDefaultStatus(taskId, actorId, opts?)` — marks a task done via its list's own first done-kind status (new `firstDoneStatusForList`, `task-queries.ts`, mirroring the existing `firstStatusForList`), a narrower sibling of `task.update` for flows with no status picker. `opts.verb`/`opts.extraPayload` let a caller record a distinct activity verb — used so a public form's completion is legibly different from a real logged-in user's own. Shared by both the new `task.complete` tRPC mutation (Home page checkbox, below) and `form.ts`'s `submitPublic`.
  - `form.getPublic`/`submitPublic`: when `form.taskId` is set, the public page gets the task's title/due date instead of custom fields, and submitting calls `completeTaskWithDefaultStatus(form.taskId, form.createdBy, { verb: "task.completed_via_form", extraPayload: { submitterName } })` — `actorId` has to be a real user (`activity.actor_id` is `NOT NULL`) since an external submitter has no session, so it's attributed to the form's own creator, same precedent as M4.5's original `submitPublic` attributing the created task the same way.
  - New public, `publicToken`-gated upload route `POST /public-forms/uploads` (`routes/attachments.ts`) — same shape as the existing session-gated `POST /uploads`, reusing `storage.ts`/`image-processing.ts` unchanged, writing into the same `attachments` table with `taskId` = the form's bound task. **Needed a new Vite dev-proxy entry** (`/public-forms` → the API, `vite.config.ts`) — caught by an actual failed upload in the live e2e run (`Upload failed (404)`), not by typecheck, since the browser's `fetch` was silently hitting the SPA dev server instead of the API.
  - New client component `public-task-attach-zone.tsx` — the external-submitter counterpart to the existing `ReferenceAttachZone`, same drag-and-drop/chip UX, posting to the new public route instead of a credentialed one.
  - Public page (`forms.$publicToken.tsx`) now branches on `taskId`: bound forms show the task, a "only submit once you've actually finished this task" notice, a required "Your name" field, and the attach zone; unbound forms are the unchanged fields-based `IntakeForm`.
  - Admin editor (`forms.$formId.tsx`) gained a "Bound task" section — search-and-bind (mirrors `GoalTaskLinks`' search pattern) or Unbind; the custom-fields editor hides itself once bound, since a task-completion form doesn't use fields (a deliberate scope cut over letting bound forms also carry custom fields — simpler, and covers exactly what was asked: attach + a single done signal).
  - Notifications: **"X finished a task" already showed the real actor name + a real date/time** (M3.9-era `task.completed` rendering) — reused verbatim for the new `task.completed_via_form` verb, just swapping in the payload's self-reported `submitterName` instead of `n.actorName` (`notifications-bell.tsx`), since there's no logged-in actor to read a name off of. This is exactly "X person finished X task in X date and X time" from the ask.
- **Home page quick-complete**: `task.highlights` now joins `statuses` and excludes done/closed-kind tasks (it never filtered by status before — a task marked done elsewhere would still show up until it aged out of "recent"). `TaskHighlightRow` gained a small check-circle button (new `task.complete` mutation, `useCompleteHighlightTask` — same cancel/snapshot/optimistic-set/rollback/invalidate shape as the existing `useOptimisticTaskUpdate`) that marks the task done and drops it from the cached `task.highlights` result immediately; both the Home page's sections and the login summary panel read the same query/component, so one change covers both surfaces the user named. No separate work needed to reflect it as Done in Spaces — `task.complete` goes through the same status-write + `publish()` as any other completion, so the existing task-list realtime invalidation already picks it up.

### Decisions

- **No dependency-blocks-completion enforcement** — asked and declined via `AskUserQuestion`; dependencies stay exactly as informational as M3.4 left them.
- **Task-completion forms carry no custom fields** — the "keep fields" flexibility the user picked between options was weighed against scope; attach files + a name + one submit covers the literal ask without also solving "where do arbitrary field answers go on a task" (a comment? the activity payload? visible how?), which wasn't asked for.
- **`activity`'s `actorId` for a public submission stays a real member** (`form.createdBy`), never null — matches the constraint already on the column and the precedent M4.5 set for the same reason.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 errors) across every package; migration `0052` applied with zero drift. 330 API unit tests still passing (no new pure logic warranted its own suite — `firstDoneStatusForList`/`completeTaskWithDefaultStatus` are thin DB-touching helpers in the same style as the existing, also-untested `firstStatusForList`).
- Extended `forms.spec.ts` with a new task-completion-forms spec: binds a form to a task, an anonymous context attaches a real file (`test-doc.txt` fixture) and submits with a self-reported name, confirms the task lands in the Done column on the board, and confirms the workspace's Operations Manager sees a notification naming the _external submitter_, not the form's owner. Re-ran `forms.spec.ts` (both), `goals.spec.ts`, `home-highlights.spec.ts` (both), `ops-manager-finish-task.spec.ts`, `time-tracking.spec.ts`, `docs-toolbar.spec.ts`, `attachments.spec.ts`, `task-detail-panel.spec.ts` live against a fresh dev server — 10/10 green, no regressions from the `task.highlights` filtering change, the realtime invalidation change, or the Forms schema relaxation.

## 2026-07-30 follow-up #18: fixed "first login attempt just refreshes the page" — done

User reported that logging in sometimes silently fails on the first try (lands back on `/login` with no error) and only works on a second, identical attempt. Root-caused by reading the actual flow rather than guessing — confirmed live in the browser afterward, not just by inspection.

**Cause**: `login.tsx`/`signup.tsx`'s `onSuccess` called `await utils.auth.me.invalidate()` then immediately navigated to `/`. Nothing on the login/signup page itself subscribes to `auth.me`, so React Query v5's `invalidateQueries` (default `refetchType: "active"`) had no active observer to refetch — it just marked the old, pre-login (logged-out) cached result stale and resolved immediately, without waiting for a real refetch. `RequireAuth` then mounted as the _first_ observer of that now-stale query, synchronously read the still-cached "no user" value (`isLoading` is `false` once there's any cached data, even stale), and its effect immediately redirected back to `/login` — before the background refetch it had itself just triggered had a chance to land. The second login attempt works because by then that background refetch has already completed and the cache is correct.

**Fix**: both call sites now use `await utils.auth.me.invalidate(undefined, { refetchType: "all" })`, which forces the refetch regardless of active observers and awaits it — so by the time `navigate("/")` runs, the cache already holds the real logged-in user. A third `auth.me.invalidate()` call site (`index.tsx`'s logout handler) was checked and left alone: it navigates to `/login`, which has no auth gate to bounce from, and its own page (`Dashboard`) is already an active observer via `useSession()`, so it was never affected.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors.
- Reproduced and confirmed fixed live: logged out and back in on a real account in the browser — landed on the dashboard on the first attempt, no bounce.

## 2026-07-30 follow-up #19: direct-to-storage uploads for large files (3D/Blender assets, clips, chat attachments) — done

User's 3D designer needs to send/receive files "a lot of GB" in size via chat or task attachments, and asked for storage that isn't the developer's own Mac disk. Two separate asks: (1) an alternative storage backend, (2) actual support for large files. Storage-provider choice (Cloudflare R2 recommended — S3-compatible, no egress fees, unlike S3/Supabase which both charge per GB downloaded) deferred by the user for later comparison; this pass builds the upload architecture pointed at the existing local MinIO, so switching providers later is purely an env-var change (`S3_ENDPOINT`/`S3_BUCKET`/credentials in `.env`) — nothing in the code names a provider.

The real blocker wasn't disk space, it was memory: `POST /uploads` (M1.9) buffered an entire upload into the API process's RAM via `@fastify/multipart`'s `part.toBuffer()` before ever writing to S3, capped at 25MB. A multi-GB file would exhaust server memory long before hitting any disk limit — raising the byte cap wouldn't have fixed the actual problem.

Built:

- **Direct browser→storage upload**, reversing M1.9's original "no presigned URLs, the API streams every byte itself" decision — but only for the upload half; downloads are untouched (`GET /uploads/:id` still streams through the app with a permission check every time, already using proper stream piping, not buffering, so it already scaled fine). `attachment.presignUpload` (new tRPC mutation) authorizes the target (task or chat message, same permission checks as before, now shared via a new `requireAttachmentTarget` helper — `apps/api/src/lib/attachment-auth.ts`) and mints a short-lived signed PUT URL for one exact object key + Content-Type (`storage.ts`'s new `getPresignedUploadUrl`) — no DB row yet, so an abandoned upload just leaves a harmless unreferenced object in the bucket, never a phantom attachment. `attachment.confirmUpload` re-checks permission, does a `HeadObject` to verify the file actually landed and get its **real** size (never trusts the client's claimed `sizeBytes`), runs the same image-thumbnailing (`processImage`) as before for image mimetypes — downloading the object back from storage first, since thumbnailing needs real bytes and large 3D/video files never hit this branch — then creates the `attachments` row.
- **Client-side**: new `apps/web/src/hooks/use-attachment-upload.ts` (`useAttachmentUpload`) — presign → XHR `PUT` direct to storage with real upload-progress events (`fetch` has none; a multi-GB upload can take minutes, so a bare "Uploading…" label wasn't enough) → confirm. Shared by the three places that used to hit `POST /uploads`: `attachments-section.tsx` (task attachments), `clips-section.tsx` (screen recordings — exactly the kind of large file this exists for), and `channel-messages.tsx` (chat). The old buffered route itself is deleted from `apps/api/src/routes/attachments.ts`; only the public, session-less form-completion upload (external submitters, normal-sized deliverables) still uses the old multipart-buffer shape, left alone as out of scope.
- **A sanity ceiling, not a technical one**: `MAX_ATTACHMENT_UPLOAD_BYTES` (20GB, `packages/shared/src/schemas/attachments.ts`) on the presign input — comfortably above "a few GB," just stops a garbage request from minting a signed URL for an absurd size, since file bytes no longer pass through the API's memory at all.
- **Fixed a pre-existing gap found while researching this**: neither the old `/uploads` route nor a naive replacement ever published a realtime event on attachment creation — an attachment only ever appeared for the person who uploaded it; anyone else needed a manual reload. `confirmUpload` now publishes the existing `task`/`message` "updated" realtime event types (no new "attachment" entity needed) — a message event already refreshes `chat.message.list`, which embeds each message's attachments; a task event needed `use-realtime.ts` to also invalidate `attachment.list` (a separate query from `task.get`), added alongside it.
- **`docker-compose.yml`**: MinIO gained `MINIO_API_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGIN`. Found live, not by reading docs: MinIO doesn't implement the S3 `PutBucketCors` API at all (a real bucket-level CORS call 501s "NotImplemented") — it needs its own server-wide CORS env var instead. `storage.ts`'s `ensureBucketExists()` still attempts the real `PutBucketCorsCommand` first and only warns-and-continues on failure, rather than skipping it outright — real S3/R2 do support it, so a future provider switch gets it applied for real; only MinIO falls back to its own mechanism.

### Decisions

- **Storage provider left as MinIO/local disk for this pass** — the user wants to compare pricing further before committing to R2 (or another provider). Nothing here is provider-specific; switching later is an env-var change.
- **Chat's "message must exist before a file can be tagged with its id" ordering is unchanged** — fixing the resulting brief "empty message, then attachment pops in" gap would mean restructuring `chat.message.create` to accept pre-uploaded attachment ids, real additional scope beyond what was asked (send/receive large files) and beyond what the realtime-publish fix above already improves (other viewers now see the attachment land at all, just not atomically with the message).
- **No resumable/chunked upload** — the user confirmed "up to a few GB" rather than tens of GB+, so a single presigned PUT (which itself has no inherent size ceiling) is sufficient; resumability would only earn its complexity at a size/flakiness combination this ask didn't describe.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors. 330 API unit tests still passing.
- Updated `attachments.spec.ts`/`clips.spec.ts` (their upload-wait assertions targeted the now-deleted `POST /uploads`; retargeted to `attachment.confirmUpload`) plus a full re-run of every attachment/chat-adjacent spec (`attachments`, `chat-attachments`, `clips`, `chat-drag-drop`, `private-channel-members`, `direct-messages`, `dm-notifications`, `task-detail-panel`, `chat`) — 9/9 green on a fresh dev server. (A mass 79-spec run showed widespread unrelated signup timeouts under 5-worker load against one long-lived dev server; confirmed by isolation that this was resource contention from an extended manual-testing session, not a regression — every affected spec, including ones unrelated to this change, passed cleanly once re-run against a fresh server.)
- Live-verified in the browser beyond the test suite: dispatched a synthetic **300MB** file (12× the old 25MB cap, generated client-side in the page so it never needed to leave the browser) at the task attachment upload input — uploaded successfully via the real presign/PUT/confirm flow, the `attachments` row recorded the size as exactly 300.0MB (server-verified via `HeadObject`, not the client's claim), and downloading it back through the unchanged streaming route produced a local file of exactly 314,572,800 bytes — a byte-for-byte match, confirming both directions handle large files correctly.

## 2026-07-30 follow-up #20: DB indexes on newer tables + rate limiting on public form/upload routes — done

Self-audit at the user's request ("is there anything else that needs attention") surfaced a longer list (dead `GEMINI_API_KEY`, unvirtualized Board/Gantt/Calendar, a placeholder "Import from Seri" button, recurring e2e-infrastructure flakes, etc.) — the user picked two concrete, low-risk items to act on now; the rest were left as a known list, not touched.

**1. Indexes.** `forms`, `goals`, `automations`/`automation_runs`, and `api_keys`/`webhooks`/`webhook_deliveries` were the only workspace-scoped content tables in the schema with zero indexes on the columns their own routers actually filter by — every sibling table from the same milestones (`docs`, `channels`, `image_folders`, etc.) already had one. Added, following the exact existing `docs.ts` convention (`(table) => [index(...).on(table.column)]`): `forms.workspace_id`, `goals.workspace_id`, `automations.workspace_id`, `automation_runs.automation_id`, `api_keys.workspace_id`, `webhooks.workspace_id` (also the hot path in `webhook-runner.ts`, which runs on every triggering task event), `webhook_deliveries.webhook_id`. Migration `0053`.

**2. Rate limiting.** `form.getPublic`/`form.submitPublic` and `POST /public-forms/uploads` were the only fully-public, session-less, state-mutating/storage-consuming surfaces in the app with zero abuse protection — unlike login/signup and the REST API v1, which already went through `apps/api/src/lib/rate-limit.ts`. Anyone holding a form's `publicToken` could previously spam-create tasks or hammer the upload endpoint with no throttle beyond a per-file size cap.

- Added `PUBLIC_FORM_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` (30/min) and `PUBLIC_UPLOAD_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` (20/min) to `rate-limit.ts`, plus a generic `assertPublicRateLimit(bucket, max)` helper (`assertAuthRateLimit` stays as the auth-specific ip+email dual-bucket wrapper it already was).
- Renamed `AuthRateLimitError` → `RateLimitError` (same shape, same message) since it's no longer auth-only; updated the two existing throw sites in `trpc/routers/auth.ts` and the OAuth redirect route (`routes/auth.ts`).
- Moved `clientIp(req)` (trusts only the last `X-Forwarded-For` hop — see its own comment on why) out of being private to `auth.ts` and exported it from `rate-limit.ts`, since `form.ts` and `attachments.ts` now need it too.
- `form.getPublic`: one IP-keyed bucket. `form.submitPublic`: an IP-keyed bucket **and** a `publicToken`-keyed bucket checked independently — catches both "one IP hitting many forms" and "one form link hammered from many rotating IPs." `POST /public-forms/uploads`: an IP check before the multipart body is even parsed (rejects abuse before spending bandwidth/CPU on it), then a second `publicToken`-keyed check once the token is known but before the S3 write.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors. 330 API unit tests still passing. `pnpm db:generate` after the schema edits showed one migration (`0053`) containing only the 7 new `CREATE INDEX` statements; re-running `pnpm db:generate` after applying it showed zero drift.
- Re-ran both `forms.spec.ts` specs (intake + task-completion) live — both green, confirming normal usage (a handful of calls per test run) isn't accidentally throttled by the new 20–30/min budgets.
- Live-verified the limit actually fires, not just the code path: looped a real `fetch` against `form.getPublic` from the browser console — requests 1–28 returned `200`, request 29 onward returned `429` with body `{"error":{"message":"Too many attempts. Try again in a minute.","code":-32029,"data":{"code":"TOO_MANY_REQUESTS","httpStatus":429,...}}}` — the exact threshold and message the implementation intends, confirmed against a live server, not inferred from reading the code.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #21: virtualize Board/Gantt/Calendar (closing the 2026-07-30 audit's hard-rule gap) — done

The 2026-07-30 self-audit flagged Board, Gantt, and Calendar as the three views still violating CLAUDE.md's "every list/table virtualized — assume 5k+ rows" rule (List/Table already used `@tanstack/react-virtual`; these three rendered every row unconditionally). This pass closes it.

- **Board** (`task-board.tsx`): each status column's task list is now its own bounded (`max-h-[calc(100vh-16rem)]`), independently-scrolling virtualized list (`useVirtualizer` + `measureElement` for dynamic card heights — a card's height genuinely varies with the inline delete-confirm row). The column's `useDroppable` ref and the virtualizer's scroll ref point at the same DOM node (`setScrollNode`), since dnd-kit's drag-autoscroll also keys off that being the nearest scrollable ancestor. `SortableContext` still gets the full (unvirtualized) id array so sort order stays correct; only the DOM rendering is windowed. **Known, accepted trade-off**: dropping precisely onto an off-screen (unrendered) card isn't possible — it falls back to the column's own existing "no specific `overTask`" append-to-end path, same fallback the code already had for dropping on empty space.
- **Gantt** (`task-gantt-view.tsx`): row labels and bars/milestones now share one `useVirtualizer` keyed on row count, with the label column and the day-grid's bar layer both windowed off the same virtual-item list (kept in sync since both use fixed `ROW_HEIGHT`). Restructured the label column + day grid into one shared vertical scroller (`max-h-[calc(100vh-22rem)]`) so both axes scroll together — previously the whole page scrolled instead. Made both header rows (`sticky top-0`) to avoid a UX regression from that new bounded height: without it, scrolling down would hide the day-of-month header and lose all context for what column is what. **Deliberately not virtualized**: dependency-arrow SVG lines (bounded by edge count, which a user hand-creates one at a time, not by task count — cheap to always render in full) and horizontal (day-column) virtualization (out of scope; the audit's concern was row/task count, not date-range breadth).
- **Calendar** (`task-calendar-view.tsx`): the month grid itself is already bounded to 42 cells regardless of task count, so it didn't need virtualization — the actual unbounded list here (and identically in Gantt) is the "No date" bucket, which could hold every task in the list if none had dates.
- **New shared `UndatedTasksList`** (`undated-tasks-list.tsx`), used by both Gantt's and Calendar's "No date" section: replaced the previous unvirtualized `flex flex-wrap` chip list with a virtualized single-column list in a bounded scroll box. TanStack Virtual has no wrap-layout mode, so this is a deliberate small visual change (a scrollable list instead of wrapped chips) traded for actually being bounded at 5k+ tasks. Calendar's copy keeps its original `draggable`/`onDragStart` (drag onto a day cell) via an optional prop; Gantt's is click-only, matching what each already had.
- **Not touched**: per-day-cell task lists in Calendar (`dayTasks.map`) — bounded in practice (a day's own task count, not the list's total), and building a real cap-with-"+N more" affordance wasn't asked for.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors.
- Live end-to-end against a real dev server: seeded a throwaway space with 1,200 real tasks via direct tRPC calls (700 dated, 500 undated) — confirmed via `document.querySelectorAll` that Board rendered 15/1200 cards, Gantt rendered 23/700 row labels+bars and 18/500 undated rows, Calendar rendered 18/500 undated rows, all bounded regardless of total count. Confirmed Board drag-and-drop across columns still works at this scale (dragged a card from the 1200-row "To Do" column into "Done", landed correctly). Confirmed Calendar's undated-bucket click-to-open still works; native HTML5 drag-and-drop (Calendar's `draggable`/`dataTransfer` API, unchanged by this pass) doesn't simulate reliably via synthetic mouse-only automation, so that path was verified through Playwright instead, which drives real drag events. Test space deleted after verification.
- `gantt-view.spec.ts`, `calendar-view.spec.ts`, `board-drag-and-drop.spec.ts`, `dependencies-and-milestones.spec.ts` — 4/4 passing, confirming both the row-index math refactor (`i` → `virtualRow.start`/`virtualRow.index`) and the undated-list swap didn't change any existing behavior the suite checks.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #22: audit log filters + real pagination — done

Went in planning to build "audit log UI" as a from-scratch Phase 6 item, per PROGRESS.md's own outstanding-scope notes (2026-07-24 and 2026-07-30 both listed it as not-started). **Reading the actual code first caught that this was stale**: follow-up #6 (2026-07-28) had already built a virtualized workspace-wide audit log into the Admin page (`AuditSection`, `activity.listWorkspace`, gated `workspace:manage`) — the later audits just never got amended to reflect it. What that section actually lacked, confirmed by reading it: no filters, and a hardcoded `limit: 100` with the router's own `before`-cursor pagination never wired to any "load more" UI — so anything past the newest 100 rows was silently unreachable. This pass closes that real gap instead of duplicating the page.

- **`activity.listWorkspace`** (`packages/shared/src/schemas/activity.ts`, `apps/api/src/trpc/routers/activity.ts`): replaced the flat `limit`/`before` shape with `limit`/`cursor` (an activity id — UUIDv7, so id order and time order are the same thing, matching the exact `imageAsset.library` cursor-pagination convention already established) plus four new optional filters — `actorId`, `entityType` (exact match), `verb` (ILIKE substring, so "task" matches every `task.*` verb without needing an exhaustive enum), `from`/`to` (createdAt range). Returns `{items, nextCursor}` instead of a bare array, for `useInfiniteQuery`.
- **New `activity.entityTypes`** query: `SELECT DISTINCT entity_type` scoped to the workspace, same `workspace:manage` gate — populates the entity-type filter dropdown dynamically rather than a hardcoded enum, since new entity types get logged as features grow (confirmed live: a fresh workspace showed exactly `list`/`space`/`task`/`workspace` after a couple of real actions).
- **`AuditSection`** (`workspace.$workspaceId.admin.tsx`): actor select (from the already-fetched `workspace.members`), entity-type select, a debounced free-text action search, and two date inputs, plus a "Clear filters" affordance that only appears once something's set. Switched from a plain `useQuery` to `useInfiniteQuery` with the same auto-fetch-on-scroll pattern the Library page's `imageAsset.library` already uses (watch the virtualizer's last rendered index, `fetchNextPage()` when it nears the end) — the existing virtualized scroll box needed no structural change, just a real data source behind it.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors.
- New `admin-audit-filters.spec.ts`: creates a space/list/task (three distinct entity types in one real audit trail), filters by entity type and confirms exclusion, filters by a free-text action substring and confirms the opposite exclusion, filters by a future-only date range and confirms the empty-state copy, clears filters and confirms everything's back. Re-ran `admin.spec.ts` (both tests) — still green, confirming the owner/admin gate and the section's own visibility are untouched.
- Live-verified beyond the test suite: seeded ~1,900 real activity rows (leftover from the virtualization load-test above) and confirmed in the browser console that the entity-type dropdown lists real distinct values, filtering by `space` narrows the list correctly, and scrolling the audit box to its bottom twice grew the loaded set from 50 → 150 rows (three fetched pages) — the pagination path that was previously dead code now actually fires.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #23: realtime coverage for comments, checklists, and the sidebar tree — done

The other half of the "WS realtime invalidation only covers task/status/message" gap flagged in both the 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-30 audits. `use-realtime.ts`'s discriminated-union protocol (ARCHITECTURE.md: bare `{entity, id, ...scope, kind}` invalidation events, no payloads) only had three entity variants ever published — every other mutation in the app (comments, checklists, the sidebar space/folder/list tree, and more) had **no** live wire-up at all, so a second viewer of the same task or workspace only ever saw those changes after a manual reload. Picked the two highest-value gaps for "two people looking at the same screen": the task detail panel (comments + checklists, both visible in the same panel M1.7/M1.6 already made collaborative in every other respect) and the sidebar navigation tree (a teammate creating a space/list should show up without a refresh). Left the remaining un-wired entities (goals, forms, automations, dashboards, custom fields, tags, brand kits, image folders, roles) as a known, smaller residual gap — same "wire the core case, leave room for more" precedent M5.4/SCIM already established.

- **`realtimeEventSchema`** (`packages/shared/src/realtime.ts`): three new discriminated-union variants — `comment` (scoped by `taskId`), `checklist` (scoped by `taskId`, deliberately covering both a checklist and its items since both map to the same `checklist.list` invalidation), and `hierarchy` (scoped by `workspaceId`, covering space/folder/list create/update/delete since all three collapse to the same `hierarchy.tree` refetch).
- **Publish call sites added**, mirroring the exact `logActivity`-adjacent `publish()` pattern `status.ts` already established: `comment.ts` (create, delete, reaction add/remove — 4 sites), `checklist.ts` (checklist create/delete, item create/update/delete — 5 sites), `hierarchy.ts` (space/folder/list × create/update/delete — 9 sites).
- **`use-realtime.ts`**: three new branches invalidating `comment.list`/`checklist.list` (by `taskId`) and `hierarchy.tree` (by `workspaceId`) — same shape as the existing task/status/message branches.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors. 330 API unit tests still passing.
- `comments-mentions-notifications.spec.ts`, `subtasks-and-checklists.spec.ts`, `search-and-realtime.spec.ts`, `core-work-graph.spec.ts` — all green, no regressions from the new publish calls or schema variants.
- Live cross-tab verification (not just the test suite): opened the same task's detail panel in two browser tabs under the same account, created a checklist in tab one — appeared in tab two with no reload. Posted a comment in tab one — appeared in tab two with no reload, same "just now" comment. Confirms the actual delivery path (mutation → `publish()` → Redis pub/sub → WS → `use-realtime.ts` invalidation → refetch) works end-to-end, not just that the code compiles.

### Decisions

- **Not wired**: goals, forms, automations, dashboards, custom fields, tags, brand kits, image folders, roles/permissions. None of these are as commonly "two people looking at the same screen simultaneously" as a task panel or the sidebar, and extending the protocol to every remaining entity in the app was judged disproportionate to what was asked (fix the flagged gap, not achieve 100% realtime coverage) — flagged here rather than silently left as if the gap were fully closed.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #24: real Gemini image adapter HTTP call — done

Last of the three flagged-and-deferred gaps from the 2026-07-30 self-audit. `GeminiImageAdapter` had stayed a hardcoded placeholder since M2.1 even after `GEMINI_API_KEY` was added to `.env.example` and the OpenAI adapter got its own real-HTTP pass (2026-07-29 follow-up #11) — this was the one adapter never brought to parity. Brought it to the exact same shape `OpenAIImageAdapter` already established: real call when a key is present, deterministic mock fallback otherwise, key injected by `getImageEngine` from `env.GEMINI_API_KEY` rather than read inside the class.

- **`GeminiImageAdapter`** (`gemini-adapter.ts`) now calls Google's Generative Language API `generateContent` endpoint for `gemini-2.5-flash-image` — one endpoint for both `generate()` and `edit()` (unlike OpenAI's separate generations/edits endpoints); the difference is only whether image parts are attached alongside the text prompt/instruction. `n` (multiple variants) maps to `generationConfig.candidateCount`; the app's three `AspectPreset`s map to Gemini's own `imageConfig.aspectRatio` enum (`1:1`/`2:3`/`3:2`, the closest documented match to the existing fixed pixel pairs). Reference images (`generate`'s `referenceImageUrls`, `edit`'s `sourceImageUrl`) are fetched and attached as base64 `inlineData` parts in the same request.
- **No first-class inpainting-mask parameter exists in Gemini's public image API** (unlike OpenAI's edits endpoint) — `EditRequest.maskUrl`, when given, is attached as one more `inlineData` part rather than silently dropped, same "keep it affecting real output" precedent the OpenAI adapter's own reference-image threading already established. Flagged in-code as a reasonable-not-definitive choice, not a documented Gemini feature.
- **`getImageEngine`** (`image-engine/index.ts`) now passes `env.GEMINI_API_KEY` into the constructor, same as it already did for OpenAI's key.
- **`estimateImageCostUsd`** (`ai-usage.ts`) gained a real, sourced Gemini rate — Google's published $30/1M output tokens × a flat 1290 output tokens/image = $0.0387/image — replacing the original $0.02 rough placeholder now that the adapter can make real calls whose cost actually needs tracking accurately.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors. 335 API unit tests passing (330 + 5 new).
- New `GeminiImageAdapter (real API key — HTTP path)` test block in `gemini-adapter.test.ts`, mirroring the OpenAI adapter's own test structure exactly: request shape (prompt + `candidateCount` + `imageConfig.aspectRatio`) and URL, reference images attached as inline parts, edit() attaching both source and mask, a non-OK response throwing instead of returning fake data, and an empty-response-body case throwing a clear "no image data" error (OpenAI's adapter has no equivalent of this last case since its response shape can't structurally be image-less the way Gemini's `candidates[].content.parts[]` can).
- **Not live-verified against the real Gemini API** — no `GEMINI_API_KEY` exists in this environment (same gap this exact adapter has had since M2.1), so the real-HTTP branch is verified by mocking `fetch` in unit tests only, the same limitation the OpenAI adapter's own real-path tests already accepted. The mock-fallback path (what actually runs in this environment today) is unchanged byte-for-byte from before this pass and remains covered by the pre-existing `(no key — mock fallback)` test block, so there's no behavior change for anyone without a real key configured.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #25: root-caused and fixed the recurring e2e flakes — done

Last of the 2026-07-30 self-audit's flagged items. Rather than guessing, reproduced it directly: ran a real 38-spec batch at `--workers=5` against a live dev server (the exact "5-worker load against one long-lived dev server" scenario prior entries kept attributing to vague "resource contention"). Got **27 failed, 26 passed** — a real, reproducible failure rate, not noise.

**Root cause #1 — the dominant one, 23 of 27 failures**: every failure showed the literal UI copy `"Too many attempts. Try again in a minute."` at the exact point `signUp()` submits the signup form. `AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX` (20 signups+logins per IP per 60s, added 2026-07-28 follow-up #5) is IP-keyed — every parallel Playwright worker signs up from the same `127.0.0.1`, so five workers each running specs that call `signUp()` (most specs call it at least once; several call it 2-3×) blow through a 20/min budget within seconds of the suite starting. Not a bug in the app — the rate limiter is doing exactly its job of blunting credential stuffing from one IP — it just can't distinguish "five real attackers" from "five test workers," the same way the production auth rate limit legitimately shouldn't be relaxed.

**Root cause #2 — found only after #1 stopped drowning it out**: re-running the same batch still showed 2 consistent (non-flaky) failures — `api-platform.spec.ts` (M5.4 webhooks) and `integrations.spec.ts` (M5.6 Slack notify) — both asserting that a locally-spun-up `http.createServer` on `127.0.0.1` receives a real delivery. Server logs showed `Blocked outbound address for 127.0.0.1: 127.0.0.1` — `safe-outbound-url.ts`'s SSRF guard (added 2026-07-29 follow-up #13, _after_ both these specs were originally written and verified) now unconditionally blocks every private/loopback address, including the two specs' own local mock receivers. This has silently broken both specs' actual delivery assertion for over a week; every full-suite run since then either got masked by root cause #1's noisier failures or wasn't run at the scale needed to notice a 2-failure signal in a 27-failure batch.

Both are real, narrowly-scoped test/production seams — not new limits and not disabled protection, since production behavior for either fix is completely untouched:

- **`AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`/`AUTH_EMAIL_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`** (`rate-limit.ts`) are now env-configurable (`env.ts`, same defaults: 20/10) instead of fixed literals, following the exact `SCHEDULER_TICK_MS`/`GITHUB_API_BASE_URL` precedent already established in this file for test-only overrides.
- **`SAFE_OUTBOUND_ALLOW_PRIVATE`** (new env flag, default `false`): when true, `safe-outbound-url.ts`'s `resolveAndValidate` skips both the hostname blocklist and the private-IP check entirely. Guarded in `assertProdNotLocalhost` (env.ts) alongside the existing dev-default checks — the API refuses to boot in production if this is ever accidentally true, the same way it already refuses a localhost `DATABASE_URL` or the default MinIO credentials.
- Both new env vars are set only in `playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer.env` block — real deployments, and any local `.env`, are completely unaffected. Same pre-existing caveat as every other override already in that block: only takes effect when Playwright actually spawns a fresh server (`reuseExistingServer` skips it against an already-running one — restart the dev server, or run with `CI=1`, to pick these up during local iteration).

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors. 335 API unit tests still passing — `rate-limit.test.ts` and `safe-outbound-url.test.ts` needed no changes since both read the exported constants/behavior relatively, not against hardcoded literals.
- **Re-ran the exact same 38-spec, 5-worker batch with `CI=1`** (forcing a fresh server spawn so the new env overrides actually apply) against freshly-started servers: **51/51 passed, 0 failed, 0 flaky** (51 not 38 — some spec files contain multiple tests). Confirms both root causes are actually fixed, not just plausible-sounding.
- To run this repro safely, stopped the two already-running dev processes (freeing ports 3001/5183), ran the verification, then restarted `pnpm dev` and confirmed `/health` and the web app both came back up clean — the developer's normal local session is unaffected by this investigation.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #26: Tauri desktop wrapper (Phase 6) — done

Last item in ROADMAP.md's Phase 6 list ("PWA/mobile pass; Tauri desktop wrapper"). Rust wasn't installed in this environment — confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` before installing a system-wide toolchain rather than doing it unprompted; they chose to install it and have this actually built/verified rather than left as unverified scaffolding.

- **Installed Rust** via the official `rustup` script (`stable`, default profile) — `rustc`/`cargo` 1.97.1.
- **New `apps/desktop` workspace** (`@canvas/desktop`), scaffolded with `tauri init --ci` rather than hand-written, then adjusted: `productName`/window `title` → "Canvas" (was the CLI's placeholder), `identifier` → `com.canvas.desktop` (was `com.tauri.dev`), window `1280×832` with a `900×600` minimum (was a cramped default `800×600`). Points at the existing `apps/web` app unchanged — `devUrl: http://localhost:5183` for `tauri dev`, `frontendDist: ../../web/dist` for `tauri build` — this is a pure wrapper, no native-feature code added (no `@tauri-apps/api` calls from the web app), matching the ROADMAP line's literal scope ("Tauri desktop wrapper," not "native OS integration").
- **Scripts named `tauri:dev`/`tauri:build`** (not the bare `dev`/`build` `tauri init` defaults) — the root `pnpm dev`/`pnpm build` commands run every package's `dev`/`build` script via turbo, and a bare `dev`/`build` name here would have silently pulled a native GUI window into the ordinary web+API dev loop and the production web/API build. Root package.json gained `dev:desktop`/`build:desktop` convenience scripts instead, scoped only to this package.
- `.prettierignore` gained `apps/desktop/src-tauri/target` and `.../gen` — prettier has no built-in `.gitignore` awareness, and without this it was formatting-checking (and failing on) Cargo's ~350-file build-artifact tree the moment it existed on disk.

### Decisions

- **CLAUDE.md's repo layout section was not updated.** `.prettierignore` groups CLAUDE.md with PRD/ARCHITECTURE/DATA_MODEL/ROADMAP as "spec docs — source of truth, not ours to reformat," and nothing in this file's history shows any of those five ever being edited by a session — every prior addition, including new top-level concepts (Copywriter, Library, Admin, custom roles), was recorded here instead. Followed that precedent rather than editing CLAUDE.md's own instructions.
- **No app icon design** — shipped with `tauri init`'s generated placeholder icon set (a default Tauri glyph) rather than a Canvas-branded one, since no source artwork exists in the repo to generate real icons from and that wasn't part of what was asked. Trivially replaceable later via `tauri icon <source.png>`.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors — confirmed `@canvas/desktop` (no `typecheck`/`lint` scripts; Rust has its own `cargo check`/`clippy`, not wired into this pass) doesn't get swept into the root pipeline: task count stayed at 11 before and after adding the package.
- **Actually built and launched it**, not just scaffolded: `pnpm --filter @canvas/desktop tauri:dev` cold-compiled ~350 Rust crates in 35.76s and launched `target/debug/canvas-desktop`. Confirmed the native window is genuinely rendering the real app (not blank/crashed) three independent ways: the process stayed alive with no panic in the log; macOS spawned real `WebKit.WebContent`/`WebKit.Networking` XPC child processes at launch (a WKWebView actually initialized); and `lsof` showed the WebKit networking process holding an `ESTABLISHED` TCP connection to `localhost:5183`. Cross-checked against the API's own request log: real batched tRPC calls (`hierarchy.tree`, `task.list`, `checklist.list`, `comment.list`, etc.) arrived from the desktop app for the exact workspace/task used in this session's earlier realtime testing — proof the window loaded the actual authenticated SPA and is live-polling real data through the real API, not a placeholder page. Killed the dev process afterward.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #27: built the actual .app/.dmg installer — done

Follow-up to #26 — `pnpm build:desktop` (release compile + bundle) rather than just the dev build.

- **`bundle_dmg.sh` (Tauri's DMG packaging step) failed on the first real run** with no useful error surfaced (`failed to run bundle_dmg.sh`, no detail). Root-caused by reading the script itself: past the app/DMG creation, it runs a Finder-prettifying AppleScript step (icon layout, background image) via `osascript` — which needs an interactive GUI/Automation-permitted session. This sandbox's Bash tool doesn't have one (same underlying reason `screencapture` failed earlier in follow-up #26's verification: no attached WindowServer session). The script has its own documented escape hatch for exactly this (`--skip-jenkins`, "useful in Sandbox and non-GUI environments"); Tauri's CLI wires that up automatically when it detects a CI environment, so re-running with `CI=true` (not the `CI=1` some tools accept — Tauri's own arg parser wants `true`/`false` literally) fixed it immediately, and the same release artifacts (skipping only the DMG's cosmetic Finder window arrangement) came out clean on retry.
- **Baked `CI=true` into `build:desktop` permanently** (root `package.json`) rather than leaving it as a one-off manual flag — this isn't sandbox-specific; any headless CI runner building this installer would hit the identical failure for the identical reason. Trade-off: the DMG's window won't have hand-arranged icon positions/background image, which is cosmetic only, in exchange for the build actually working unattended everywhere. A future pass wanting the fancier DMG can still run `tauri build` directly (without `CI=true`) from a real interactive desktop session.

### Verified

- `pnpm build:desktop` now completes cleanly end-to-end from a clean invocation, no manual flags: produced `Canvas.app` and `Canvas_0.1.0_aarch64.dmg` (3.6 MB) under `apps/desktop/src-tauri/target/release/bundle/`.
- **Verified the DMG is real, not just present on disk**: mounted it with `hdiutil attach` — full checksum verification passed on every partition, volume mounted cleanly at `/Volumes/Canvas` with the expected `Applications` symlink and `Canvas.app` inside, then detached cleanly.
- **Verified the packaged `.app` itself launches standalone** (not just the dev-mode build from follow-up #26): `open`ed it directly from the built bundle, confirmed via `Info.plist` that `CFBundleIdentifier`/`CFBundleName`/`CFBundleVersion` match the configured `com.canvas.desktop`/"Canvas"/"0.1.0", confirmed the process stayed alive with real `WebKit.WebContent`/`WebKit.Networking` child processes spawned (same rendering-proof pattern as follow-up #26), then quit it cleanly.
- Re-ran `pnpm check` after the `package.json` script edit — still green, 0 errors.

### Decisions

- **Not code-signed or notarized** — no Apple Developer certificate exists in this environment. The unsigned `.app`/`.dmg` will show a Gatekeeper warning on another Mac ("can't be opened because Apple cannot check it for malicious software") unless the user right-clicks → Open the first time, or the app is later signed with a real certificate. Normal and expected for a local/CI build without paid developer credentials — not a bug in this pass.

## 2026-08-04 follow-up #28: PWA + mobile pass — done

Last unstarted item in ROADMAP.md's Phase 6 list ("PWA/mobile pass"). Everything else in Phase 6 — custom roles/overrides, semantic+visual search, data export, performance hardening, audit log UI, and the Tauri desktop wrapper (follow-ups #26/#27) — was already done, per the "Where things stand after the Phase 6 session" note and follow-up #22's own read of the code.

**PWA**: added `vite-plugin-pwa` (`generateSW` strategy) to `apps/web/vite.config.ts` — precaches the built JS/CSS/HTML app shell only, with `/auth`, `/uploads`, `/trpc` explicitly denylisted from the navigate fallback. Deliberately app-shell-only, not full offline data: this app's data is realtime (WS invalidation everywhere per follow-up #23), so a stale cached API response would be a worse failure mode than a visible loading state on a flaky connection. A real web app manifest (`name`/`short_name`/`display: standalone`/theme+background colors pulled from the actual `--background`/sidebar tokens in `globals.css`, not made up) plus a real icon set (`pwa-192.png`/`pwa-512.png`/`maskable-512.png`/`apple-touch-icon.png`) generated from the existing 1024×1024 `canvas-logo.png` via `sips` — the source file's actual bytes are a JPEG despite its `.png` name, so generation forces `-s format png` rather than silently re-exporting mislabeled JPEGs. `index.html` gained a `theme-color` meta, `apple-touch-icon` link, and `viewport-fit=cover` (safe-area support on notched phones).

**Mobile**: the workspace shell (`workspace.$workspaceId.tsx`) had zero responsive treatment before this — a fixed `w-64 shrink-0` sidebar with no toggle, which on a real phone-width viewport squeezed the entire app down to almost nothing. Confirmed via `grep` across `apps/web/src` that essentially no `sm:`/`md:`/`lg:` breakpoint classes existed anywhere in the app shell or hierarchy sidebar before this pass.

- The `<aside>` becomes a `fixed`, off-canvas drawer below the `md` breakpoint (`-translate-x-full` → `translate-x-0`, `transition-transform`), and reverts to the original always-visible static sidebar at `md:` and up — byte-for-byte the same desktop layout as before. A slim mobile-only top bar (hamburger + logo, `md:hidden`) opens it; a dimmed backdrop and an explicit in-drawer close (X) button both close it, plus `Escape` and any route navigation (via `useRouterState`'s `pathname`, closing on change) — reused the existing `useModalA11y` hook (the same one already wiring focus-trap/Escape for the Generate panel) rather than inventing new dialog-a11y logic.
- `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal` on the `<aside>` are conditional on `mobileNavOpen`, not applied unconditionally — the drawer is only a true modal while acting as the mobile overlay; asserting `aria-modal` on the always-present desktop sidebar would have wrongly told assistive tech the main content was inert.
- **Found and fixed a real bug while live-testing the drawer** (Playwright at a 390×844 viewport, not just eyeballing a screenshot): the notifications bell and an `<h1>` workspace-name heading both existed twice in the DOM once the mobile top bar and the drawer's own header were both present (one hidden via CSS, not `display:none`-removed) — `getByText`/testid-based Playwright locators don't filter by CSS visibility, so this immediately broke `helpers.ts`'s `createWorkspaceAndOpen` (`getByText(name)` strict-mode violation) across dozens of specs. Fixed by not duplicating either element: the mobile top bar shows only the hamburger and logo (no bell, no name-heading), so there's exactly one `NotificationsBell` and one workspace-name heading in the DOM at all times, matching every spec's existing assumption.
- **Found and fixed a second real bug this way, in the List view itself**: `task-list-view.tsx`'s `grid-cols-[1fr_160px_140px]` (Title/Status/Created) has no minimum width and no horizontal-scroll fallback — at 390px, Status(160)+Created(140)=300px alone barely leaves the container's `p-6` padding-adjusted width, so the Title column collapsed to a sliver and task titles rendered effectively blank. Wrapped the header row and the virtualized row container in a shared `overflow-x-auto` ancestor with `min-w-[520px]` inner wrappers on both — the two stay column-aligned because they scroll together as one unit (same parent, same `scrollLeft`), and TanStack Virtual's own virtualization is untouched (still only renders visible rows; the wrapper just changes how the row's existing `width: 100%` is computed). Desktop is visually unchanged since 520px never exceeds a normal desktop viewport, so `overflow-x-auto` never actually engages there.

### Decisions

- **Did not redesign Board/Gantt/Calendar/Table views for mobile.** Spot-checked the default List view and the task detail panel (already `w-full max-w-lg`, full-width on mobile with no changes needed) live in the browser at 390px — both read fine once the shell itself stopped squeezing them. A full per-view mobile redesign of every remaining page (Board's drag columns, Gantt's timeline, Calendar's grid, dozens of admin/settings pages) is a much larger surface than "PWA/mobile pass" implies and wasn't attempted — this pass fixed the one universal, all-pages-affected blocker (the shell) plus the one concretely-broken view found by actually testing on a phone-width viewport, rather than guessing at every view's mobile treatment un-tested.
- **No offline data / background sync** — the service worker precaches the app shell only (see above); this is a realtime, multi-user work-management app, and stale offline task/comment data would be actively misleading, not helpful. A user who installs the PWA gets a fast, app-like launch and a shell that survives a flaky connection, not offline editing.
- **Maskable icon uses the same full-bleed logo as the other sizes**, not a version redrawn with extra safe-zone padding — no source vector/layered artwork exists in the repo to redraw from (same constraint follow-up #26 noted for the Tauri app icon), and the existing mark (a contained circular emblem on a solid black square) already tolerates most maskable-icon cropping reasonably well. Flagged here rather than silently treated as a fully-correct maskable icon.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors. `pnpm --filter @canvas/web build` succeeds; confirms the service worker (`sw.js`) and manifest (`manifest.webmanifest`, 9 precached entries) generate correctly.
- Live-verified with real Playwright browser automation (not just a static read of the JSX), at both a 390×844 mobile viewport and a 1400×900 desktop viewport against the real dev stack: mobile shows the hamburger-only top bar, opening it slides in the full drawer (search, Generate/Brain, nav tree, account footer) over a dimmed backdrop, closing it via the X button returns to the closed state, and desktop renders pixel-identical to before this pass (static always-visible sidebar, no top bar). Also created a real space/list/task at 390px and confirmed the previously-blank Title column now shows truncated text correctly, with the Created column reachable by horizontal scroll and staying header-aligned.

## 2026-08-06 follow-up #29: cross-cutting audit — tasks, security/roles, copywriter, library/brand kits, non-chat AI — done

Five parallel review passes (tasks CRUD, security/RBAC, copywriter, library + brand kits, and every AI feature besides Brain chat) against CLAUDE.md's hard rules, followed by fixes for everything confirmed and in-scope for a same-session fix.

**Fixed:**

- **Critical: SAML/SCIM account-takeover via unverified email identity-linking.** `findOrCreateUserByEmail` let any workspace's self-registered SAML IdP (or SCIM token) silently log a session into — or attach a membership to — any _existing_ user matching an asserted email, with no check that the IdP was ever authoritative for that identity. Since a workspace owner fully controls their own IdP cert with no domain verification, this was a full unauthenticated account-takeover: create a workspace, register a SAML IdP, assert a victim's email as NameID, get their session cookie. New `findOrCreateSsoUser` (`lib/user-provisioning.ts`) only auto-provisions brand-new identities or authenticates users already a member of _that_ workspace; an existing-but-unrelated account now gets a `SsoIdentityConflictError` instead of a session. Wired into `routes/saml.ts` and `routes/scim.ts`. Also added the missing `email_verified` check to the Google OAuth callback (`routes/auth.ts`) — same class of gap, lower severity since Google itself is normally the one vouching for the email.
- **High: `workspace.invite`/`updateMemberRole`/`setOperationsManager`/`removeMember` hand-rolled `can()` directly** instead of `assertCan()`, skipping the custom-role/space-override resolution pipeline entirely — a custom role built specifically to revoke `workspace:manage` from a member was silently ignored on these four call sites. Switched all four to `assertCan`.
- **Automation `generate_image` action never re-checked permission** against the automation creator's _current_ role (only quota) — re-added the same `imageAsset:create` check Brain's tool loop already applies for the identical action, so a demoted/removed creator's automations stop silently generating images.
- **`critique_image` Brain tool and all embedding paths (task auto-embed, image-understanding auto-embed, semantic-search query embed) had no AI quota check at all** — unbounded free spend against the shared monthly $ cap. Added a new `"embed"` quota kind (`AI_EMBEDDINGS_PER_DAY`, `packages/shared/src/ai-quotas.ts`) and a non-throwing `isWithinAiQuota` for the two background/best-effort auto-embed call sites (skip silently rather than fail the task/image mutation that triggered them) vs. a hard-throwing check on `critique_image` and the user-facing semantic search query (same pattern as generate/edit).
- **Realtime invalidation gaps**: tag create/delete, custom-field def create/update/delete, custom-field value set/clear, and attachment delete/`attachExternal` never published a WS invalidation event — other connected clients only saw these changes on next full reload. Added `tag`/`customFieldDef`/`customFieldValue` to `realtimeEventSchema` and wired `publish()` calls; attachment delete/attachExternal now reuse the existing task/message-event trick `confirmUpload` already established.
- **Copywriter's `approve` mutation never wrote an activity row** (every sibling mutation in that router did) — added `copy_generation.approved`.
- **Brand kit bugs**: deleting the workspace's default kit left it with zero defaults (no auto-reassignment) — now promotes the oldest remaining kit; a stale hardcoded `"gemini"` fallback (superseded by `DEFAULT_IMAGE_PROVIDER` = `"openai"` back in follow-up #24) replaced with the shared constant; `imageFolder.delete` now rejects deleting a system-managed (`copy_root`/`copy_client`) folder server-side rather than relying on the web UI hiding the button.
- **`task.bulkUpdate` never wrote a per-task `task.updated` activity row** (only one list-scoped `task.bulk_updated` summary) — a task edited via bulk update never showed the edit in its own activity feed. Added the same per-task row `task.update` writes.
- `.env.example` was missing `AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`/`AUTH_EMAIL_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`/`SAFE_OUTBOUND_ALLOW_PRIVATE` (all three real, already-enforced env vars per `env.ts`) — added.

**Found, deliberately not fixed this pass — flagged for a dedicated follow-up:**

- **Image understanding (alt-text/auto-tags) and image critique are permanently mocked with no live branch at all**, unlike every other AI surface in the app (Brain, Copywriter, both image-gen adapters), which all select a real provider when its API key is configured and only degrade to a mock otherwise. `.env.example`'s own comment ("Anthropic (Brain chat + vision understanding)") implies this was meant to make a real Claude vision call and never did — this reads as an unfinished feature, not a deliberate stub. Their `ai_usage` rows are also hardcoded to `model: "claude-vision-mock"` regardless of whether a real key is set, polluting cost/attribution data. Not attempted here: wiring a real Claude vision call (image bytes fetch + base64 encode + a genuinely new request shape) is a feature-sized addition with its own test surface, not a same-pass bug fix.
- **The task activity feed (`activity.ts`'s `list`) only ever shows `entityType = "task"` rows**, so checklist/custom-field/attachment/tag changes on a task are invisible in that task's own activity feed even though every one of those mutations does write _an_ activity row (just under its own entity type, with no `taskId` correlation back). Fixing this properly needs each of those routers to start carrying `taskId` in `payloadJson` plus a new query shape (and likely a new expression index — `activity_entity_idx` only covers `(entityType, entityId)`, and this feed is opened on every task-detail-panel view) to avoid an unindexed scan on an unbounded table. A real fix, but schema/index work of its own, not a drop-in query tweak.
- SAML's `validateInResponseTo: never` (accepted v1 replay-window tradeoff, documented in follow-up on SSO) is worth re-evaluating now that the identity-linking bug above showed this trust boundary is higher-stakes than first modeled — not changed this pass since NotOnOrAfter/audience/signature checks still bound the window to the assertion's short validity period.
- `safe-outbound-url.ts`'s IPv4 block-list doesn't cover documentation/benchmark ranges (192.0.2.0/24, 198.18.0.0/15, 203.0.113.0/24, 192.88.99.0/24) — low practical risk (RFC1918/loopback/link-local/metadata/IPv4-mapped-IPv6 and the DNS-rebinding TOCTOU are all already covered), not fixed this pass.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green (0 new errors; pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warnings in `apps/web` untouched by this pass). All 335 API unit tests pass, unmodified, after every fix above.
- Playwright regression pass: targeted specs (`core-work-graph`, `task-detail-panel`, `table-view`, `board-drag-and-drop`, `gantt-view`, `calendar-view`, `workload`, `dashboards`) all green after the fix for the duplicate-DOM-node bug above. A reduced-concurrency (`--workers=2`, avoiding follow-up #25's documented per-IP auth-rate-limit flake) full 80-spec run against the already-running dev server: 53 passed, 27 failed. **Confirmed all 27 are pre-existing and unrelated to this pass**, not a regression: `git stash`ed every file this pass touched, re-ran the same failing specs (`brain-chat`, `version-tree`, others) against unmodified `main`, and got byte-identical failures (same locators, same timeouts, same error text) before restoring the stash. The pattern (Brain-reply/image-generation-mocked specs failing on a wait for mocked async output) points to an environmental issue in this sandbox unrelated to any code in this pass — not investigated further since it predates and is outside this pass's scope.

## 2026-08-07 follow-up #30: root-caused the real GitHub Actions `e2e` CI failure (a test bug, not an app bug) — done

The `e2e` CI job has been red on every run since well before follow-up #29 (checked via GitHub's REST API — no `gh`/token available in this environment, so job logs weren't directly downloadable, but run/job/step metadata is public). Reproduced locally against the same fresh-server, fresh-Postgres conditions CI uses (`CI=1 pnpm --filter @canvas/e2e test:e2e`, stopping the locally-running dev servers first so Playwright spawns its own, matching follow-up #25's established repro method) instead of guessing from the CI UI.

**Root cause — `brain-history.spec.ts`, 100% reproducible, not a flake**: deleting the conversation currently open in the Brain panel intentionally starts a fresh conversation and closes the history panel (`brain-chat-panel.tsx`'s `deleteConversation.onSuccess`, existing documented behavior — confirmed correct via a temporary network-response logger showing every `brain.list` fetch after the delete correctly excluded the deleted conversation). The test asserted `not.toContainText` directly on the `historyList` div immediately after clicking delete — racing its own unmount as the panel navigated away, which fails deterministically (not intermittently) once that navigation completes, on every single run. Fixed by waiting for the panel to actually finish closing (`await expect(historyList).toHaveCount(0)`) before reopening history and asserting on its content there instead — verified against the still-mounted, stable state rather than a div mid-unmount. Confirmed fixed with 4 full local 80-spec runs at real CI parity (`CI=1`), all clean on this spec.

**Ruled out, not fixed (not real CI bugs)**:

- Both `saml-scim.spec.ts` tests failed locally on some repro runs with `sso_identity_conflict`/409 — traced to `jane.doe@example.com`/`carlos@example.com` rows left over in this machine's long-lived local Postgres volume from a prior manual run (2026-07-23), which follow-up #29's new `findOrCreateSsoUser` correctly now rejects as an unrelated existing identity. Confirmed by deleting the two stale rows: both tests pass immediately after. Real CI's Postgres is a fresh ephemeral service container every run, so this never occurs there — no code change needed.
- `docs-collab.spec.ts` and `email-digest.spec.ts` each failed once across 4 full local runs (the latter recovered on Playwright's built-in CI retry both times) — consistent with follow-up #25's already-documented parallel-worker resource contention, not a new or deterministic issue, and this machine's default worker count is higher (more parallel load) than GitHub's standard hosted runner. Left alone.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors.
- 4 full local `CI=1` 80-spec runs: 2 fully clean, the other 2 only showed the already-ruled-out local-DB-pollution and known parallel-load flakes above — `brain-history.spec.ts` itself passed in all 4.
- Confirmed live on GitHub's own infrastructure, not just locally: run #36 (`b434f05`) shows both `ci` and `e2e` green.

## 2026-08-07 follow-up #31: cleared every remaining CI annotation (Node 20 deprecation + fixable lint warnings) — done

CI had zero errors after follow-up #30, but GitHub's own Annotations panel on every run still flagged 12 warnings — asked to clear those too rather than leave a run that's merely "not red."

- **Node.js 20 deprecation**: `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, and `pnpm/action-setup@v4` all still target the Node 20 actions runtime GitHub is retiring (being force-run on Node 24 for now, hence the warning, not a hard failure yet). Bumped to their current majors — `actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/setup-node@v7`, `pnpm/action-setup@v6`, and (same class, e2e job) `actions/upload-artifact@v7` — verified each new major's `action.yml` actually declares `using: node24` before pinning (fetched from GitHub directly, not assumed from version number).
- **`react-refresh/only-export-components` in shared UI components** (`button.tsx`, `avatar.tsx`, `charts.tsx`, `automation-actions-editor.tsx`, `form-fields-editor.tsx`) and **two `no-unused-vars`** (`task-list-view.tsx`/`task-table-view.tsx`'s dead `Status` type alias): fixed for real, not suppressed. Each offending file exported a non-component value (a `cva` variants object, plain helper functions, a draft-factory function) alongside its component — split each into a sibling file (`button-variants.ts`, `avatar-utils.ts`, `chart-colors.ts`, `automation-action-draft.ts`, `form-field-draft.ts`) and updated every importer. `button-variants.ts` matches shadcn/ui's own upstream fix for this exact warning.
- **Deliberately left alone — the remaining 71 warnings**: every one is either `main.tsx` (the app entry point, which structurally can't "only export components") or a `routes/*.tsx` file, which TanStack Router's routing convention requires to export both a `Route`/`createRoute(...)` object and the page component from the same file. "Fixing" these would mean restructuring routing across 40+ files to fight the router's own required pattern, for a dev-only Fast Refresh nicety with no functional or CI-blocking effect — out of proportion to the actual problem, so left as the accepted, inherent tradeoff of file-based routing.

### Verified

- `pnpm check` green, 0 errors (71 pre-existing, now-explained warnings remain, down from 81).
- `pnpm build` succeeds (both `apps/api` and `apps/web`).
- Full local `CI=1` 80-spec Playwright run: 80/80 passed.
